DeepQuarry Final API

Against origin/master 6dcd1e30f9 2 October 2026 · revision 5: test driver and capture fixes Replaces the OM layer, the sys layer, interactions and every legacy declaration form

This is every public form game code writes once the migration is done. That covers declaring a type, scheduling work, holding and changing state, linking entities, performing actions, reacting to things, drawing, talking to players and to Rust. Each section ends with what it replaces. Section 17 maps the old forms that matter to their new ones, and says which lint retires each; the full table, checked mechanically against the API surface on master, is doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv. Section 19 is the plan that gets us there.

Vocabulary

Several words meant more than one thing in earlier drafts, and some clashed with BYOND built-ins. Each now has one meaning here, and the other uses have their own names. The table is the place to look when a word seems to be used two ways.

WordThe one meaningWhat the other uses became
partOne unit of op behaviour, acting in one or more of the four op stages (section 9).A machine's installed components are components(slots), req_component(T) and component_swap. A layer of a look is look.layer() and look_layer().
stageAn op stage: Match, Require, Wait or Do. A build stage is a rung of a construction ladder (stage(), STAGE_*, section 12).The construction step() is stage(), which no longer clashes with BYOND's step().
act, actionA /datum/act context: an op or a world action, anything that can be refused (section 8).The verb-panel entry that lists gameplay abilities is "Abilities".
holdThe verb hold(): a runtime contribution to a stat, kept by a source (section 5).What a provider or storage takes is accepts =. The item in the actor's hand is A.held.
statA value composed from contributions by one rule, declared with STAT(T, name, RULE). Body factors (BF_*) are stats too, as STAT_* ids.BYOND's stat() and /mob/var/stat are not ours to reuse, which is why the declaration is an uppercase macro.
settingA tracked var that a player or an admin chose (TRACKED, toggle_setting()).The configurable values of a capability are its params. The setting() entry is gone.
keyAn op key is a literal string, a capability key is CAP_X, a tag is TAG_X. The build keeps the three kinds apart.Stats, capability state, sources and build stages have their own ids (STAT_X, COVER_OPEN, SRC_X, STAGE_X).
sourceWhoever holds a contribution to a stat or a grant: a datum, or a SOURCE_DEF flyweight such as SRC_AI_CONTROL. Text and null are errors.The context's A.source is the source of the activation that is running. A lasting hold by one actor uses that actor as its source.
activation, scopeAn activation is one record applying a definition to a holder for a while: its source, params, state, contributions and scope (while its source exists, while slotted, while a condition holds, for a time). Foundation X1 (section 5).Grants, entry-level when(), while_slotted, observe, memberships and timed grants are all activations, not separate mechanisms.
resource, reservationA resource is something a discrete cost takes (RES_*): fuel, charge, a stack, blood, a slot's capacity. A reservation holds an amount until the op commits or releases it. Foundation X2 (section 9).Continuous draw (power_draw) is a stat, not a resource.
workflow, stepA workflow is an ordered set of typed steps (a prompt, a database query, a path request, a window) with typed outcomes. Foundation X3 (section 13).asks() and request() start one; a step is a /datum/request.
schemaThe declared kind of a value, with range, normaliser, default and persistence (int(0, 100), enum(LIST), text(32)). Foundation X5 (section 4).Validation is never written by hand beside a setter, an arg or a column.
outcomeHow something ended. An action ends ACT_COMMITTED, ACT_REFUSED or ACT_REPLACED; an op effect reports OP_OK, OP_REFUSED, OP_REPLACED or OP_FAILED; a request ends REQ_ANSWERED, REQ_CANCELLED, REQ_TIMED_OUT and so on. A listener's outcome = filter adds ACT_ROLL_FAILED and ACT_ANY to the three action outcomes. perform_op() returns a plain /datum/op_result carrying the outcome, never the pooled act.A null field never means several things.
inline, marked, drain pointA stat is inline when it is settled before the setter returns (the written entity, and one single-valued relation edge away), and marked when the write only flags it for the next marked drain. A drain point is the end of a trigger or the start of phase D, P, S or R; a marked drain is the drain at the start of D, P or R, the only ones that recompute marked stats, under the budget of each stat's lane (section 7).Earlier drafts used three different tests for what is marked; the build now derives one rule from the relation declarations.
condition, requirementA condition is a boolean. A requirement is a condition plus a reason. needs() takes requirements; when() and the rest take conditions (ground rule 6).req_stat, req_set and req_clear are req_is() and req_at_least().
capability, bundleA capability is a named, configurable bundle of entries that something can address by key (configure, extend, without, grant). A bundle is any proc returning entries or parts.Wrap a bundle in a capability only when someone must address it. machine_basics, wall_machine and maintenance_hatch are capabilities.
system/datum/system: world-level state and periodic work owned by the kernel (section 2).The old "sys layer", world services and subsystems are gone. Host machinery such as the database and tgui transport is made of systems.
notice, hookA notice announces that something happened (/datum/notice/<past tense>). A hook is a handler attached to an action, a notice or a state change (section 10).There are no OM events and no signals.
origin, reach, provider, authority, channelFive separate facts about an input (section 8). The origin is where it arrived (ORIGIN_*); the reach policy is the spatial relation the op needs (REACH_*); the provider is what performs it (a hand, a held tool, a module, a natural weapon); the authority is the permission it is exercised under (AUTH_*); the channel says whether the op is the actor's own or the target's.An affordance (AFF_*) is what a provider can do. An intent (INTENT_*) is what a gesture means; the old ACT_* gesture names are INTENT_*.
bindingOne input of an op: hand(), tool(Q), ui_act(), menu(), ai() (section 9). An op has one or more. Every binding runs the same Match, Require, Wait and Do.A second op that borrows the first one's effects is the explicit, rare shares_effects(key).
on =Where an effect lands: ON_TARGET (default), ON_HOLDER, ON_ACTOR, ON_HELD. while_slotted() adds ON_CONTENTS.ON_SELF never had a definition and is gone.
request, prompt, askA request is anything that waits for an answer: one step of a workflow. A prompt is a request a player answers. asks() is how a trigger makes one; request() is how code outside a trigger does.io_request(), ask() and the OM prompt calls are one form (section 2).

The model

Six ideas cover the whole API. Everything else in this document is a constructor for one of them, or a library built from them.

Entries

What a type declares, in one inheriting list per type: CAPABILITIES(T) and its indented entries. Capabilities, ops, relations, triggers, outputs, look layers. Built once per type and interned. Identifiers (stats, actions, sources) are declared once, each with its own macro.

Parts

The unit of behaviour inside a trigger. Each part acts in one or more of four op stages: Match, Require, Wait and Do. Interned values that nest into bundles.

Stats

A value composed from contributions by one rule, declared once with STAT(T, name, RULE). Its effective value lives in a plain var that only the engine writes, and recomputes inline the moment anything it depends on changes on the same entity or one single-valued edge away; farther dependents are marked for the next marked drain, the drain at the start of phase D, P or R. Built on the existing OM contribution store.

Sources

Whoever holds a runtime contribution: a datum (an item, a spell, an admin, a capability's op) or a named SOURCE_DEF flyweight. One source holds once. Everything a source applies is an activation with a scope (section 5), so releasing or deleting the source takes back what its scope covers, by the one teardown path.

Triggers

Things that run parts. Ops (chosen by an actor, through any of their input bindings), actions (refusable world events), notices (it happened), changes, timers and periodic work. Every trigger has a typed context, and every handler takes it: x(datum/act/A).

Systems

World-level owners of global state and periodic work, scheduled by the kernel with boot order, budgets and membership. They replace subsystems, world services, world lanes and the OM scheduler. The host machinery (input, database, tgui transport, garbage, air, lighting) is made of them too.

client input→input inbox (typed event)→gesture→bind profile + stance→intent (INTENT_*)→candidates: target, held item, actor→1 Match: actor gate (origin), providers and reach gate, input binding, when→priority→2 Require (and the pre-checks of the actions the effects start)→3 Wait (cancels early)→reserve costs→4 Do: effects→commit or release→notice (committed only)
setter / hold / activation / relation write→stats on the entity and one single-valued edge away recompute inline (a gating condition is an input of the stat)+stats farther away, and other readers, marked (only if something reads them)→drain point: hooks attach and detach, on_change hooks run, outputs queue; the drains at the start of D, P and R also recompute marked stats, under their lane's budget→phase S: push_to_rust→phase N: native frame→phases U, D, P: urgent work, deadlines, phased work (D and P each start with a drain point; U has none)→phase R: draw, ui_data, outputs() (after its own drain point)

Execution model. Writes are immediate. A write marks its readers dirty, in time proportional to the number of readers, and publishes nothing that nobody reads. Logical state is settled before the setter returns, on the written entity and one relation edge away. Stats on the written entity, and on the entity at the end of a single-valued relation edge (a holder; a slot occupant; the container of a slot of capacity 1; a one-to-one link), recompute inline, in rank order, so the writer's next line reads the new value. That includes a contribution gated by a condition, such as an open door's when(DOOR_OPEN, contributes(STAT_DENSITY, FALSE, ...)). Permissions (the actor gate and the inputs of the reach gate) and containment are settled the same way. Every other stat the write reaches is marked, and recomputed at the next marked drain, in rank order, under the budget of its lane: a stat reached through a collection or membership edge (an area's machines, a locker's contents, a system's members), through a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, or two or more edges away. The build derives which is which from the relation declarations, and no author declares it. Follow-up work is deferred to a drain: attaching and detaching hooks, observes and outputs granted under a condition, on_change hooks, outputs and cleanup, in a rank the build assigns from the generated reads graph. A drain point is the end of the current trigger and the start of phases D, P, S and R, and phase R runs only outputs. Only the drains at the start of D, P and R recompute marked stats (the marked drains); the others do the follow-up work and recompute nothing marked, so a click's trigger never carries a cascade and phase S, which is never shed, never waits on a budget. A cycle among stats and conditions is a build error, and an iteration cap per drain logs the key chain and spills the rest to the next drain point. Section 7 states the guarantee exactly, and spike E0's eighth proof measures the marked case.

Ground rules

  1. One form per job. Each need has exactly one form, listed in this document. A second form is a defect: the lint for the old one is shrink-only, and reaches zero by the end of the migration.
  2. Every key is a declared identifier. Stats, tracked vars, capabilities, capability state, sources, build stages, actions and messages are declared once, by a macro that generates their id. A string key (an op key, a timer key) is a literal the build step resolves. An unresolved key is a build error, and there are no computed keys. The few keys that are data rather than identifiers are listed in section 1.
  3. Declarations are pure and per type. Entries never read instance state. Anything that depends on an instance is a condition (when(), a contribution's condition) or a grant, evaluated at runtime. A capability's param can name a var or a proc, which is resolved per instance at runtime.
  4. State changes only through setters, stats, holds and relation accessors. These are the only things that publish. Raw writes to tracked state are build errors.
  5. Reads are generated, never typed. The build derives what every condition, requirement and output reads, including reads through A.actor, A.held and A.target, and through global helpers annotated READS_FROM(arg). The only hand-written reads are READS_AS, for a producer key that is not a var, and that READS_FROM annotation. Capability state needs neither.
  6. Conditions and requirements. A condition is a boolean. A requirement is a condition plus a reason, so a refusal always says why. needs() takes requirements; when(), contributes(), every(when =) and the rest take conditions. Both are pure: test builds run them under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs, so menus, examine and early cancels can ask at any time.
  7. One handler signature. Every proc the engine calls to run a trigger or a part takes (datum/act/A): effects, requirements, conditions, hook handlers, timers, every() work and request callbacks. A.holder is always set. Section 9 has the table of which fields each place sets. The exceptions are engine interfaces with a signature of their own, each stated where it is defined: the standard outputs draw(look) and push_to_rust(), a capability's entries(), the procs of a resource adapter (section 9), a schema's normalize (x(datum/act/A, value)) and a system's lifecycle hooks (initialize(), on_shutdown()).
  8. Every effect runs inside a trigger. Effects never sleep. Waiting is a wait() part, a timer (after(), delayed()) or a request (asks(), request()). Only the kernel and the request layer suspend a chain. There is no spawn, gameplay sleep, INVOKE_ASYNC or do_after.
  9. Global state lives in systems. There are no new globals, subsystems, world services, Master Controller or fire(). The host machinery is systems too.
  10. Memory scales with distinct declarations, not with types or instances. Everything declarative is interned, and a type that adds nothing shares its parent's compiled tables. A type-level capability allocates no activation until it holds state (section 5).
  11. One mechanism per concern. A lasting effect on a holder is an activation, a discrete cost is a resource transaction, a wait for an answer is a workflow step, a staged progression is a state graph, and a declared value has a schema (Foundations). A form that needs one of these is defined on it, never with a lifetime, a spend-and-refund sequence or a validator of its own.

Foundations

Five generic systems carry the rest of the API. Each existing form keeps the name an author writes, but is defined on one of them, and says so in one line where it is introduced. Lifetime, cost, waiting, staging and validation are therefore stated once here and in their own sections, not again for every mechanism.

FoundationWhat it isWhat is built on itDefined in
X1 Scoped activationOne record that applies a definition to a holder, with a source and a scope. One attach path (build, validate, apply atomically) and one teardown path.grant(), entry-level when(), while_slotted, observe(), join() and memberships, species capabilities, worn and held item effects, timed holds and timed grants, capability params, state and stackingSection 5, with 11
X2 Resource transactionsReserve an amount, then commit it or release it, against a resource's adapter.costs(), consumes(), fuel, cell charge, stacks, blood, dark energy, reagent transfers, slot and storage capacity, the construction ledger, and the op commit contractSection 9
X3 WorkflowsAn ordered set of typed steps, with typed outcomes, captured fields and one resumption rule.asks(), confirms(), request(), the prompt kinds, database queries, path requests, windows, prompt chains and job()Section 13, with 2
X4 State graphsNamed stages as nodes and ops as edges; per instance a current stage, a history stack and a ledger.Construction and deconstruction, dismantle(), becomes(), deployablesSection 12
X5 Value schemasA declared kind with range, normaliser, default and persistence.TRACKED, STAT bases, capability params, op args, ui_shape, prompt and request input, SQL row fields, preferences, VV editsSection 4

How they fit. An op's Wait stage is a workflow (X3), and every asks() runs there. Its costs are reservations (X2), taken after the last answer and committed after the effects. A capability's ops and contributions are an activation (X1), whose params are schema-checked (X5). Construction is a state graph (X4) whose ledger records the resources its edges took (X2). Request input, op args and UI data are checked against schemas (X5).

What they do not cover. A machine mode is a plain tracked enum, not a state graph. Continuous draw (power_draw, a per-tick drain) is a stat feeding its domain, and only discrete spends are transactions. A workflow is not a scripting system: it is the wait of one op or request, with a fixed list of steps.

1Declarations

#define CAPABILITIES(T) ##T/__capabilities() // a block: the header registers T's entry list with the table builder, the entries are its indented statements; no instance is created, and there is no ..() to forget
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/thing)
	op("press", hand(), then(PROC_REF(pressed)))             // one entry per line: no trailing commas, no backslashes
	extend("ui_open", needs(req_operable()))          // an entry may span lines inside its own parentheses
  • One composition root per type. A type's entries are in one CAPABILITIES list. A second list for the same type is a build error. A data-only subtype is a header and one line: CAPABILITIES(T) then configure(...). A long list is split into sections (below), not into other procs, so everything a type declares stays on the type.
  • Sections. A section(name, "doc") line inside the block groups the entries after it, up to the next section or the block's end, so sections sit at the end of the block. A section is only a heading: its entries are the type's own, so handlers are PROC_REF(x) and vars nameof(v), nothing enters the global namespace, and every reader (generated reads, UI types, op keys, op order) treats them like any other entry. Each compiled entry carries its section with its file:line, and Explain Type and Explain Interaction print apc.dm:231 section controls. A section can't be addressed (no without or configure of a section) and lives in the type's one block: there is no section in another file, because one root per type is what makes the explain output and the two_blocks check simple, and a type whose block outgrows its file has a capability waiting to be extracted. An empty, repeated or misnamed section is a build error.
  • Block form. The marker line is a header and the entries are its indented body (STATE_GRAPH(graph) is the same; CAPABILITY_DEF reads a body the same way). Every entry name is a real constructor proc with named parameters, so the compiler and DreamChecker check names and arity like any call; nothing ever runs the block, analyze gen declare reads it. Two spellings differ from a call you would write by hand: links(A::a, B::b) (the pair entry; link is a BYOND reserved word) and configure(constructor(param = value)) (the capability's own constructor carries the params, so configure(CAP_X, param = value) is gone). An adjusts field path is text: adjusts("packet.amount", scale = 0.5).
  • Inheritance is automatic: the builder compiles each type from its parent's compiled table plus its own list, so the parent's entries always come first and ancestors are not re-run per type.
  • Build time: the table builder walks the list once per type, interns every entry, and splits it into the runtime indexes: op resolver, trigger index, relation table, stat table, look layers and the verb table (for verb_entry() commands). It records __FILE__:__LINE__ for every entry and keeps an origin list per interned entry in every build (a file and a line per entry, measured by the boot-memory gate), so the explain tools can say where anything came from in production too (section 15).
  • Sharing: a type whose list adds nothing shares its parent's compiled tables.
  • Boot: capabilities initialize inside Initialize's ..(), before the type's own code that follows it. Initial stat values are computed silently, with nothing published, until map load completes.

Sections and bundlesA section(name, "doc") line groups the entries of one type's block (section 1). Reuse is a capability, or a bundle: a plain proc returning list(entries) or parts, named by a name() entry. There is no BUNDLE marker11

Declared identifiers

Entries compose behaviour. The names they refer to are declared once, on their own lines, and each declaration generates the id. Nothing refers to a stat, a source or a build stage by an undeclared string.

IdentifierDeclared byWritten asSection
StatSTAT(T, name, RULE, ...): declares var/name, its default and the id (one id per name: two types share a name only with the same rule and base, else a build error)STAT_NAME5
Tracked varTRACKED(T, a, b, c, schema = ..., default = ...): declares each var with its default and generates set_<v>(), validated against the schemanameof(v) on src4
Validated varSCHEMA(T, var, schema): the same schema for a var that is not tracked and is declared on its own line (a row column, a request or prompt field)the var4
Per-instance listLIST_STATE(T, var, alloc =, kind =, owns =, share =, row =, empty_differs =): the var, its four independent policies and two modifiersthe var4

MessageMSG_DEF(path, text)MSG(path)13
SystemSYSTEM_DEF(name)SSname2
  • Op keys are literals. A capability's ops are namespaced automatically: cover()'s "open" is "cover.open". A type's own ops are bare ("breaker").
  • Capability keys are (CAP_X, selector). The definition declares its selector explicitly with key =, naming a param or NONE (CAPABILITY_DEF(cover, CAP_COVER, key = name, ...)): nothing is inferred from parameter order, so changing a constructor's signature never changes its identity. Two covers on one type are cover("panel") and cover("hatch"), and configure, without and the state accessors take the selector. An identical repeat (the same interned signature) dedupes. The same key with different params is a build error naming both lines (section 11).
  • Three kinds of key: op keys, capability keys and tags are distinct. The build resolves every literal reference in extend, without, configure, shares_effects, perform_op, on_op and priority(above()). perform_op with an unknown key at runtime is an error, not a no-op.
  • Sources are a datum or a SOURCE_DEF(ai_control) flyweight (SRC_AI_CONTROL). Text or null is an error. An activation is a datum and a valid source. An op's holds() and grants() default their source to the op's activation when they land on its holder, so they end with it (outlives = TRUE opts out), and to the op source (the pair of holder and op key, owned by the target's store) when they land on another entity. source = SRC_HELD_ITEM opts in to "the item owns it while it is in that hand or slot", and an op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold is a build error. A per-actor source (source = A.actor) is the pattern for "this AI's bolts". Section 5 has the full rules.
  • Typed names elsewhere: sequence facts are typed vars on the sequence's context (F.alive), request fields are vars on the request datum set by named args, and UI args arrive as typed proc params (set_channel(datum/act/A, channel, mode)). The build checks the names against the declarations.
  • nameof(v) stays for tracked vars and relations on src. A hold or contribution on a type that does not declare the stat is an error naming the type and the stat.
  • Schema identifiers and runtime data. Stats, tracked vars, capability and tag keys, op keys, sources, stages and messages are schema: declared once and resolved by the build. The few keys that are values are runtime data: the key of a COOLDOWN_KEYED_* table (an ability id), the name vars_write receives, the id behind a keyed relation, and a capability selector computed at runtime. The build cannot check data, so each has one typed home and a runtime error on a miss, and each is validated when it arrives (by a schema, section 4). The rule is one distinction: what the build can resolve is declared, and what arrives as a value is data.
ACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on)     // a generator marker, not a plain macro. The build step emits /datum/act/fall,
                                                     // act_fall(holder, landing, landed_on) and the notice /datum/notice/fell

var/datum/act/fall/F = ACT_TRY(src, fall, T, M)      // the final act, after every adjusts(); ACT_PASS (nothing hooks it and no listener wants its notice, nothing allocated); or null (refused or taken over)
if(!F)
	return
...do the fall to ACT_FINAL(F, landing, T) and ACT_FINAL(F, landed_on, M): the act's fields, not your locals...
act_done(F)                                          // ends it ACT_COMMITTED and publishes the notice. act_cancel(F) releases without publishing

The notice is named for the verb in the past tense (/datum/notice/fell, inserted, buckled); override it with notice =. Every action ends with one outcome (ACT_COMMITTED, ACT_REFUSED or ACT_REPLACED), and the notice reaches a listener only for the outcomes it asked for, by default committed. A lint requires act_done or act_cancel on every path of the proc that called ACT_TRY. Section 8 lists the world actions.

Precedence

The same order applies everywhere, lowest to highest:

  1. library defaults (tool profiles, a capability's own parts);
  2. the op's own parts;
  3. group extends (extend(CAP_X | TAG_X | /datum/act/x)), in list order;
  4. extend("key").

At each level a subtype beats its parent. When candidates come from different entities, ties go target first, then held item, then actor, then subtype before parent, then capability declaration order. Requirements always accumulate, so no level can drop one that another level added, except by naming it (extend("key", drop = "id")). Single values (a wait, a priority, a message) take the most specific declaration. without(key) applies at its position in the list.

Capabilities granted at runtime (by a species, a trait, while_slotted or a body effect) are a second table beside the type's own, with the same precedence, resolved when the click happens. extend(CAP_X, ...) addresses a capability by key, so it applies to a granted one as well.

Interning and memory

  • Flyweights. Parts, requirements, ops and capabilities are interned by signature, so tool(TOOL_MULTITOOL) is one datum everywhere. That's already true today for capabilities and /datum/req.
  • Per-instance cost: stat vars; one bitfield per boolean stat for contribution results; the hold list, allocated only while something is held; per-activation capability data (a typed /datum/cap_data/<cap>, one per activation, allocated on first use); relation vars; and the lists declared with LIST_STATE.
  • Bit limits. DM numbers are exact to 24 bits, so a stat's static-contribution bitfield and a capability's state keys each fit 24 bits. More is a build error naming the stat or the capability.
  • Procs stay off base types. Library behaviour lives on capability datums or global procs taking the holder, never on /atom: each /atom proc costs about 0.5 MB, and B2 measured 13 MB saved for 27 procs.

Replaces:

  • Declaration procs: capabilities(), reactions(), relations(), ownership(), derived(), declared_capabilities(), CAPABILITY().
  • Macro families: DECLARE_INTERACTIONS and EXTEND_INTERACTIONS, every lifecycle DECLARE_*, REGISTRY_MEMBERSHIP, DECLARE_VERB*, DECLARE_APPEARANCE*, DECLARE_UI* and MAP_RESOLVER (as an entry).
  • Entries that were never one form: stat(), setting(), derive() and derive_<v>(), capability(KEY, entries..., settings = list()), and hand-written READS_AS for capability state.

2Kernel and systems

The tick

One kernel replaces the Master Controller, SSbehaviours (the OM scheduler), world lanes and the subsystem fire() model. Each server tick runs fixed phases under one budget, in the order of the table: K, S, N, U, D, P, R, G. The order is deliberate: input resolved in K reaches the same frame's native step, because S and N follow it, while work that is scheduled (deadlines in D, phased work in P) reaches the native step the next frame.

PhaseRuns
K hostHost systems, in a fixed order: the input inbox first, then the database, tgui transport, garbage, ticker and lighting
S simulation syncEverything Rust needs from DM, pushed immediately before the native step: the push_to_rust() outputs of entities whose pushed values changed, and the native_write() calls they make. Input resolved in K is therefore visible to the same frame's native step, for the effects settled inline (the written entity and one single-valued edge away, section 7); a marked fan-out lands when its drain completes. Never shed
N nativeThe native frame: the air system (the LINDA and Rust atmos driver), then the verdigris system's one vg_frame(elapsed, budget); its outbox becomes publishes, notices and band crossings
U urgentReserved slice for kernel_urgent() and on_cross(..., urgent = TRUE)
D deadlinesDue timers (after()), hold expiries, op waits ending
P phased workevery() work items and sequences (Life), ordered by runs_after edges, paid from lane budgets
R presentationQueued presentation outputs, once per entity: draw, ui_data and outputs() (looks, UI pushes, HUD). Nothing else runs here, and nothing here can delay a push to Rust, which is phase S
G garbageDeferred deletion work
  • The drain (section 7) runs at the end of every trigger and at the start of phases D, P, S and R, and these are the drain points. Hook attachment, on_change hooks and outputs wait for it, and so do marked stats, which recompute only in the drains at the start of D, P and R (the marked drains; phase U has no drain point). Stats on the written entity and one single-valued edge away do not wait: they are settled before the setter returns.
  • Lanes (LANE_SIMULATION, LANE_PRESENTATION, LANE_WORLD, ...) split the budget. A lane's budget is in microseconds of tick time per server tick, and the kernel profiler reports in the same unit. job(budget =) is the one exception: it is a percent of a tick. A marked stat is charged to its own lane: LANE_SIMULATION for an entity's stat (a machine's operable, an area's power channel, a light's range), and the lane a system declares for a stat on the system itself. Night shift's own lane = LANE_WORLD budgets its every() work; the area and light stats it causes are LANE_SIMULATION. The marked drains of D, P and R spend that lane's per-tick budget between them, and test_budget(lane, units) sets it. The budget bounds the marked evaluation only: hook attachment and detachment, on_change hooks and queued outputs always complete at their drain point.
  • Latency classes L0–L3 decide what's shed first under load. L0 is never shed: the input inbox, the host systems, phase S and urgent work. A system's class defaults from its lane.
  • Metrics: every work item, step and system reports its cost through metrics() into the kernel profiler, which replaces the OM profiler.

Systems

SYSTEM_DEF(power)                               // /datum/system/power, reachable as SSpower
/datum/system/power
	needs = list(/datum/system/atmos)              // boot order: one graph, validated (cycles are init errors)
	roles = list(POWER_ROLE_LOAD, POWER_ROLE_SOURCE)  // the membership roles this system accepts
	lane = LANE_SIMULATION
	latency_class = LATENCY_L2

CAPABILITIES(/datum/system/power)
	every(MACHINE_SERVICE_INTERVAL, then(PROC_REF(balance)))   // world-level periodic work
	every(2 SECONDS, then(PROC_REF(step_member)), members = CAP_POWERED_BY)   // once per member; the member is A.target
	on_notice(/datum/notice/round_started, then(PROC_REF(reset_grid)))

/datum/system/power/proc/balance(datum/act/A)
/datum/system/power/proc/step_member(datum/act/A)
/datum/system/power/proc/reset_grid(datum/act/A)

/datum/system/power/initialize()           // boot, after everything in `needs`
/datum/system/power/on_members_ready()     // after map members joined
/datum/system/power/on_shutdown()
  • Access: the SSx name that SYSTEM_DEF(x) generates. system(/datum/system/x) returns the same datum for generic code and tests. A system's state is VAR_PRIVATE; other folders call its api.dm procs only (system_boundary_lint).
  • On demand: a system with no work parks its items. every(when =) wakes it.
  • Membership is a relation, not a roster:
    • join(system, E, source, role) and leave(system, E, source, role);
    • members_of(key, role);
    • declared statically with membership(joins = ...).
    Roles are declared by the system with roles = list(...), and a join or a members_of with an undeclared role is a build error. A join is an activation (X1, section 5) with the system role as its definition and the joining source as its source: it lists the entity in members_of(key, role) and calls the system's on_member_join(E, role), and its teardown calls on_member_leave(E, role). Joins count sources, so the entity stays listed while any activation remains, and removal is O(1). A static membership() is the activation scoped to its holder, and to its when() if it has one. A registry (REGISTRY_*) is a membership key with no system. A member passes no callbacks: a system reads its members through its own api (for example power_draw(E)). powered() is a capability over powered_by(system, role), defined in the power domain (section 22).

Gas and heat

A persistent heat flow is a declared entry of the type that causes it, and lives exactly as long as its scope: at type level under when(...), under while_slotted(..., on = ON_CONTENTS) while an occupant is inside. Rust integrates it every world step with the exact solution, so any step length is stable and no pair passes equilibrium. An endpoint is HEAT_HOLDER (the occupant of a slotted entry, else the machine), HEAT_DECLARER, HEAT_AIR (the declaring side's air; re-made when it moves), HEAT_HULL (its turf's solid), HEAT_PORT(i) (the gas of the pipeline port i is in, through merges and splits), HEAT_SPACE, HEAT_SKY(K), HEAT_AMBIENT (the station's 20 °C heat-rejection loop, booked as leaving the station) or nameof(v) (a gas mixture or an atom the declaring side holds; an atom's heat body, a mob's body, a pipe machine's first port).

  • heat_link(a, b, conductance, emissivity = 0, area = 0): conduction, plus radiation εσA(Ta4−Tb4).
  • heat_pump(controlled, other, watts, target, mode = HEAT_PUMP_BOTH, resistive = FALSE, carnot_fraction = 0.5, max_cop = 10): drives controlled toward target at a Carnot-bounded COP; resistive heating turns work into heat one for one. It also contributes STAT_POWER_DRAW = watts, and heat_entries_bill(holder) is the exact work Rust booked since the last bill, which the machine pays from its cell or grid.
  • heat_engine(hot, cold, efficiency, conductance): efficiency of the heat that flows, capped at Carnot, leaves as electricity (heat_entries_power(holder), W).
  • One-off events: heat_move(from, to, joules, source) (from null: an external source; to null: an external sink), heat_add(thing, joules, HEAT_SOURCE_*), heat_set(thing, kelvin) (an authority write: spawn, admin, test), gas_react(air, GAS_REACTION_*, extent, deltas) (a gas reaction: Rust applies the moles and its enthalpy), heat_set_energy(thing, joules, source), heat_equalize(a, b, fraction), heat_conduct(a, b, conductance, seconds) (the exact pair solution over a stretch of time), heat_engine_once(a, b, efficiency), heat_set_solid(turf, kelvin). Every one is booked under its source in vg_heat_books().
  • A datum that is not an atom (a material batch, an assembly's material service) keeps a Rust heat body by handle: a heat store (heat_store_create(), heat_store_link(), heat_store_engine(), heat_store_add(), ...), named to the one-off procs as HEAT_STORE(h).
  • Rust wakes the pipe network or turf whose gas it changed; nothing marks a network dirty after heat.
  • A mob's body temperature is its heat body's (body_temperature()). Life links the body to its surroundings through clothing with set_surroundings(air, surface, sky_area): convection shared between its tile's air and the air of the tiles open to it, contact with the floor solid under it and the walls beside it, and radiation to the 2.7 K sky in space or near vacuum; all zero inside the comfort range. Its metabolism is the body's power (set_metabolic_power()).
  • Tooling: the admin verb Show Heat Links lists a reservoir's edges with each one's last step (heat out of a, heat into b, work in, work out) and the network's books.
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/atmospherics/unary/cryo_cell)            // the occupant and the cell's gas trade heat while it works and they are inside
	when(cond_all(nameof(cooling), STAT_OPERABLE), while_slotted(OCCUPANT_SLOT_CRYO, heat_link(HEAT_HOLDER, HEAT_PORT(1), CRYO_OCCUPANT_CONDUCTANCE), on = ON_CONTENTS))

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/space_heater)                              // a heat pump on the room's air, paid from the cell in machine_step()
	when(nameof(pumping), heat_pump(HEAT_AIR, HEAT_AMBIENT, nameof(heating_power), nameof(set_temperature), HEAT_PUMP_BOTH, TRUE))

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/atmospherics/pipe/simple/heat_exchanging)  // radiates to space through its surface
	when(PROC_REF(he_pipe_in_space), heat_link(HEAT_PORT(1), HEAT_SKY(HE_PIPE_SKY_TEMPERATURE), 0, 1, nameof(surface)))

/obj/machinery/power/generator/machine_step()                         // a TEG: one engine pass on the gas its circulators moved
	last_thermal_gen = heat_engine_once(air1, air2, thermal_efficiency)

Host systems

The machinery that wraps BYOND and the process is made of ordinary systems, declared with SYSTEM_DEF, with phase = KERNEL_PHASE_K (or KERNEL_PHASE_N) and latency_class = LATENCY_L0:

SystemPhaseOwns
inputKThe input inbox and client/Topic (below)
databaseKThe connection, round records and schema check; /datum/io/sql as backend (below)
tgui transportKWindow pool, prewarm, assets, open, close and transfer (section 13)
garbageKThe GC queue and hard-delete accounting; deferred deletion runs in phase G
tickerKRound state
airNThe LINDA and Rust atmos driver, in the native frame
verdigrisNvg_frame and its outbox
lightingKThe lighting update queue
  • There are no subsystems and no Master Controller. SUBSYSTEM_DEF, fire() and SS_KERNEL_HOSTED are deleted, so "no subsystems" is literally true. The Master Controller's MC_* metric defines keep their math under kernel names: KERNEL_AVERAGE, KERNEL_RUNNING and the rest of KERNEL_*.
  • Vendored code (TGS and the like) lives in code/host/vendor/, exempt from every lint and from the layer rules (section 15).

The input inbox

One inbox replaces SSinput, SSverb_manager, the speech lane and the click holdback.

  • Every client entry point (Click and its modifiers, drag, client/Topic, verbs, tgui actions, say and emote, held keys) records a typed input event. There are no opt-in macros and no callbacks. The inbox owns client/Topic.
  • Order and budget: an event resolves on the spot if the input budget has room, and queues otherwise. Phase K drains the queues round-robin across clients, each in arrival order, under the input budget, so one flooding client cannot starve the rest.
  • Bounded queues. Each client has an inbox of at most INPUT_CLIENT_MAX events (default 64). Repeatable inputs coalesce: held movement keys and repeated clicks on the same target keep only the latest. Over the cap the oldest coalescible input is dropped and logged; an input that cannot coalesce, such as admin commands and say, is never dropped. The inbox is never shed by load (L0), and it cannot grow without bound either.
  • Resolution happens at drain time. Each event goes through the action path (section 8) when it is drained, not when it arrived, so a target that has gone by then is dropped with the gate's reason.
  • Metrics: the tick meter's per-input wait metrics (arrival to resolution) live here.

Requests and waiting

asks(/datum/request/x, field = v, ..., step =, resume =, keeps =) // inside a trigger: a step of the op's Wait workflow (sections 9 and 13) request(owner, /datum/request/x, then(PROC_REF(done)), valid = PROC_REF(ok), field = v, ...) // outside a trigger; done(datum/act/A) gets the request as A.request sql_write(query, params) // parameterized only; format_table_name() for tables; nothing waits on it await(datum/waiter W) // suspends the calling chain; only the kernel and the request layer may suspend waiter_resolve(W, result) / waiter_cancel(W)

A request is one step of a workflow, foundation X3. Section 13 defines workflows, the one outcome enum, captured fields, cancellation and resumption; this section is the call forms and the kinds.

  • One concept. /datum/request is anything that waits for an answer. Its kinds are /datum/prompt/* (a player answers), /datum/io/sql, http, rustg_job and path (a backend answers), and /datum/client_query/winget, measure_text and the like (a client round trip).
  • What they share: typed fields, each with a schema (X5); entity fields held as handles that read null once the entity is gone; a timeout; a re-check when the answer arrives; cancellation when the owner dies; a typed outcome (A.request.outcome); and metrics.
  • Fields are typed vars on the request datum, set by named args and checked at build time. A prompt takes answerer =. A.answer is the answered request, typed (var/datum/prompt/text/rename/R = A.answer); A.request is the latest request whatever its outcome.
  • Field values. Inside a declaration (asks() in an op) a field value is a literal, nameof(var) (read from the holder, and captured when the op first suspends, so the op uses the value the player saw, section 13) or PROC_REF(x) (x(datum/act/A) is called then and returns the value). In request(), which runs in code, they are ordinary expressions.
  • valid = is a condition re-checked when the answer arrives, for a request() outside an op that has no op requirements to re-run: a ghost-role request checks that the role is still open, and an answer to a role that has gone ends as REQ_CANCELLED.
  • SQL kinds declare their query once: query = "SELECT ... WHERE id = :book_id", where each :field binds the request's typed var of that name, and a row type, row_type = /datum/io/sql/row/x, whose columns are schema-declared vars (SCHEMA, section 4). The answer's rows is a list of those row datums, one per result row. A query that matches nothing ends REQ_NO_RESULT, not an empty answer (16.13).
  • Examples: asks(/datum/io/sql/load_notes, ckey = PROC_REF(actor_ckey)) in an op; request(src, /datum/prompt/yes_no/ghost_role, then(PROC_REF(role_answered)), valid = PROC_REF(role_open), answerer = ghost, role = R) from a system.

The database

  • One system. system(/datum/system/db) owns the connection, the round records and the schema check, with /datum/io/sql as its backend. Content never opens a query itself.
  • Content has two forms: asks() or request() with a /datum/io/sql/* kind (a typed datum holding its parameterized query, its bound fields and a typed row type for the result), and sql_write() for writes nobody waits on. NewQuery() and Execute() are deleted.
  • db_query_now() blocks its caller. It exists for the two places BYOND itself waits: world/IsBanned and the boot schema check. The lint allows it nowhere else, and accepts no ALLOW.
  • Login lookups are asynchronous. A client is "ready" when its data arrives, not when it connects.

Chunked work and paths

job(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =) // step(datum/act/A) returns JOB_MORE or JOB_DONE; the job dies with its owner request(bot, /datum/io/path, then(PROC_REF(have_path)), from = start, to = goal)
  • Chunked one-shot jobs: a long job that must give the tick back is a job(). budget caps how much of a tick one step may use, as a percent of a tick. The map loader, site generation, the POI queue and zone cleanup are its first users, and replace their stoplag() and INVOKE_ASYNC. The loader is a host job, because BYOND's New() may sleep inside it.
  • Pathfinding: /datum/io/path is a request kind served by a budgeted path system. Each request is its own record, so there is no mutex and no spin-wait, and bot AI can be a non-sleeping every() step.

Faults, parsing and boot

  • Faults: safe_call(PROC_REF(x), args...) runs a proc and returns a /datum/result (ok, value, error) instead of letting a runtime escape. It is for code at a boundary that can fail by design. try_parse_json(text) returns the parsed value, or null for malformed text. Tests use EXPECT_RUNTIME(...) to assert a runtime. Raw try/catch lives only inside the kernel, where it isolates a faulting handler.
  • Boot-phase blocking IO: before the first tick nothing is waiting on the tick, so a system's initialize() may block on IO. The database system's boot step reads the schema, and the admin and rank tables that are needed before the first tick, with db_query_now(), and shell() is allowed there too. After the first tick the lint rejects both; db_query_now() remains legal only in world/IsBanned.

Replaces:

  • The Master Controller, SUBSYSTEM_DEF, fire() and SS_KERNEL_HOSTED; every subsystem, including SSinput and SSverb_manager (now the inbox).
  • SSbehaviours; OM lanes and world_lanes.dm.
  • /datum/world_service, GLOB.x_service, LAZY_SERVICE, boot_after and order_after.
  • /datum/cadence, CADENCE_* and PERIODIC_*.
  • System rosters (system.members, cap_system).
  • Requests: om_io, io_request, the ask() calls outside triggers, om_sql_write (now sql_write), NewQuery() and Execute(); each is a workflow step now (section 13).
  • Hand-rolled yields: stoplag() and INVOKE_ASYNC in the map loader, POI queue and zone cleanup (now job()); the pathfinder mutex.
  • Bare try/catch outside the kernel (now safe_call and try_parse_json).
  • The OM profiler.

3Time

Clocks

ClockMeaning
CLOCK_WORLDReal server time. Never paused.
CLOCK_OWN (default)The entity's own time. Pauses while it's suspended; runs at its clock_rate stat.
CLOCK_BIOBiological time. It runs at clock_rate times clock_rate_bio, so stasis slows or stops it on top of the entity's own clock. Stasis sources hold clock_rate_bio, a MIN stat with base 1, so the deepest stasis wins.

A clock's rate is a stat, so stasis beds, cryo, bellies and admin freezes are holds on that stat, not special code paths: while_slotted(SLOT_BUCKLE, holds(STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0.1), on = ON_CONTENTS) on a bed, or hold(M, STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0, source) with the admin as source.

A timed hold's lasts runs on the clock the hold names (clock =, default CLOCK_OWN). A hold on a clock-rate stat always runs on CLOCK_WORLD, because a stasis hold timed on the clock it slows would never end. There is one deadline per entity per clock. Every remaining-time read (hold_left, after_left, COOLDOWN_TIMELEFT, status_remaining) returns deciseconds on the clock of what it reads.

The forms

There are five time forms: after(), every(), hold(lasts =), COOLDOWN and STAMP/ELAPSED. Everything else here is a value they take, or belongs to another section. Named intervals such as MACHINE_SERVICE_INTERVAL and LIFE_CYCLE are plain defines.

NeedFormNotes
Do something once, laterafter(owner, delay, then(PROC_REF(x)), key =, clock =, with =); cancel_after(owner, key), after_pending(owner, key), after_left(owner, key)Stored as a timer relation: dies with the owner. x(datum/act/A) runs with A.holder = the owner. A keyed timer replaces an earlier one with the same key. Keys are literals, and a cancel_after on a key no after declares is a build error. with = list(...) arrives as A.args; it is for scalars and flyweights only, a datum in it is a lint error (reach entities through a relation), and the default is null, so nothing is allocated.
Do something later, owned by the current stateafter_in_state(delay, go(/datum/capability/x)) or after_in_state(delay, then(CAP_PROC(x)))An entry of a state capability (section 11, Modes). A timer the activation owns: armed when the state starts, cancelled when it ends, by any route (a go(), a revoke, the deletion of the holder). delay is a number or a PROC_REF, on the holder clock.
Do something later, inside an opdelayed(t, parts...)after() on the holder's clock, owned by the holder, so it dies with the holder (section 9).
Do something repeatedly while a condition holdsevery(interval, parts..., when =, lane =, key =), ordered with runs_after =interval is a number or a PROC_REF (computed per run). when is a condition: its reads are generated, and the work parks and wakes by itself. The handler gets A.dt, the time since its last run, so scale work by it. An entity's every() runs on its own clock (CLOCK_OWN). key = lets a subtype retune or drop it with extend("scan", ...) or without("scan"). Inside a system, members = CAP_X runs it once per member, with the member as A.target.
State that lasts a whilehold(E, STAT_X, value, source, lasts =)Section 5. Re-applying follows the stat's reapply rule. A timed hold outlives its source by default; an untimed one dies with it.
Rate limit, nothing reacts to itCOOLDOWN_DECLARE / COOLDOWN_START / COOLDOWN_FINISHED; for keys that are data (ability ids), COOLDOWN_KEYED_DECLARE / COOLDOWN_KEYED_START / COOLDOWN_KEYED_FINISHEDNo timer, one compare, defined as sugar over STAMP/ELAPSED. The keyed form is a lazy table that prunes itself, and the one place a key is data rather than a declared identifier. If anything must react when it ends (a UI "ready" light), use a hold instead. A per-op rate limit is the cooldown(t) part (section 9): when a button or window displays it, the engine schedules an invalidation at its expiry (an after() on the holder's clock), and a cooldown nothing displays publishes nothing.
"How long since"STAMP(v) / ELAPSED(v)The one timestamp form.
A timed player actionA wait(t) part on the opRe-checks everything when it ends, and cancels early when a requirement now refuses (section 9).
A random durationbetween(min, max)Usable wherever a duration is: lasts =, wait(), every(). It rolls when it is applied (each run, in every()), never at declaration, because declarations are interned and pure.
The time on an entity's clockclock_now(E, clock)Replaces om_clock_now.
A long job that must yieldjob(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =)Section 2.
An answer from a player or a serviceasks() / request(), with their timeoutSection 2.
Many triggers, one runon_notice(/datum/notice/x, coalesce(interval), then(...)); on_change(nameof(v), ANY, coalesce(interval), then(...))A part of an after hook (section 10). The first trigger of a quiet period opens one window of interval (deciseconds of the holder clock, or a PROC_REF answering them); triggers inside it are absorbed; the parts after coalesce() run once when it closes. At most one run per interval, nothing scheduled while nothing triggers, cancelled with the holder or the capability that brought the hook. Built on om_after_unique(), keyed by holder and hook.
Coalesce a noisy outputoutputs(..., at_most = t)At most one run per t per entity; held changes arrive together.
Must run before the next tickkernel_urgent(member, work, deadline); on_cross(..., urgent = TRUE)Reserved slice, kernel and systems only; a breach is a metric.
Per-entity ordered frames (Life)A /datum/sequence with seq_step() entriesBelow.

Only while a player is near. Cosmetic and ambient periodics park when nobody could see them: a type sets proximity_tracked = TRUE and writes every(interval, then(...), when = STAT_RELEVANCE). The proximity system (code/controllers/subsystems/proximity.dm) counts each client eye (the mob, or an AI camera, observer or remote view) into an 8-turf cell per z-level and holds RELEVANCE_NEAR on the tracked things in that cell and the eight around it, with the cell as the hold's source; the hold is released when the cell empties, and a thing that moves in or out follows. A carried thing stays relevant. every() parks on a stat when = the way it does on a tracked var. Work that changes simulation state does not opt in: it runs everywhere.

AI brains. A mob's brain is one loop on the mob, not an object model behaviour: an action loop (every(ai_action_interval, ..., when = STAT_RELEVANCE); its interval is the active tactic's tick_interval, x3 for IDLE and BACKGROUND tactics below RELEVANCE_VISIBLE, and with no tactic the action tick with a target and a 2 s calm tick without), with the slow work (idle choice, a backstop perception pass) inside it on its own cadence; a calm brain with nothing to do parks until its pack perceives something. It parks on the mob's relevance (a living player on its z-level) and runs on the mob's own clock, so stasis pauses it. The pack (/datum/ai_pack) is the unit of thinking and the brain the unit of acting: every brain is in a pack (of one unless its faction sets pack_join_radius); the pack perceives once for its members (coalesce() on chunk activity, a member hurt or heard; candidates from a per-chunk living-mob index; line of sight checked nearest member first, one lookup per hostile; members classify a sighting with their own standing_toward(); hostiles reach the other members after the faction's alert delay), picks targets once (SPREAD with a cap, or FOCUS) and shares one flow field per goal. A brain's state is modes(nameof(ai_state)): calm, alert, engaged, fleeing, regroup; each state declares which tactics it allows and how soon its pack perceives. What a brain thinks of another mob is standing_toward() of rows that providers place (faction relations, pack_member, serves(lord), grudges, effects, admin); a pack's leader is the member with the highest STAT_AI_AUTHORITY; roles (lord, sworn, sentinel) are capabilities and orders are intend() intents with a source and a lifetime. Tactics read the world only through brain accessors (known_hostiles(), primary_target(), path_to(), active_intents()) and never know whether a brain or its pack answers. A tactic is a flyweight /datum/ai_behavior that scores itself (evaluate) and acts (start/tick); what it does to the world goes through the same ops a player's input reaches, with the mob as actor (perform_op(mob, target, "mob_attacks.melee"), natural_weapon.attack), so the AI meets the same requirements, cooldowns and refusals, and a refusal fails the tactic. Reactions (a hit, a sound) are on_notice handlers that only set brain state and wake it early (invalidate_selection()); they never act. AI mobs do not opt in to proximity_tracked: they change simulation state (see above), so they keep running while any player shares their z-level. A brain is traced with brain.traced or GLOB.ai_trace_all; a pack with pack.trace() (formation, joins, leaves, merges, splits, perception passes, assignments, state changes).

The old spellings, and what each became

The old forms table had 14 rows. Each is listed here with what it became; two more spellings lived in section 9.

Old spellingBecame
1. after(owner, delay, then(...), key =, clock =, with =), with cancel_after, after_pending, after_leftKept as after(). Keys are literals the build checks, and with = is scalars and flyweights only, delivered as A.args.
2. every(interval, parts..., when =, members =, phase =, after =, budget =, lane =)every(interval, parts..., when =, lane =, key =) with runs_after = for order. after = is runs_after =. members = remains only inside a system. phase = and budget = are gone: the lane sets the budget.
3. hold(..., lasts =)Kept. Re-apply follows the stat's reapply.
4. COOLDOWN_DECLARE / START / FINISHEDKept, as sugar over STAMP/ELAPSED, plus the COOLDOWN_KEYED_* family for data keys.
5. STAMP(v) / ELAPSED(v)Kept: the one timestamp form.
6. wait(t) partKept, as the Wait stage of an op (section 9).
7. lifetime = N / expire(N)expire(N), the lifecycle verb (section 6), which is an after() underneath. The type var is gone: a type that always expires calls expire(N) from Initialize. It works on any entity.
8. between(min, max)Kept, as a value that rolls when applied.
9. clock_now(E, clock)Kept.
10. callable(owner, PROC_REF(x), args) / invoke()Deleted. then(PROC_REF(x)) is already a deferred call, and requests carry their own typed fields.
11. after_init(delay, parts...)Stays as the entry for an after() armed when the instance initializes. It is not a separate mechanism.
12. output(..., at_most = t)outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =) (section 7).
13. request_urgent / on_cross(..., urgent = TRUE)Kept for the urgent slice, with request_urgent() renamed kernel_urgent() so that "request" means only /datum/request. Kernel and systems only.
14. seq_step in a /datum/sequenceKept for per-entity ordered frames. after = is runs_after = and the facts are typed.
Section 9: then_later(delay, parts...)delayed(t, parts...): the holder's clock, owned by the holder, dies with it.
Section 9: cooldown(t) partStays an op part. It is the same one compare as COOLDOWN.

Sequences

/datum/sequence/life
	interval = LIFE_CYCLE              // one frame per cycle of the mob's CLOCK_BIO
	max_catchup = LIFE_MAX_CATCHUP
	min_relevance = RELEVANCE_NEAR     // parked out of the sweep below this

/datum/seq_frame/life                  // the frame: pooled, a /datum/act subtype. Its facts are typed vars
	var/placed
	var/alive

/datum/seq_frame/life/compute_facts()  // once per frame, before any step
	placed = isturf(holder.loc)
	alive = holder.stat != DEAD

CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/carbon/human)   // excerpt: the full list is in 16.4
	seq_step(PROC_REF(life_breathing), runs_after = LIFE_INPUT, when = PROC_REF(placed_and_alive),
		should_run = PROC_REF(life_breathing_due))   // the reads of both are generated

/mob/living/carbon/human/proc/placed_and_alive(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/seq_frame/life/F = A
	return F.placed && F.alive
  • Ordering: steps are procs on the entity type, ordered by runs_after edges and anchors.
  • Sleeping: a step sleeps when should_run returns FALSE, and wakes when one of its generated reads publishes. A rewake timer is optional (rewake =).
  • Frames: the facts (placed, alive) are typed vars on the pooled frame, computed once per frame, and when is any condition over them. A step returns STEP_ABORT to end the frame.
  • Parking: a member is in the sweep only while it has an awake step and is relevant.
  • Missed wakes: the missed-wake audit fails test builds.
  • Outside the sweep: run_step_now(E, PROC_REF(step)) runs one step immediately (after a revive, say), through the same facts and gates. The rest of the sequence machinery is internal: facts are frame vars and plans are built once per type, so seq_condition, seq_replan and seq_plan_key are gone.

Replaces:

  • Timers: om_after and its variants (unique, replace, stagger, drift, list), after_slot, OWN_TIMER, om_cancel_timer, om_deadline (now internal), om_qdel_after (now expire), callable/invoke and om_callable/om_run, and then_later (now delayed).
  • Periodic work: PERIODIC_START, DECLARE_PERIODIC_WHILE(_ALL), DECLARE_REPEAT, periodic_cadence/periodic_interval/periodic_step, machine_step, MACHINE_WAKE/SLEEP.
  • Tasks: om_task_start, om_task_periodic, om_task_slices (the last is now job()).
  • Timed state: timed_set, EXPIRY_* (except the stamp), hand-written revert timers.
  • Life machinery: DECLARE_START_TIMER, OM pipelines, stages, plans, variants, string facts and seq_condition.

4Tracked state

Two concepts cover all state. Settings and plain state are vars one party writes: they are TRACKED, with a schema when the value has a legal range. A stat is effective state that several things can force: a value composed from contributions by one rule (section 5). Two declarations refine them. cap_keys() generates a capability's state bits as typed keys, and LIST_STATE declares how a per-instance list is allocated. Nothing else holds state. This section also defines foundation X5, value schemas: the one declaration of what a value may be, used wherever a value is checked, shown or stored.

TRACKED(T, a, b, c) // variadic: declares each var (the schema's zero value unless default = says otherwise) and generates T/proc/set_<var>(value): validate, compare, write, mark dependents dirty TRACKED(T, a, b, schema = int(0, 100), normalize = PROC_REF(x), default = 50, persist = TRUE, on_invalid = CLAMP) // the options apply to every var on the line SCHEMA(T, var, schema, normalize =, default =, persist =) // the same schema for a var that is not tracked and is declared on its own line: a row column, a request or prompt field STAT(T, name, RULE, ..., schema =) // composed state: declares the var, its base and the id STAT_NAME (section 5) cap_keys(CAP_X, KEY = MSG(x), ...) // capability state: generates the key ids, accessors and default reasons LIST_STATE(T, var, alloc = LAZY | EAGER, kind = LIST | SET | MAP, owns = NONE | ROWS, share = NONE | COW_TABLE, row =, empty_differs =) // a per-instance list: four independent choices and two modifiers
  • Settings and plain state are declared by TRACKED, which declares the var and generates the setter. The default = option is the one home of the default (the schema's zero value when it is absent: 0, FALSE, null or the first enum choice), so a separate var/x = 5 line for a tracked var is a lint error. A TRACKED line for a var that a parent type already declares only marks it tracked, and default = on it resets the default for that subtype. The lint rejects any write outside the setter, and admin edits go through it (vars_write, below). A setter has no hand-written body. The side effects of a change are an on_change hook (section 10), and a value that must be limited or normalised declares a schema (below). A stat is for a value that several things can force, never for validation.
  • Settings vs effective state. A var is a setting (what someone chose) or effective state (what is currently true), never both. Effective state that more than one thing can force is a stat. The turret's enabled is a setting; its armed is a stat.
  • Base vars. Three base vars are tracked: anchored (its side effects are an on_change hook), broken (written only by atom_break() and atom_fix(), section 14) and client on mobs (published by Login and Logout). density and opacity are stats, because more than one source forces them.
  • Derived values are stats: STAT(T, v, FORMULA, formula = PROC_REF(f)) is one formula, evaluated in the stat layer, with generated reads. There is no separate derive form and no magic derive_<v>() name.
  • Capability state is declared once, next to the capability, and each fact exists once. emagged, locked and the cover and panel bits are keys of their capabilities, not machine fields as well. A bit that several things can force is a stat instead.
  • Keys that aren't one var (MOB_KEY_EQUIPMENT, OCCUPANTS_KEY) are published by their producer with PUBLISH_CHANGE(E, key). An accessor proc that stands for such a key declares it with READS_AS (section 7).

Value schemas (X5)

A schema is the declared kind of a value. One schema drives every place the value is checked, shown or stored, so none of them is written by hand beside a setter, an arg or a column.

KindAcceptsNotes
boolTRUE or FALSE
int(min, max)A whole numberBounds are optional: int alone is any integer. Input that is not whole is refused, and an internal setter write rounds
num(min, max, step)A numberstep snaps the value to a grid
enum(LIST)One of a list of ids or textsThe list may be a PROC_REF that returns it, for choices known only at runtime
flags(LIST)A bitmask of the declared flagsAt most 24 flags (DM numbers are exact to 24 bits)
text(max_len, sanitize =)A string no longer than max_lensanitize = names a rule from a closed list (SANITIZE_NAME, SANITIZE_PLAIN, ...), so "sanitize free text" is a declaration, not a call to remember
ref(type)An entity of that type, held as a handleUI and request input name it by token (section 6)
path(type)A type path that descends from type
list_of(schema), map_of(k, v)A list, or an associative list, whose members have the given schemas
row(type)A typed record row, such as a SQL row, whose own vars carry schemasAn inline row(field = schema, ...) is allowed in ui_shape
OptionMeaning
normalize = PROC_REF(x)Runs after validation and returns the value to store (trim, lower-case, snap). x(datum/act/A, value) is pure: A is the evaluation context of the holder and value is the validated input
default =The one home of a default: the value when nothing has been written, checked against the schema at build time. TRACKED and SCHEMA take it as an option beside schema =. A capability param or an op arg, whose value slot holds the schema, takes it as an option of the schema constructor (num(1 SECOND, 5 MINUTES, default = 30 SECONDS)). A capability param with no schema keeps its default in the param = default of the definition line, and nowhere else. Never both, and never a var line as well (CAPABILITY_TYPE declares the param's var itself)
persist = TRUEThe var is saved with the round or the preferences file, and loaded back through the same validation
on_invalid = CLAMP | REJECTWhat a failed write does. CLAMP is the default for a numeric schema and REJECT for the others
reason =The message shown when input is refused. Default: MSG_DEF(schema/<kind>, ...)

A write that fails. In a setter, a number outside its range is clamped (and logged) and any other failure is rejected (logged, and the old value stays), unless on_invalid says otherwise. An int setter rounds a fractional value from an internal write to the nearest whole number before it tests the range. Input that arrives from a player, a UI arg, a prompt answer or a request field, is checked at the boundary, before Match and before any handler runs (for a UI press, against the op that the action name selects). A value of the wrong kind, a non-integer for an int, or one that on_invalid = REJECT refuses, ends the op ACT_REFUSED with the schema's reason, and publishes nothing beyond what any refused op does (on_op listeners that asked for ACT_ANY). A number outside its range is clamped the way a setter would clamp it. Clamp logs are rate limited per source and var: the first is written in full, then one summary line per SCHEMA_LOG_INTERVAL (10 seconds) with a count.

Where a schema appliesWhat it decides
TRACKED setterValidates each write, then normalises; a failure clamps or rejects and logs
STATThe base value's schema. Test builds validate every contribution against it: a contribution of 7 to a num(0, 5) stat is an error naming the source
Capability paramsA param may declare one: recharge = num(1 SECOND, 5 MINUTES, default = 30 SECONDS). configure() is checked at build time, and the default is the schema's
Op argsarg(name, schema) validates UI and topic input before Match, and the handler receives a typed parameter. arg(name, from = nameof(v)) takes the tracked var's schema (section 9)
UI dataA ui_shape() field names a tracked var and takes its schema, or gives one. The generated TypeScript types come from the schemas: int and num are number with their range in a doc comment (/** int 0..100 */), enum a union of literals, text a string with its length in a doc comment (section 13)
Prompts and requestsEach prompt kind's value and each request field has a schema; an answer that fails it is refused with the schema's reason (section 13)
SQL rowsThe columns of a row_type are SCHEMA vars. A row that fails ends the request REQ_FAILED with the column named
Preferences and persistencepersist = TRUE vars are serialised and loaded through their schema; an old saved value that no longer validates is normalised or dropped, with a log line
VV and admin editsvars_write() validates against the schema, and VV draws the control it implies: a slider for num, a dropdown for enum, check boxes for flags
 
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/atmospherics/pump, target_pressure, schema = num(0, MAX_PUMP_PRESSURE, step = 1), default = ONE_ATMOSPHERE)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/atmospherics/pump)
	interface("Pump", title = "Gas Pump")
	ui_shape(target_pressure)   // a var named here takes its schema
	op("set_pressure", ui_act(arg("pressure", from = nameof(target_pressure))), then(PROC_REF(set_pressure)))
/obj/machinery/atmospherics/pump/proc/set_pressure(datum/act/A, pressure)   // pressure is already valid and typed
	set_target_pressure(pressure)
// A UI write of 99999 reaches the handler as MAX_PUMP_PRESSURE and is logged; a text value is refused with the schema's reason.
// The generated types carry the range as a doc comment, which is what E0's tenth proof compares with the schema:
//   type PumpData = { /** num 0..MAX_PUMP_PRESSURE step 1 */ target_pressure: number };
//   type PumpActions = { set_pressure: { /** num 0..MAX_PUMP_PRESSURE step 1 */ pressure: number } };

Capability state: cap_keys

cap_keys(CAP_COVER, OPEN = MSG(cover/closed), REMOVED = MSG(cover/removed))
// generates the ids COVER_OPEN and COVER_REMOVED, the accessors cover_open(holder) and cover_removed(holder)
// (always <cap>_<key>(holder): panel_open, lock_locked, emag_emagged),
// a default reason for each key, and the read registration
toggles(COVER_OPEN)   sets(COVER_REMOVED, TRUE)   needs(req_is(COVER_OPEN))   when(COVER_OPEN)
  • Storage: each key is a bit in its capability's own state word, held in the activation's capability data, which exists once a bit is set. A capability has at most 24 keys (DM numbers are exact to 24 bits), and more is a build error. The id carries (capability, bit), so two capabilities never collide.
  • Publishing: a write publishes that key on the holder, if anything reads it. Reads are generated: no hand-written READS_AS for capability state.
  • Reasons: the message on a key is the default reason for the requirement the library itself writes on that key: req_is(COVER_OPEN) says the cover is closed, and req_is(COVER_REMOVED, FALSE) says it has been removed. A requirement written the other way round gives because = (section 9).
  • Writing keys: keys are written with the op parts toggles() and sets() (section 9) or through the generated accessors. Raw writes to the state word are build errors.

Per-instance lists and records

A list that really is per-instance and is neither a relation to other entities nor a constant table is declared, so the instance-list lint passes without an annotation. An undeclared var/list/x = list() on an instance is the only thing the lint still rejects. Four choices are made independently, and each has a default.

DimensionChoicesMeaning
allocLAZY (default), EAGERLAZY: null until the first add, and null again when emptied (empty_differs = TRUE keeps an empty list distinct from null, for checks that treat the two differently). EAGER: allocated with the instance, for a list that is almost never empty
kindLIST (default), SET, MAPA plain list; a set, where adding a member twice is a no-op; or an associative list whose key and value take schemas (map_of). A set whose members have sources (a language granted by a trait) is a set stat (section 5) instead
ownsNONE (default), ROWSMembers are plain values or references; or owned /datum/record rows (row = /datum/record/x), written with the owns_many writes
shareNONE (default), COW_TABLEPer-instance only; or a shared per-type table (TYPE_TABLE) copied on the first write, for species and type defaults an instance may rarely edit
 
LIST_STATE(/datum/mind, objectives)                                    // lazy list: the defaults
LIST_STATE(/obj, req_access, empty_differs = TRUE)                     // an empty list and null mean different things to access checks
LIST_STATE(/obj/machinery/computer/chess, board, owns = ROWS, row = /datum/record/chess_square)
LIST_STATE(/datum/species, languages, kind = SET, share = COW_TABLE)   // a set from a shared per-type table, copied on the first edit

The four combine freely: an owned set that is lazy and copied on write is a legal declaration. Two modifiers go with them: empty_differs = TRUE (with alloc = LAZY) and row = /datum/record/x (with owns = ROWS). Writes go through rel_add, rel_remove, rel_clear and, to replace a shared table, rel_set (section 6); owned rows use the owns_many writes.

vars_write(E, name, value, source = SRC_VV) // the one by-name write: VV, SDQL, state serializers, the map loader, DNA and record copy
  • The only computed key. Every other key in this design is a declared identifier the build resolves. vars_write resolves its name at runtime because VV, serializers and the loader have nothing else, so a lint allows it only in those files, each with a reason code.
  • It routes by what the var is. A tracked var goes through its setter. A stat becomes a hold_override by source, so VV shows "override by <admin>" and the edit is removable. A relation or LIST_STATE var goes through the rel_* writes. A plain undeclared var is written as it is.
  • No restore-by-initial. Nothing writes initial(var) back to undo a change. A change that must be undone is a contribution or a hold, which is released and restores the composed value.

Which form do I use

NeedUse
A value only one thing setsTRACKED var
A value several things can forceSTAT, fed by contributes and hold
A value computed from other stateSTAT(..., FORMULA)
A bit of one capability's statecap_keys
An effect while a condition holdscontributes on a stat, or when(cond, entries...)
An effect for a periodSee the table in section 5
A value with a legal range or a fixed set of choicesTRACKED(T, v, schema = ...) (X5)
A list that changes per instanceLIST_STATE
A link to another objectref_one / ref_many / owns_one / owns_many / link
An object that sits inside the holderslot
Membership in a systemmembership / powered_by
React once when state changeson_change

Replaces:

  • OM_FIELD, OM_FLAG_FIELD, OM_DERIVE_FIELD, OM_FIELD_SETTER, 3-argument TRACKED, SETTER and hand-written setters, and TRACKED_BRIDGED. Until the machine and Life pipelines that consume the legacy channels are deleted, a TRACKED setter also raises the CHANGE_* channel recorded for it (section 19).
  • om_changed and changed(E, CHANNEL), the CHANGE_* channels, and the 368 manual *_changed() calls.
  • derive() with derive_<v>(), setting(), and from = lists on stats.
  • cap_set's catch-all publish, machinery fields duplicated as capability bits, hand-written READS_AS for capability state, and stat_add/stat_remove for owned bits.
  • Trait var_changes loops that write vars[] and restore with initial() (section 5).
  • Hand-written clamps, sanitize calls and range checks beside setters, UI handlers, prompt callbacks and SQL row readers (now a schema), and LIST_STATE's single policy word (now four dimensions).

5Stats, holds, grants and overrides

A stat is a value composed from contributions by one rule. Its effective value sits in a plain var that only the engine writes. It recomputes eagerly and inline when a contribution, a hold or a read it depends on changes, before the writer's next line (on the written entity and one single-valued edge away; farther stats are marked for the next marked drain, section 7), and it publishes when its value changes. The engine is the existing OM contribution store (contribution.dm): rows of (stat, source, value, expiry, key), timed and held entries, release on source deletion, combine rules, re-apply rules and composites. Old om_apply and om_hold callers and the new forms share that store, so they interoperate while the migration runs. This section is its final API.

Scoped activation (X1)

Everything that is applied to a holder for a while is an activation: one record with a definition, a source, a holder and a scope. A grant, a conditional entry, a slot effect, an observer, a membership, a species' capabilities, a worn item's effects and a timed hold are not separate mechanisms with their own lifetime rules. Each is an activation, and each form in this document names the scope it uses.

FormDefinitionSourceScope (lifetime)
grant(E, x, source)xthe sourceWhile the source exists, and until revoke
Entry-level when(cond, entries...)the entriesthe holder's typeWhile cond holds
while_slotted(SLOT, entries..., on =)the entriesthe occupying item for ON_HOLDER; the slot entry, the owning type's declaration per holder, for ON_CONTENTSWhile the item is in that slot
observe(source, notice, listener, parts...)a hookthe listenerWhile the listener exists
membership(), join(system, E, source, role)a system rolethe sourceWhile the source exists, or until leave
species_capabilities()the species' bundlethe speciesWhile the mob's species relation names it: a species change is a relation write, and an edge of the graph
Worn or held item effects (SRC_HELD_ITEM)the entriesthe itemWhile the item is in that hand or slot; separately, until the item is deleted
Timed holds(..., lasts =)a holdthe op, or the source namedFor t. A hold on another entity outlives its source by design; a hold an op's activation places on its own holder ends with the activation (below)
grant(..., lasts =), timed grantsxthe sourceFor t or while the source exists, whichever ends first
  • One record shape. An activation holds its definition key, its source, its holder, its params (checked against their schemas, section 4), its state (cap_data lives here, one per activation), the contributions it made (stat contributions, hooks, ops, observes, outputs, owned grants), its scope, and the stacking policy of its definition (section 11). One source applies one definition to one holder once.
  • Type-level entries are lazy. A type-level capability, and an entry-level when() block, do not allocate an activation per instance. A type-level capability gets one activation per (holder, definition), created on the first state write or the first cap_data read; entry-level when() blocks are bits in a per-holder mask, not records. A granted capability has its activation from the grant.
  • One attach path. Build the contributions and validate them (schemas, the cycle check of section 7) before anything is applied, so a failure applies nothing: grant() returns the activation, or null with the reason logged. Stat contributions apply at once, and the stats that read them recompute inline; hooks, ops, observes and outputs are registered at once and go live at the next drain (section 7), so an attach is late by a drain.
  • Detach is not late. Revoking, expiry, or the deletion of the source or the holder marks the activation dead synchronously, and dispatch skips a dead activation at once: a hook of a revoked activation never fires after revoke returns. Only the rebuild of the interned indexes waits for the drain.
  • One teardown path. When the scope ends, the source is deleted, the holder is deleted or the activation is revoked, the same steps run in this order: end the activations it owns, remove its contributions (the stats recompute inline), run its on_deactivate, and drop its record.
