The destroy transaction calls this for every activation of a dying holder or source. An activation may end itself from inside one of its own handlers (the phase shift's drain handler in 16.9 ends the shift that owns it): DM cannot unwind a running proc, so that handler finishes, then the record drops, and nothing of the activation runs again.
  • Ownership. What an activation starts that has a lifetime of its own, a hold or a grant its ops place on its holder, a child activation, is owned by it and ends with it, timed or not. outlives = TRUE on the hold or grant makes it independent, sourced by the op source instead.
  • Scope changes are graph edges. A while cond scope re-evaluates when the inputs of its condition change, and a while slotted scope when the slot changes, through the same tracked dependency graph as every other read (section 7). Nothing polls, and nothing is released by hand.
  • Explain. explain_activations(E), the admin verb "List Activations", prints every activation on an entity with its source, scope and contributions, in production builds too (section 15).
explain_activations(Rae)                     // admin verb "List Activations"
  CAP_PHASE_SHIFT     source species/shadekin          scope while species set        owns CAP_PHASED
  CAP_PHASED          source activation CAP_PHASE_SHIFT  scope until revoked           contributes density=FALSE (force), invisibility, acts_via, every 1 s
  CAP_MIRROR_PLATING  source laserproof vest           scope while worn (SLOT_SUIT)   stacks BEST(reflect_chance): winning
  CAP_MIRROR_PLATING  source mirror shield             scope while worn (SLOT_BACK)   stacks BEST(reflect_chance): shadowed

Declaring

STAT(/obj/machinery, operable, ALL)                        // declares var/operable and the id STAT_OPERABLE (an int)
STAT(/obj/machinery, power_draw, SUM)                      // loads add
STAT(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, armed, ALL)
STAT(/mob/living, acts_via, MASK_AND, base = ORIGIN_ALL)
STAT(/mob/living, clock_rate_bio, MIN, base = 1)           // stasis: the deepest source wins, not the product
STAT(/mob/living, stun, MAX, reapply = REAPPLY_MAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE)   // a status: numeric intensity
STAT(/area, lights_nightshift, ANY)
STAT(/obj/machinery/power/apc, charge_percent, FORMULA, formula = PROC_REF(compute_charge_percent))   // derived: one formula
  • One line declares everything. STAT declares the var, its base, its id STAT_<NAME> and its rule, so the var line and the rule can't disagree. It is a file-level declaration like TRACKED, not an entry. Body factors (BF_*) are STAT_* ids in this same namespace.
  • One id per name. The id is per name, not per type. Two types may declare the same name only with the same rule and the same base, which is how one meaning is shared across unrelated types (operable on every machine and on anything else that can lose power). The same name with a different rule or base is a build error naming both declarations.
  • A stat belongs to its type. A contributes() line or a hold that names a stat the type doesn't declare is an error naming the type and the stat: a build error for a declaration, a runtime error for a hold. A subtype can't redeclare a stat with another rule (build error).
  • Base. The base is the value when nothing contributes. It comes from the rule (the table below) or from base =. A type changes it by setting the var in its own definition: light_range = 4 is a type-level contribution of 4 at default priority, read when the instance initializes. A map edit of a stat var becomes an instance contribution whose source is the map (the generated varmap, section 15).
  • schema = (X5, section 4) gives the base value and every value a contribution may carry a declared kind and range: STAT(/obj/machinery/pump, flow_limit, MIN, base = 100, schema = num(0, 100)). Test builds validate every contribution and hold against it, so a contribution of 140 to that stat is an error naming its source.
  • Reads are plain var reads. A stat on the written entity, or one single-valued edge away, is settled before the setter that changed its inputs returns, so the next line reads the new value. A stat reached through a collection or membership edge, a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR or two or more edges is marked for the next marked drain (section 7).
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery)
	contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, PROC_REF(has_power))   // a condition: x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSE; reads generated
	contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, not(nameof(broken)))
	contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, req_built_final())   // a library requirement's boolean view; its reason is not used here
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/porta_turret)   // excerpt: the full list is in 16.3
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, nameof(enabled))   // a tracked setting
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, STAT_OPERABLE)   // another stat
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/floodlight)
	contributes(STAT_LIGHT_RANGE, 4)   // a fixed value on a numeric stat
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/door)
	when(DOOR_OPEN, contributes(STAT_DENSITY, FALSE, priority = PRIORITY_FORCE))   // TOP stat: an open door is not solid. DOOR_OPEN is an input of the stat, so density is right the moment the door opens
// inside the absorbed capability that a belly grants:
contributes(STAT_ACTS_VIA, ORIGIN_NONE, reason = MSG(vore/absorbed))   // MASK_AND: no input at all; the actor gate (section 8) shows the reason
contributes(STAT_X, cond | value | STAT_Y, priority =, reason =, key =) contributes_to(nameof(rel), STAT_X, cond | value, priority =, key =) // a contribution to a stat on a related entity
  • Conditions. A condition is boolean with no reason. On a boolean stat a contribution is a condition. On a numeric stat a PROC_REF(x) returns the number, and its reads are generated the same way, so it recomputes when they change. A requirement (section 9) is a condition plus a reason, and a contribution never needs one.
  • reason = is a message type shown by a gate that reads the stat (acts_via, can_act, operable). When the gate refuses, the strongest refusing contribution or hold supplies the message.
  • priority = orders contributions where order matters (TOP, and overrides). It is an integer. Named levels: PRIORITY_DEFAULT (0), PRIORITY_FORCE (100), PRIORITY_ADMIN (1000).
  • key = names the entry so a subtype can drop it with without("key") (section 9).
  • contributes_to puts a contribution on a stat of the entity a relation names, for example the APC's night-shift setting on its area's lights_nightshift. It is released when the relation changes or either end is deleted.
FormAccepted as a condition
nameof(v), STAT_X, a cap keyA truthy test of that var, stat or key
not(c), all_of(c...), any_of(c...)Combinators over conditions
PROC_REF(x), CAP_PROC(x)x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSE. A.holder is the entity. PROC_REF runs on the holder; CAP_PROC runs on the capability datum
A library requirement datumIts boolean view

Strings are never conditions. A condition that gates a stat contribution is an input of that stat: its reads are the stat's reads, and the stat recomputes inline when they change. A condition that matches something (a contribution's value, when() on an op, look_layer(when =)) is evaluated when it is read. A condition that gates hooks, observes or outputs (an entry-level when() around them, every(when =) starting work, an on_change edge, while_slotted) is evaluated at the drain (section 7). An op under when() is not attached or detached: its applicability is read when resolution reads it. Outside an op the kernel passes a reused /datum/act/eval context with holder set, and conditions run under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs. A condition used inside an op's Match or Require gets that op's own context instead, with its actor, held item and target (section 9).

The combine rules

RuleEffective valueBaseValue typeTie-breakVV override
ALLTRUE when every contribution allowsTRUEBooleanNone: every contribution countshold_override, either way
ANYTRUE when any contribution holdsFALSEBooleanNonehold_override, either way
SUMThe sum of the contributions0NumberNoneThe override replaces the sum; it does not add into it
PRODUCTThe product of the contributions1NumberNoneThe override replaces the product
MAX / MINThe extreme contribution, or the base when there are noneThe stat's base =NumberNoneThe override replaces the extreme
TOPThe value of the highest-priority contribution, or the base when there are noneThe stat's base =AnyHighest priority; then the most recentAn override is a contribution at PRIORITY_ADMIN
SETThe union of the membersThe empty setTokens (paths, ids)None: a set has no tiesGrant or revoke by the admin; no override
MASK_ANDThe bits every contribution allowsAll bits (base =)Bitmask, at most 24 bitsNoneThe override replaces the mask
MASK_ORThe bits any contribution sets0Bitmask, at most 24 bitsNoneThe override replaces the mask
FORMULAThe result of one formula proc, evaluated in the stat layerThe formula's ownAnyNone: it takes no contributionsThe override replaces the result until released

Body-factor rules map onto this table: MULT is PRODUCT, ADD is SUM, MAX and MIN are unchanged, and FLAGS is MASK_OR. The two indexed factor families become one stat per index: BF_ARMOR(kind) is a SUM stat per armour kind (STAT_ARMOR_BLUNT to STAT_ARMOR_BLAST), and BF_INCOMING(category) a PRODUCT stat per injury category (STAT_INCOMING_PHYSICAL, STAT_INCOMING_THERMAL, ...). The body domain's injury_armor(kind) is the one place that picks one of them by a kind known only at runtime. acts_via is MASK_AND: an actor can act through an origin only if every contribution allows it.

Base stats, declared once

StatOnRuleBaseFed by
operable/obj/machineryALLTRUEPower, broken, build state, EMP and admin holds
power_draw/obj/machinerySUM0Each capability's load
can_act/mob/livingALLTRUEThe status stats (stun, paralysis, sleep), unconsciousness
can_move/mob/livingALLTRUEConscious and upright, restraints, grabs
acts_via/mob/livingMASK_ANDORIGIN_ALLAbsorption (ORIGIN_NONE), possession, remote control, phase shift (abilities only)
density/atomTOPFALSEThe type's own density = TRUE, open doors, phase shift, lying down
opacity/atomANYFALSEThe type's own opacity = TRUE, smoke, closed doors
invisibility, light_range/atomMAX0Cloaks, phase shift; lamps, coatings, glow
suspended/atom/movableANYFALSEAbsorbed prey, bodies kept for reforming
clock_rate/atomMIN1Suspension, admin freezes
clock_rate_bio/mob/livingMIN1Stasis beds, cryo, bellies
stun, paralysis, sleep, .../mob/livingMAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE0Statuses (below)
  • One stun path. Statuses contribute to can_act and nothing holds can_act by hand: contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_STUN)) on /mob/living. stunned is has_status(STATUS_STUN), not a second stat.
  • Density is a stat. A thing forced non-dense by phase shift, an open door or a collapsed mob needs a contribution to density, so it can't stay a tracked setting. anchored stays TRACKED (section 4). Side effects of a density or opacity flip (adjacency, lighting) are on_change hooks on the stat. Vars that BYOND reads directly (density, opacity, luminosity and the like) are always stats recomputed inline: they can never be pulled on read, because the engine reads the var itself.
  • There is no busy stat. An actor's busy state is its pending op (section 9), a kernel record with a busy policy. A thing that is occupied for a while (a slot machine spinning, a scanner recharging) declares a stat named for that state, STAT(/obj/machinery/slot_machine, spinning, ANY), holds it with lasts =, and its ops refuse with req_is(STAT_SPINNING, FALSE, because = ...).
  • Clocks. CLOCK_OWN runs at clock_rate. CLOCK_BIO runs at clock_rate times clock_rate_bio, so stasis slows biology on top of the entity's own clock. Both stats take the deepest source (MIN), so two stasis beds never stack to 1%.
  • Base stat declarations live in code/base/ (section 22).

Holds: runtime contributions

hold(E, STAT_X, value = null, source = null, lasts = null, priority = PRIORITY_DEFAULT, clock = CLOCK_OWN, reason = null, bound = FALSE) hold_until(E, STAT_X, value = null, source = null, until =, priority =, clock =, reason =, bound =) // an exact deadline hold_override(E, STAT_X, value, source =, priority = PRIORITY_ADMIN) // replaces the composed value release(E, STAT_X, source =) // source = SRC_ALL drops every source's hold on the stat release_all(E, source =) // everything one source holds on E held_by(E, STAT_X) // the sources: a fresh list, safe to hold while releasing held_by_source(E, STAT_X, source) // TRUE if that source holds it; allocates nothing hold_left(E, STAT_X, source) // time left on a timed hold, in deciseconds of the hold's clock
  • The source is required. It is a datum or a SRC_* flyweight (below), and null or text is an error, so no hold is unreleasable. It is the fourth argument, and it is written named (source = x) wherever the value is skipped, as it is on a boolean stat.
  • Boolean stats take no value. On an ALL stat a hold is a veto (forces FALSE). On an ANY stat it forces TRUE. Passing the forcing direction is accepted; the other direction (TRUE on ALL, FALSE on ANY) is a lint error because it can never change anything. hold(src, STAT_BOLTED, source = SRC_AI_CONTROL) bolts the door.
  • Forcing the other way. hold_override is for any stat except SET. While an override exists the stat's value is the highest-priority override's value (ties: most recent). The contributions keep being tracked, so releasing the override restores the composed value. This is how an admin forces an ALL stat TRUE, or sets a SUM to 0.
  • Re-applying from the same source follows the stat's reapply = rule (below). One source holds once per stat.
  • Reasons. A hold on a stat a gate reads takes reason =, a message type. When the gate drops every candidate, the strongest refusing hold's reason is shown (section 8).
  • Admin edits of a stat var are hold_override by the admin, visible in VV and removable. They're never a raw write the engine would overwrite.
reapply =Holding again from the same sourceUse for
REAPPLY_MAX (default)Keeps the stronger value for the stat's rule and the later expiry. Never shortensStatuses, EMP, most holds
REAPPLY_EXTENDTakes the new value and adds the new duration to the time remainingDoses that stack in time
REAPPLY_REPLACETakes the new value and the new expiry; may shortenA mode forced by whoever called last

hold_until is exact whatever the stat's rule says: it replaces the value and sets the expiry, and it may shorten. It is what status_set and status_adjust use, so a timer can be reset or cut.

Clock and units

CallUnit
lasts =, until =Deciseconds on the hold's clock = (write 4 SECONDS). until is an absolute time on that clock: clock_now(E, clock) + n
hold_left()Deciseconds of that same clock. On a CLOCK_BIO hold it is biological time, and world time left is that divided by the clock's rate
status_* amounts, status_remaining()The status stat's units =. LIFE_CYCLE is 6 seconds of the mob's CLOCK_BIO, so stasis pauses every status
  • One deadline per entity per clock. An entity keeps at most three expiry queues (world, own, bio), each with one next deadline.
  • A clock-rate stat can't be held on the clock it feeds. A hold on clock_rate or clock_rate_bio takes CLOCK_WORLD only. A stasis hold on CLOCK_BIO would stretch its own duration and never end at rate 0, so that is an error.

Sources

SOURCE_DEF(ai_control)                                        // SRC_AI_CONTROL: a flyweight source, no strings
hold(src, STAT_BOLTED, source = SRC_AI_CONTROL)
hold(M, STAT_SLOWDOWN, 0.5, source = src, lasts = 4 SECONDS)   // a numeric stat takes a value
SourceUseAn untimed hold ends
A datum (an activation, an item, a spell, a mob)An effect tied to that thingOn release, or when the datum is deleted
A SRC_* flyweight from SOURCE_DEFA role or system that has no instance: AI control, a wire, SRC_STATUS, SRC_VVOn release
The op source (the default for an op's holds() and grants() that land on another entity)This op's effect on this targetOn releases(), or when the target is deleted
The op's activation (the default for holds() and grants() that land on the op's own holder)What a capability's op does to its holderWhen the activation ends, timed or not, unless outlives = TRUE
A.actor (source = ON_ACTOR in an op part)Per-actor effects: this AI's boltsOn release, or when the actor is deleted
The occupying item (while_slotted(..., on = ON_HOLDER)) or the slot entry (on = ON_CONTENTS), section 10An effect while something is in the slotWhen its scope ends (X1): the item is removed, transferred, spilled or deleted
SRC_HELD_ITEMAn effect while an item is in that hand or slotWhen the item leaves that hand or slot, however it leaves; and separately when the item is deleted (the destroy transaction releases what it sourced)
  • Defaults. An op's holds() and grants() default their source by where they land. On another entity (the baton's stun, a pill's effect) the source is the op source, the pair of holder and op key, owned by the target's store: it names the holder without referencing it, so deleting the baton or the pill does not release the effect. On the op's own holder the source is the op's activation, so the effect ends with the capability. source = SRC_HELD_ITEM opts in to "the item owns it while it is in that hand or slot". An op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold is a build error, because the item deletes itself at commit and would release its own effect.
  • Timed holds outlive their source. A hold with lasts = or until = runs its course when its source is deleted: a stun from a projectile that deletes itself on hit still lasts its 4 seconds. Untimed holds die with a datum source. bound = TRUE opts a timed hold in to dying with its source. The two exceptions are activation-owned: a hold an activation's op places on its own holder is bound to the activation unless outlives = TRUE, and a slot source (the occupying item, or the slot entry) is always bound.
  • Who owns a stat's holds. The stat's declaring capability or type documents it. The doors() capability, for example, declares bolted and owns it, and an AI's bolts are held with source = A.actor so each AI releases only its own.
  • SRC_ALL is a wildcard accepted by release() and status_end() only (a full heal).
  • Releasing a source with many holds. A release recomputes the stats it touches inline, like any write. A source holding more than HOLD_RELEASE_BATCH holds (default 256, a night-shift or admin hold across a z-level) is a marked case of section 7: it releases in slices in phase G, coalesced per target, so a deletion never spikes a tick, and the remaining holds still apply until their slice runs. Phase G is not a drain point, so the recompute a slice marks runs at the next marked drain, the next tick's D. It is the one place a released hold stays visible for a few ticks.
  • Toggling. toggles_hold(STAT_X, value, source =) (no value on a boolean stat) is an op part (section 9) that releases the source's hold if it has one and places it otherwise. It replaces the held_by() read-modify-write.

Standings: standing() and standing_toward()

standing(E, toward = target, value = STANDING_HOSTILE, source = grudge, lasts = 5 MINUTES, priority = 60)
standing_toward(E, target)                       // a number; lower is more hostile; STANDING_NEUTRAL when no row applies
unstanding(E, target, source)
  • Hold semantics, on the hold rows. A standing is a row in the hold store of E (the H_KEY column carries the subject, H_STAT is the pseudo-stat HOLD_STANDING). It is source-attributed, bound to its source (released when the source is deleted, timed or not), expires after lasts, and one source holds one row per subject: stancing again replaces its value, priority and deadline. It uses the existing expiry timer and teardown, and the stat recompute never sees it.
  • Subjects. A datum, a faction key (text), STANDING_PLAYERS (any mob a player controls) or STANDING_ANY. The faction of a datum is standing_faction() (its faction var by default); whether a player controls it is standing_player() (the client of a mob). Player reputation content is not built; the form supports player subjects and faction-toward-player rows.
  • Composition. The rows that apply to a subject are the ones toward it, toward its faction, toward STANDING_PLAYERS when a player controls it, and toward STANDING_ANY, in that order of specificity. They compete in one pool: the highest priority wins; on equal priority the lowest value wins (the most hostile); on equal value the more specific row wins. Values: STANDING_HOSTILE -100, STANDING_WARY -50, STANDING_NEUTRAL 0, STANDING_FRIENDLY 50, STANDING_ALLY 100.
  • Cache and notice. Answers are cached per (E, subject) and read back while the faction and player state of the subject are unchanged. Any change to a stance row of E (placed, replaced, released, expired, source deleted) drops the cache of E and publishes /datum/notice/standing_changed on E. GLOB.forms_trace logs the drops.
  • Cost. An entity with a standing has a hold list, so its stats take the general recompute path rather than the no-holds fast path. Put standings on a holder that has no stats of its own: an AI keeps them on its /datum/ai_brain, never on the mob, so mobs keep the fast path.

Statuses

status_at_least(M, STATUS_X, units, intensity = 1, source = SRC_STATUS) // at least this long; never shortens or weakens status_set(M, STATUS_X, units, intensity = 1, source = SRC_STATUS) // exact: hold_until; 0 units ends it status_adjust(M, STATUS_X, delta, source = SRC_STATUS) // adds to or cuts that source's time; at or below 0 ends it status_end(M, STATUS_X, source = SRC_STATUS) // source = SRC_ALL ends every source's hold status_remaining(M, STATUS_X) // units until it lapses on its own has_status(M, STATUS_X) immune_to(STATUS_X, when =) // an entry: a contribution that zeroes the status
  • A status is a numeric stat. A stat declared with units = is a status, and its id is also written STATUS_<NAME>. The effective value is the intensity (the strongest source wins under MAX), and the time left is the hold's expiry. has_status is "the effective value is above 0".
  • The sugar is defined over holds. status_at_least is hold(M, STAT_X, intensity, source = source, lasts = units * LIFE_CYCLE, clock = CLOCK_BIO) for a LIFE_CYCLE status, under REAPPLY_MAX. status_set is hold_until. status_adjust changes that one source's remaining time, and starts a hold from a positive delta if the source has none. status_end is release. status_remaining is the longest time left across all sources, in units.
  • Sources. Calls without source = use SRC_STATUS, so the roughly 700 existing callers share one hold per status and stack by REAPPLY_MAX as they do today. A cause that needs its own timer (a baton, a spell) passes its own source, and status_adjust and status_end then touch only that source unless SRC_ALL is named.
  • Immunity. Each status stat has a companion boolean ANY stat STAT_<NAME>_IMMUNE, generated by the same STAT line. While it is TRUE the status reads 0 and status_* calls and holds on it are refused. immune_to(STATUS_X, when = cond) is the entry that contributes TRUE to it, and a runtime immunity is a hold on the companion stat from a source (a mutation, an item). A hold that is already running keeps its clock while the immunity lasts.

Grants: holds on set stats

grant(E, what, source =, lasts = null, bound = FALSE) // returns the activation, or null with the reason logged revoke(E, what, source =) // marks the activation dead at once granted(E, what)
You grantwhat (a typed value)Set it joinsRead it with
A capabilityA /datum/capability/x path, or a constructor call when it takes params (cover(), mirror_plating(reflect_chance = 45)): a type check, never text parsingThe instance's capability setgranted(E, /datum/capability/x)
A traitgranted_trait(TRAIT_X)The traits set stathas_trait(E, TRAIT_X)
A languagegranted_language(LANG_X)The languages set statgranted(E, granted_language(LANG_X))
An access idgranted_access(ACCESS_X)The access set statgranted(E, granted_access(ACCESS_X))
A client, OOC or admin verbgranted_verb(path)The verb set, applied by the verb storeThe verb store
Hide a verbhidden_verb(path)The hidden set. A hide beats a grantThe verb store
  • Typed dispatch. Each token routes to its own set stat, so a trait and a language with the same id can't collide. Grants take sources and expiry like any hold, and revoke is the release for a grant. The default sources "grant" and "join" are gone. join(system, E, source, role) and leave(...) for system membership (section 2) count sources the same way. observe() is a grant of a hook capability (section 10).
  • Capabilities. A granted capability is an activation (X1) whose scope is the grant. Its entries attach by the one attach path and detach by the one teardown path: revoke, expiry or the end of its source takes all of it back together. An instance's tables are interned as (type tables, sorted set of activation definitions), so a grant or revoke is a table-id swap plus the attach cost, and a click on a mob with six granted capabilities costs one index lookup. Hook and op candidates for an action are the target's type table plus every live activation on it (a revoked one is dead at once, above), with the same precedence (section 10).
  • Verbs. Gameplay abilities are ops with menu() (section 9), not granted verbs. The verb set holds client, OOC and admin commands only.
  • Traits. add_trait(E, T, source) is grant(E, granted_trait(T), source = source), remove_trait is the matching revoke and has_trait is granted. The three names stay as sugar. The separate trait store is what goes.
  • Effects on another entity. A lasting effect one entity puts on another is a granted capability, not a set of loose holds, so the whole effect is one activation with one teardown. A belly absorbing prey is the model: absorption moves the prey into the belly's second slot, which declares while_slotted(SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED, absorbed(), on = ON_CONTENTS), so the slot entry is the source of everything the absorbed capability does (16.6).

Conditional entries

when(condition, entries...) // an activation whose scope is the condition and whose source is the holder's type

The condition's reads are generated, so the framework applies and removes the entries itself, and no code calls a "changed" proc. Species, preferences, worn items and modes all use this: when(PROC_REF(spont_vore_on), spontaneous_vore()), when(EMAG_EMAGGED, emagged_menu()). What happens when the condition flips follows the consistency rule of section 7: a stat contribution inside it is gated by the condition, which is one of the stat's inputs, so the stat is correct before the setter that flipped the condition returns; hooks, observes and outputs inside it attach and detach at the drain, and an op inside it is checked when resolution reads it.

Wrapping only ops, when(cond, op(...), op(...)) means the same as putting when(cond) on each op: they exist only while the condition holds. Both compile to one applicability check evaluated when resolution reads it, so nothing is attached or detached at runtime (section 9).

Domain APIs over stats and grants

Domain APIUnderneath
status_*, has_status, immune_to (kept)Defined above, over holds on numeric status stats
add_trait, remove_trait, has_trait (kept)grant, revoke and granted over the traits set stat
apply_body_effect(type, lasts, origin), remove_body_effect, has_body_effect (kept)An effect that only changes factors, with stacks, is a hold on a SUM stat (value = stacks). An effect that needs ticks or ops is a granted capability: grant(M, body_effect_capability, source = origin, lasts = lasts), with every() ticks and per-application state as capability data
factor(BF_X), factors = alist(...), species_factors, worn_factorsNumeric STAT_* ids with the table's rules. Reads are plain var reads. The body keeps its flat cache as the fast path until its sources contribute through declarations
set_stasis(level, source), om_suspend (kept as set_stasis)hold on STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO (MIN); hold(M, STAT_SUSPENDED, source = src)
Trait var_changescontributes() inside the trait's capability. A var a trait changes must be a stat (a numeric one with SUM, PRODUCT, MAX or MIN, a flag var as MASK_OR), so releasing restores the composed value instead of clobbering another source. A plain var is a build error naming it
operable(), incapacitated(), update_canmove()Replaced by plain reads: E.operable and !M.can_act. update_canmove() is gone, since can_move recomputes inline when its inputs change
