Overrides, in one place

OverrideForm
A subtype changes a capability's paramsconfigure(KEY, param = value), or configure(KEY, variant = /datum/capability/x/variant) to swap in a subtype keeping the params
A subtype changes an opextend(key, parts...): requirements accumulate, single values replace. extend(key, drop = "id") relaxes a named requirement, and extend(key, replaces(parts...)) swaps the effect list
A subtype removes something inheritedwithout(key). Every entry takes an optional key = so it can be addressed
A subtype changes a statIts base: set the var. Add a contribution: contributes. Drop an inherited one: without("key"). Its rule: a build error
A map changes an instanceA map var edit on a capability param becomes per-activation capability data, and an edit of a stat var becomes an instance contribution (the generated varmap)
Runtime: something forces a valuehold on any stat (on a boolean stat the hold is the forcing direction), hold(..., priority =) on a TOP stat, or hold_override to replace the composed value
Runtime: something adds behaviourgrant(E, capability, source =, lasts =)
An admin edits a varvars_write, which becomes hold_override by the admin for a stat

Cost and limits

  • Per instance. A boolean stat keeps one bit per static contribution, and only the contribution whose reads changed is re-evaluated. A type can have at most 24 static contributions to one stat (DM numbers are exact to 24 bits), and more is a build error: group them with all_of or any_of. A mask stat has at most 24 bits. The hold list exists only while something is held.
  • Per change. A flip recomputes the stats that read it on the written entity and one single-valued edge away, inline and in rank order, which is O(dependent stats), and only the contribution whose reads changed is re-evaluated. Hook attachment, hooks and outputs are only marked for the drain, and so are the stats reached through a collection or membership edge, an accessor or two or more edges (section 7).
  • At init each stat takes its base and its static contributions silently: no notices and no hooks run until map load completes, then one settling drain runs the transition conditions and hooks that have subscribers (section 7).
  • Reads of a stat in generated reads are like reads of any other var.

Replaces:

  • The contribution store's raw API: om_apply, om_hold, om_release, om_has, om_value_of, om_apply_until (now hold_until).
  • Grants: om_grant, om_grant_for, om_revoke, om_has_grant, the GRANT_* kinds, text-parsing grant_kind(), the default sources "grant" and "join", the relation ledger's grant counts, and add_trait/has_trait as a separate store.
  • The old stat forms: stat(nameof(v), RULE, ...) entries, from =, req_stat, and the BF_* ids.
  • Timed state: timed_set, timed EXPIRY_* state, emp_disable's expiry var and the EMPED bit.
  • Machine on/off state: stat bits as gameplay state, powered() as a gate, cap_powered(), is_lit(), update_canmove() and per-type on/active/enabled/disabled overloads.
  • Other parallel state: /datum/status_effect, the push and alpha source stores, hand-rolled re-enable timers, and the EFFECT_CAN_MOVE composite (now a declared stat).
  • Per-mechanism lifetime rules and teardown code: the relation ledger's grant counts, hand-written release on unequip, unbuckle and species change, and every "remove what I added" proc. They are one attach path and one teardown path now (X1).

6References and lifecycle

Relations

Every object-typed var is a relation, a flyweight, or local scratch. Relations are declared with flat constructors, one per meaning:

ref_one(nameof(v), type, on_other_deleted = CLEAR | DELETE_ME, on_unlink =, by = nameof(id_var)) ref_many(nameof(v), type, ...same...) owns_one(nameof(v), type, starts =, on_destroy = DELETE | SPILL | PRIVATE_COPY, copy_on_write = FALSE) owns_many(nameof(v), type, ...same...) links(/obj/machinery/power/apc::hacker, /mob/living/silicon/ai::hacked_apcs) // paired, declared once for both types links(..., hot = TRUE) // engine hot-path pair (lighting): no index, no publish
DeclarationMeaningWhen the holder is destroyedWhen the other end is deleted
ref_one, ref_manyA reference. The holder doesn't own the valueThe view is clearedon_other_deleted = CLEAR (default) drops it. DELETE_ME deletes the holder too. on_unlink = PROC_REF(x) runs x(datum/act/A), with A.target the other end, whenever a link goes: unlinked, replaced, or the other end died. It does not run for a holder being destroyed
owns_one, owns_manyThe holder owns the value or values. starts = is the starting occupant (below)on_destroy = DELETE (default) deletes it. SPILL drops an owned atom to the holder's location. PRIVATE_COPY deletes only a private copy and leaves a shared prototype aloneAn owned value has one owner. Deleting it from elsewhere clears the var
links(A::v, B::w)Both ends name each other. The pair is declared once, and the cardinality comes from the two vars (a list var is a "many" end). One write links both ends, and linking a new partner to a single end unlinks the old one on both sidesBoth sides are clearedThe other end clears
links(..., hot = TRUE)An engine hot-path pair (lighting corners and sources): direct lists on both sides, not indexed, nothing published, symmetric teardown declared once. A read through it is not reactive, and a lint allows it only under the engine and domain directoriesBoth sides are clearedThe other end clears
  • Keyed relations. by = nameof(id_var) makes a keyed reference. The holder and the target type both have a tracked var of that name holding an id, and the relation points at the live target whose var equals the holder's. It re-links when either side materializes, when the id is set, and when the target is deleted. A type without the var is a build error. This is the one keyed form: rel_key and keyed = are gone.
  • Owned or contained. Something that sits inside the holder in space (a cell, an occupant, belly contents) is a slot(). owns_* is for datums with no location (a reagent holder, wires, a prefs record). OWN_SPILL and OWN_CONTAINED are gone. on_destroy = SPILL on an owns_* var is for an owned atom that needs no capacity, filter or exposure.
  • Copy on write. copy_on_write = TRUE goes with on_destroy = PRIVATE_COPY. The var holds a shared prototype until a writer asks for a private copy with rel_private. With FALSE the value is only ever replaced whole by rel_set.
  • Reads: plain var reads. Reads through a relation (power_area.lights_nightshift) are generated as hops, and re-subscribe when the relation changes.
  • Edge kinds. The settle rule of section 7 counts an edge in the direction a write travels, to the entities that read through it. It is single-valued when at most one entity can read the written one through that relation: a holder reading its one slot occupant, an occupant reading its container when the slot has capacity 1 (a stasis bed's one buckle slot), the two ends of a one-to-one link. The kind follows the capacity: a slot of capacity 1 is single-valued in both directions, and a slot that holds several things (a locker, a belly) is a collection edge from its container to its occupants, since a write on the container reaches them all. It is a collection edge whenever many can read: every machine names one power_area, so a write on the area reaches many machines; ref_many, a membership and a system's members are collections too. The build reads the kind off the declarations, and no author states it.
  • Flyweights: a var typed as a registry or flyweight type needs no declaration.
  • Internal relation kinds are the framework's own, never written by content: grants, listeners, members, timers and contained items.

Writing a relation: one verb set

Every write is type-checked (a value that is neither null nor an istype of the declared type is refused and reported) and publishes both ends. The verbs dispatch on the declared kind, so the caller never remembers how a var was declared.

WriteAcceptsWhat it doesOld forms it replaces
rel_set(E, nameof(v), x)ref_one, owns_one, a single link end, and a var typed as a registry or flyweight typeStores x (or null). The old value is cleared or disposed of by the declared policy. For a link, both sides are written. A registry or flyweight value is shared, never ownedrel_set, own_set, rel_link, rel_unlink (single end), proto_set, proto_replace, shared_set
rel_add(E, nameof(v), x, key =)ref_many, owns_many, a many link end, LIST_STATEAdds one member. key = puts it under a key in an associative owns_many, and replacing a key disposes of the old valuerel_add, own_add, own_put
rel_remove(E, nameof(v), x)ref_many, owns_many, a many link end, LIST_STATEDrops one member. An owned member is disposed of by on_destroy; use rel_take to keep itrel_remove, own_remove
rel_take(E, nameof(v), member = null, key = null)owns_one, owns_manyDetaches a value without disposing of it and returns it unowned. On a many, member = picks one member and key = one keyed member; with neither it detaches every member and returns them as a fresh list. The caller adopts or deletes what it getsown_take, own_take_member, own_take_all
rel_clear(E, nameof(v))Every kindEmpties the var. Owned values are disposed of by on_destroy, links are unlinked on both sidesrel_clear, own_clear, rel_unlink (all)
rel_move(old_holder, nameof(a), new_holder, nameof(b), member = null, key = null)owns_*Re-owns a value in one write, so it is never destroyed or orphaned on the way. member = or key = picks a member of a many; a many destination adds it (under the same key, for a keyed one)own_transfer, own_move
rel_private(E, nameof(v))owns_one(copy_on_write = TRUE)Returns the holder's private copy, making it on the first callproto_private, proto_copy
rel_is_private(E, nameof(v)), owner_of(x), is_registered(x)ReadsWhether the holder has a private copy; the one entity that owns x; whether x is a shared registry instance, which is copied before anyone writes to itproto_is_private, owner_of, is_registered

An accessor that isn't in this table is internal to the framework, and a lint bans it outside code/engine/. own_key, own_locate, entity_clone (the serializers' copy) and is_flyweight are internal. shared_set is rel_set on a registry-typed var. own_values is a plain read of the var (copy the list first if members may delete while you iterate). on_owned_release is on_unlink. keyed_set_id is the id var's own set_<var>(), since by = relinks when it changes.

Naming an entity in data

ref_token(E) // a generation-checked token for an entity resolve_token(token) // the entity, or null if it was deleted or its ref recycled
  • The rule. An entity never travels in data as a ref or a string. A var that holds an entity is a relation. A number, text or list that must name an entity, because a player can copy it or it is saved (an integrated-circuit pin, a task record, a saved reference), carries a token. A token is a plain value, safe to copy, compare and store within a round, and it never keeps the entity alive.
  • Resolving is the only way back: resolve_token returns null for a deleted entity, and a recycled ref never matches an old token.
  • The same rule covers with = payloads and request fields: scalars, flyweights and tokens only (section 3). Contexts and pending ops hold their entities through kernel relations, never raw refs: weak while the op waits and strong from its reservation (section 9, "Do is a commit").

Containment and slots

slot(id, accepts =, fits =, capacity =, exposure = SLOT_EXPOSURE_EXTERNAL, at = SPACE_X, on_destroy = SPILL | DELETE | TRANSFER(resolver) | TO_LATENT | KEEP_WITH(slot)) move_into(holder, slot_id, item, actor = null) // puts item into the holder's slot, through /datum/act/insert slot_remove(holder, slot_id, actor = null) // takes the slot's item out, through /datum/act/remove slot_transfer(item, to_holder, slot_id, actor = null, authority = null) // moves a contained item into another slot: runs the remove, then the insert, with their hooks slot_empty · slot_item · slot_contents · slot_clear container_of(thing) · contents_of(holder, type) · locate_within(holder, type) · locate_on(turf, type) · is_inside(thing, container) · contents_count(holder) · FOR_CONTENTS(holder, x)
  • Slots are entries backed by the containment ledger. Storage, equipment, cell bays, occupants, belly interiors and mech hardpoints are all library capabilities built on slot(). at = SPACE_X puts the slot in a space of the holder, so the path through its doors (section 8, spaces and doors) decides who can touch what is inside; fits = is a requirement on what goes in (size_is(ITEMSIZE_NORMAL)), refused with a generated reason.
  • Equipping and inserting are actions (/datum/act/insert, /datum/act/equip), so a constraint such as "takes only cells" or "fits these body types" is a requirement: extend(/datum/act/insert, needs(...)), or accepts = on the slot, which is a type list or a requirement for what a list can't say.
  • Capacity is a resource (X2, section 9). A slot's capacity = is the adapter of RES_SLOT_CAPACITY: an insert reserves the capacity the item needs before the move, the move commits it, and a refused or replaced insert releases it. A storage that holds weight classes and a belly that holds a number of prey are the same declaration.
  • A slot entry is an activation scope. while_slotted(SLOT_X, entries..., on = ON_CONTENTS | ON_HOLDER) (section 10) is an activation (X1, section 5) scoped to the item's time in the slot: it ends when the item leaves, however it leaves, by the one teardown path.
  • A transfer is a remove and an insert. slot_transfer() always runs the remove and insert actions and their hooks, so a belly's consent requirement or a cell bay's accepts applies to it like any other move. A forced transfer, one that skips requirements, is possible only with an authority datum (slot_transfer(..., authority = A)), the same AUTH_ADMIN authority an admin op uses (section 8). There is no other bypass.
  • No raw reads. Raw contents and loc reads are banned; use the spatial read API. container_of(thing) is what a thing is inside: the holder of its slot, or its turf.

Starting contents

starts = T | nameof(type_var) | list(T...) | pick_one(list) | PROC_REF(x) | when(cond, T) starts_args = list(...) // constructor arguments for what starts creates

starts is a parameter of owns_one, owns_many and slot, and of the capabilities built on them. It replaces every Initialize that exists only to create a child.

  • Forms. T is a type. nameof(type_var) names a var that holds the type, so a mapper can edit it: the APC's cell_type, which is the one way to change its starting cell. list(T...) is for a many or a slot with capacity. pick_one(list) is a random pick, weighted by the values of an associative list: pick_one(list(/obj/item/a = 3, /obj/item/b = 1)). PROC_REF(x) is for computed contents: x(datum/act/A) returns a type or a new instance. when(cond, T) starts it only if the condition holds at init.
  • Creation. Contents are created in nullspace and moved in through the relation write, so a created cell is never ejected by the move. starts_args are passed to the constructor.
  • Before the parent's init. Work that must exist before the parent type's init code reads it (a part made in nullspace, an inherited icon cleared) is the capability hook on_holder_preinit(A).

Lifecycle

MomentForm
Per-instance setupDeclared, not written: rolls() for randomness, param()/make() for constructor arguments, initial_contents() and starts = for contents, starts_as() for a starting state, derives(), registry(), radio_listen(), adjacency(), per_type(), knows() (Lifecycle forms); capability setup from on_holder_init(A). An Initialize(mapload, ...) override that remains sets per-instance state no form says and carries ALLOW(init/CODE)
Placed in the world / removedon_materialize() / on_dematerialize()
After init, timedafter_init(delay, parts...) (an after() armed when the instance initializes, section 3)
Map-load-only atomsmap_resolver(PROC_REF(x)). Resolved atoms are never initialized
Random spawnsDECLARE_LOOT tables, loot_spawn(), loot_search() (kept; already declarative)
DeletingA verb that says why: spent(thing, by), consumed(thing, by), destroyed(thing, by, cause), dissolved(thing, by), consume(item, actor), replace_with(new_type, args...), expire(N), slot_clear(slot), ledger_empty(), or a scope that ends it (lives_while()). qdel() is the engine's (Lifecycle forms). Never del(). replace_with creates a new_type where the holder is, passes args to its Initialize, then deletes the holder; it works across kinds, so an obj can become a turf (a girder raising a wall, 16.10)
Consequences of deletionon_destroy(force) calling ..(); lifecycle_prerelease() for teardown that must read declared vars; lifecycle_keep(force) to refuse. The destroy transaction clears relations, deletes owned values, cancels timers and holds, closes UIs and tears down every activation the entity held or sourced (X1)
Short-lived records/datum/pooled with take(type) / release(); fields reset to their initial values
Per-type constant dataTYPE_TABLE, shared caches (DECLARE_SHARED_CACHE, CACHED_*), variants (kept; caching tools, not state)

Order for one instance

StepWhat runs
1The map's var edits apply, and each type's Initialize runs its code before ..()
2The root of the Initialize chain: each stat takes its base and its static contributions, unevaluated and unpublished
3on_holder_preinit(A) of each capability, in entry order
4starts = contents are created and moved in
5on_holder_init(A) of each capability, with A.mapload set while the map loads
6..() returns, and each type's code after ..() runs. It sees every capability initialized
7When map load completes, one settling drain evaluates what has subscribers. Hooks and notices are silent until then (section 7)
8on_materialize() when placed in the world, on_dematerialize() when removed
9On deletion: on_holder_destroy(A) of each capability, lifecycle_prerelease(), on_destroy(force), then the destroy transaction

Replaces:

  • Ownership declarations: ownership(), OWN/OWN_SPILL/OWN_CONTAINED, PROTO, REL/REL_LIST/REL_PAIR/REL_PAIR_LIST/REL_KEYED, owns()/shares()/proto().
  • Typed relations: rel_one(...) and rel_many(...) with their ten parameters, RELK_* and back = (now link), rel_key and keyed = (now by =), watch = (reads are generated), /datum/om/relation/*, om_link.
  • The write families own_* and proto_*: they are the rel_* verbs above.
  • Starting contents: DECLARE_DEFAULT_CHILD, DECLARE_GAS (now gas_store()), DECLARE_BIND, starts_from and starts_at (now starts =).
  • Registries: DECLARE_REGISTRY and REGISTRY_MEMBERSHIP (now membership()).
  • Slots: /datum/slot_def, SLOT_DROP_HOLDER, the third requirement language for slots (hold_spec, HOLD_ONLY, REQ_* predicates).
  • Pools: POOL_DECLARE/POOL_RESET. Entity handles in data: om_handle and om_resolve (now ref_token and resolve_token).
  • Behaviours attached to a type with om_attach (now entries) or at runtime (now grant).

Lifecycle forms

Nine forms take what an Initialize() override, a qdel(src) or a read of usr did by hand. Each is an entry of a CAPABILITIES block or an engine verb (code/engine/lifeforms/; one test file per form, dq_lifeform_*_tests.dm). Where an entry follows a var (a registry key, a radio frequency, a derives() input, a scope), the var publishes its writes: it is TRACKED or written through a setter that calls tracked_changed().

Names. contains() of the approved design is initial_contents(): a CAPABILITIES block compiles as a proc of its type, where contains is the atom's own proc. Two generator names of the approved design clash with BYOND and are renamed: range is BYOND's own proc and between is a clamp macro here, so the number generator is range_of(lo, hi, step). chance(p) is the existing op part, read as a generator inside rolls(). A roller's draws are R.number(), R.choose(), R.chance(), R.weighted(), R.unit() (BYOND's rand, pick, prob cannot be proc names).

1. rolls(): per-instance randomness

rolls(target, generator, when = null, from = null) // generators pick_one(list) · pick_weighted(list(a = 3, b = 1)) · range_of(lo, hi, step = 1) · PIXEL_JITTER(n) · chance(p) · PROC_REF(x) // x(datum/roller/R) rolls_fix_seed(seed) // tests: the same seed and position roll the same
  • When. At the root of the Initialize() chain (a plain datum: New()), before the type's code after ..() reads the value. ROLL_PIXEL with PIXEL_JITTER(n) sets both pixel offsets.
  • Seeded. Each instance draws from its own stream: the round seed with its map position (turf and index among the same type there) for a map-loaded instance, its creator's stream for an instance created while another initializes (initial_contents(), starts, make(..., by =)), otherwise the round seed and a creation serial. A draw is md5 of the stream and a counter: native, stable across builds.
  • Suppressed when the target already differs from its compiled default: a map edit, a param() or a make() argument wins.
  • Gated and ordered. when = is a condition read after the rolls before it; from = list(nameof(a)) makes the engine roll a first, and a cycle is a declaration error.

2. param() and make(): named, typed constructor arguments

param(nameof(var), schema = null, default = null, required = FALSE, pos = null) // schema: int(), num(), schema_text(), schema_ref(T), enum(), or a type path built_from(nameof(var)) // the parts a make(..., parts =) hands over make(type, at = null, by = null, parts = null, name = value...) // generated: its named params are every name a param() declares make_args(name = value...) // the same, as a starts_args value
  • A param is set before init: make() passes a record that /atom/New() (a datum's New()) applies before anything of the type runs. A given value is checked against its schema; a refused one is reported and falls back. default = (a constant or PROC_REF) fills one nothing gave; required = TRUE reports an instance made without one.
  • pos = N maps the N-th constructor argument after the location to the param, so a caller still writing new /T(loc, a) keeps working while the codemod moves the callers it can see to make().
  • Checked. DM checks each make() name against the union; the lifeforms lint checks it against the made type (make_unknown_param, make_missing_required, make_parts_without_built_from).

3. registry() and radio_listen(): membership that follows the instance

registry(REGISTRY_X, key = nameof(var), by = REG_GLOBAL | REG_Z | REG_AREA, when = null) registry_get(REGISTRY_X, key, z = null, area = null) · registry_all(REGISTRY_X, key = null, z = null, area = null) radio_listen(freq = nameof(var), filter = RADIO_X | PROC_REF(x))
  • A generalised membership(): in the registry from init to destruction, filed under its key and, with by =, its z-level and area. A write of the key re-files it; a move re-files it under its new place; its destruction removes it. The plain member list (REGISTRY_MEMBERS(id)) holds it too.
  • radio_listen() is the area air device's listener (code/domains/atmos/area_air_device.dm) as a form: it listens on the frequency its var holds from init, retunes when the var is written and stops when the instance ends.

4. adjacency(): a tracked neighbour relation

adjacency(KIND, dirs = ADJ_CARDINAL | ADJ_DIAGONALS | ADJ_VERTICAL, connects = PROC_REF(x), into = nameof(var), when = cond, changed = PROC_REF(y)) adjacency_seen(holder, KIND) // list(member = junction bit)
  • Members of one KIND see each other. The engine places a member when it initializes on a turf, re-places it when it moves, takes it out while when = is false and when it ends. Every change asks the index which members' neighbour sets changed and recomputes exactly those: connects is x(other, junction_bit), into receives the junction mask (faces as BYOND writes them, corners at ADJ_JUNCTION_NE..SW) as a tracked write, changed runs after the mask changed.
  • The spatial index and its change events are Rust (verdigris/core/src/adjacency.rs, binds vg_adjacency_* in verdigris/ffi/src/adjacency.rs), keyed by kind and cell beside the grid; DM keeps one handle per member.
  • It replaces update_neighbours() in Initialize() and on_destroy(): placing, moving, anchoring and a neighbour's creation or destruction all reach the members concerned.

5. per_type(): a lazily built read-only table

per_type(nameof(var), PROC_REF(build))

The first instance of the type calls build(); every instance's var names that one table before its init code runs. A subtype re-declaring it gets its own. The table is read-only: the lifeforms lint rejects a write to the var outside its build proc (per_type_write).

6. OWNER, initial_contents(), owns_many(count =) and knows()

initial_contents(type, slot = null, count = 1, when = null, args = list(OWNER, ...)) owns_many(nameof(var), type, count = N, starts = T) starts_args = list(OWNER) · make_args(owner = OWNER) // OWNER: the holder (in make(): by =) knows(LANGUAGE_X, when = null)

initial_contents() replaces an Initialize() that only did new /T(src): the contents are made with the capabilities' init, in nullspace, then moved into the slot or the holder, rolling from the holder's stream. knows() gives a mob a language at init.

7. starts_as() and derives()

starts_as("cap.op_key" | STATE_KEY) derives(nameof(target), PROC_REF(compute), from = list(nameof(a), nameof(b)))
  • starts_as() runs an op's effects at creation, after the capabilities initialized: no actor, no requirements, no costs, no messages (GLOB.starts_as_running is set while it runs). A state key runs the op that sets it, or sets the key when no op does.
  • derives() keeps the target equal to compute(): at init, then whenever an input in from is written. The target is published, so another derives() or an on_change() can read it.

8. Declared lifetimes

owns_one / owns_many(on_destroy =) // on a plain datum too: run from New() lives_while(nameof(scope_var) | condition, watches = list(nameof(v))) on_ending(PROC_REF(x)) // x(cause, by) spent(thing, by) · consumed(thing, by) · destroyed(thing, by, cause) · dissolved(thing, by) expire(after) · replace_with(path, ...) · consume(item, actor) · slot_clear(slot) // kept
  • Owned. A plain datum's owns_one/owns_many and their starts = run from New(); its owned values go with it.
  • Scoped. lives_while(nameof(host)) ends the holder when the datum the var names ends (cause END_OWNER); lives_while(nameof(flag)) or a condition ends it when that turns false (END_SCOPE). Windows, requests, conditions and slots use it instead of a host's on_destroy() deleting them.
  • Caused endings. Every ending publishes /datum/notice/ended with cause and by (when anything listens) and runs the thing's on_ending() hooks, first in the destroy transaction. An ending no verb named is END_ENGINE.
  • qdel() is the engine's. Content ends things through the verbs; the escape_hatches lint counts the content qdel( sites and the ALLOW(lifecycle) keeps against ceilings that only fall, and a kind at 0 is banned.

9. Input with an actor

click_on(PROC_REF(x) | "op.key") · drag_onto(PROC_REF(x) | "op.key", onto = /T) · hover(PROC_REF(x)) // x(datum/act/input/A): A.actor, A.over, A.entered tooltip(PROC_REF(x), theme = null) // x(mob/user) answers list(title, content) with_actor(actor, CALLBACK(...)) · with_actor(actor, target | GLOBAL_PROC, PROC_REF(x), args...) // the second where CALLBACK is not allowed
  • BYOND hands DM the acting mob only in the client's native hooks, as usr. For a type that declares one of these entries, analyze gen declare writes the native override (Click(), MouseDrop(), MouseEntered()/MouseExited()) into the generated engine code: it reads usr once and hands the engine the actor. An op key runs through perform_op() with that actor.
  • with_actor() is the engine's one writer of usr: admin and callback code that calls BYOND procs that still read it runs under it. usr outside the engine and verb bodies is counted by escape_hatches and banned at 0.

7Change tracking and dependencies

Writes are immediate. A write recomputes at once, inline, the stats on the written entity and one single-valued edge away that depend on it, and marks everything else that reads it. The rest of the follow-up work, attaching and detaching hooks and ops, running hooks, and refreshing looks, UIs, the HUD and Rust pushes, runs at fixed points. Every dependent is a proc or a declaration whose reads come from its body.

What is consistent when

ClassWhatWhen it is settled
ImmediateStats on the written entity, and on the entity at the end of a single-valued relation edge (a holder; a slot occupant; the container of a slot of capacity 1; a one-to-one link), including contributions gated by a condition (an entry-level when(cond, contributes(...)), a while_slotted stat entry); permissions (the actor gate, and the inputs of the reach gate); containment, relation writes and capability keys; and the vars BYOND reads directly (density, opacity, luminosity) on the written entity, alwaysBefore the setter, hold, grant or relation write returns. A gating condition's reads are inputs of the stat, so the stat recomputes inline when the condition flips. One edge, not two
Marked, then recomputed under budgetEvery other stat the write reaches: through a collection or membership edge (an area's machines, a system's members, a many-relation), through a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, or two or more edges from the write; and a hold released in slices (HOLD_RELEASE_BATCH)The write marks them; the next marked drain (the start of phase D, P or R) recomputes them in rank order, each evaluation charged to the stat's lane, until that lane's budget for the tick is spent. What does not finish resumes at the next marked drain, which may be later in the same tick (D, then P, then R) or the next tick's, with the key chain logged. A marked stat that changes marks its own readers, and the same drain loop recomputes them
Deferred to the drainAttaching and detaching hooks, observes and outputs granted under a condition; on_change hooks; outputs; cleanup. An op under when() is not attached at all: its applicability is read when resolution reads itThe end of the current trigger, or the start of phases D, P, S and R
Exceptions to "ends with its source"A timed hold (holds(..., lasts =)) on another entity outlives its source by design: it is a hold, not a contribution. A hold an activation's op places on its own holder, and a slot-sourced hold, are bound to their source even when timed, unless outlives = TRUEA timed hold ends at its time, whatever happens to its source, unless it is bound (section 5)

How the build decides. One rule, from the relation declarations and the reads graph, with no author declaration (section 6, "Edge kinds"). A stat whose read path from the written key stays on the written entity, or crosses one single-valued edge, is inline. A stat whose path crosses a collection or membership edge, a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, or two or more edges is marked. An area's power channel reaching its machines is a membership edge, so it is marked. The two classes share one rank order.

The guarantee. When a write returns, every stat on the written entity, and on the entity at the end of a single-valued relation edge, holds its settled value, and so do the actor gate, the reach gate's inputs and containment. Vars that BYOND reads directly (density, opacity, luminosity and the like) are always in this class on the written entity: they are stats recomputed inline and can never be pulled on read. Marked stats settle at the next marked drain, in rank order, and any remainder resumes at the following one, so a marked effect is late and never lost. Hook attachment, hooks and outputs have not run yet; they run at the drain, in rank order. Spike E0's eighth proof (section 19) measures marked fan-out, and may promote specific membership edges to inline if that is cheap.

The execution model

StepWhat happens
WriteA setter, stat change, hold, grant, relation write or capability key changes state at once. Through the generated reverse index it recomputes the stats that read the key inline (next row) and marks the farther stats (collection edges, accessors, two or more edges), the hooks and attachments, and the transition conditions that read it for the drain, so the cost is O(readers). A key nobody reads costs one lookup.
RecomputeStats on the written entity and one single-valued edge away recompute eagerly and inline: when a contribution, a hold, a gating condition or a read a stat depends on changes, the stat recomputes at once, in build-time rank order, before the writer's next line (bounded by the cycle check). DM has no property getters, so this is how a stat var stays a plain var read that is settled when the setter returns. A stat reached through a collection or membership edge, an accessor or two or more edges is marked and recomputed at the next marked drain instead. Conditions used for matching are evaluated when read: when() on an op, look_layer(when =), menus and requirements, at resolution or output time.
DrainAt fixed points the kernel drains what is dirty, in rank order. The points are the end of the current trigger and the start of phases D, P, S and R (not U, which has none). What runs at every one: conditions that gate hooks, observes and outputs (an entry-level when() around them; the hooks and ops of a while_slotted entry or a granted capability, whose stat contributions apply at once), every(when =) starting or stopping work, a waiting op's requirements, and the edges of on_change. A flip applies its effect at once (attach, detach, start, stop, wake, cancel). Then the on_change hooks run, once per drain for the keys that changed. Outputs are only queued. The marked stats of the row above recompute only in the drains at the start of D, P and R, in rank order, each evaluation charged to the stat's lane: LANE_SIMULATION for an entity's stat, the system's own lane for a stat on the system itself (section 2). A marked drain stops when a lane's budget for the tick is spent, and that lane's rest resumes at the next marked drain. The budget bounds this evaluation only: hook attachment, on_change hooks and queueing always complete at their drain point.
Phase SRuns push_to_rust() for the entities whose pushed values changed, so the native step N that follows sees every write made in K. Writes made in D or P reach the next frame's S. Its guarantee covers the effects settled inline (the written entity and one single-valued edge away); a marked effect is pushed by whichever frame's S follows the drain that settles it. Never shed.
Phase RRuns presentation outputs only, once per entity per frame: draw, ui_data and outputs(). Outputs can't write state, so nothing in phase R can dirty anything.
RankWhatEvaluated
0Tracked vars, holds, relations, capability keysWritten by code, never evaluated
1Stats, including FORMULAInline, as soon as anything they read changes (a marked stat at the next marked drain)
2ConditionsOn read (matching), inline with the stat they gate, or at a drain (transitions of hooks, observes and outputs)
3HooksAt a drain
4OutputsPhase S for push_to_rust(), phase R for the rest
  • Ranks come from the build. The generator gives each stat, condition and hook a rank one above the highest rank of anything it reads, from the reads graph. A cycle in the stat and condition graph is a build error that names the loop.
  • Hooks may write. A write inside a drain marks more readers dirty and never starts a nested drain: the running drain loops until nothing subscribed is dirty. It stops after DRAIN_MAX_PASSES (8). Beyond that the engine logs the key chain and spills the rest to the next drain point. A runaway loop is a logged error, never a hang.
  • Inline recompute is measured, not assumed. It pays at write time what a getter would have paid at read time. E3's phase 1 spike measures that cost on a fan-out case, one APC channel flip over 50 machines (apc_flip_50), inline and marked for the drain, against the old path; spike E0's eighth proof measures a station-wide cascade under budget, and if inline is cheap enough for a membership edge such as an area's machines, the build may promote that edge to inline (section 19).
  • Derived values live in the stat layer. A FORMULA stat is rank 1 and settled at any read, so draw sees settled values; a marked stat that is still spreading redraws its readers when it settles. Phase R's own drain settles what the budget allowed before any output runs, and runs no marked work of its own after it.
  • Expiry and native changes are triggers of their own: a hold expiring in phase D, or a value arriving from Rust in phase N, marks readers dirty and a drain follows. Values the other way, DM to Rust, are pushed in phase S.
  • Initial evaluation is silent. At init each stat takes its base and its static contributions. Nothing publishes and no notice or hook runs until map load completes. Then one settling drain evaluates what has subscribers. Capabilities initialize inside Initialize, before the type's own code after ..() (section 6).
  • Notices are delivered synchronously when act_done() publishes, in publish order, to the listeners of the action's outcome (by default committed only, section 8). ACT_MAX_DEPTH (8) caps synchronous nesting. A nested notice past it is queued to the next drain point: it is delivered late and out of publish order, logged with its chain, and a test build fails on it. A nested action past it ends ACT_REFUSED with the reason "too deeply nested", logs the chain and fails test builds, because an action returns its result to its caller and a queued one could not.

An example. An APC's equipment channel turns off. set_equipment() writes the tracked var, and inside that call the APC's own stats, and the stats one single-valued relation edge away (its area's channel), recompute. The machines in the area read that channel through the area's membership, a collection edge, so each is marked, and the next marked drain recomputes each machine's operable in rank order under the budget of LANE_SIMULATION. A machine whose operable flipped marks what subscribes to it (an every(when = STAT_OPERABLE) work item, an on_change(STAT_OPERABLE, EXIT, ...) hook), and the same drain parks the work and runs the hook. draw is queued and runs once per machine in phase R.

The roles

RoleWhat it isFormsRe-runs
OutputComputes something from state and applies it. It must not write tracked state.draw(look), ui_data(A), push_to_rust(), outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =). examine(A) is on demand, not an outputOnce per entity per frame after any read changed: push_to_rust() in phase S, the rest in phase R
ConditionA pure boolean that gates something (section 9).Stat contributions, when() (entry-level or on one op), every(when =), look_layer(when =), requirement procs, sequence steps' when =Matching conditions when read; a condition that gates a stat inline with the stat; a condition that gates hooks, observes or outputs at the drain. A flip recomputes a stat, attaches or detaches entries, starts or stops work, wakes a step, cancels a wait, or redraws
Hook"This changed: do this once."on_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT, parts...) · on_change(key, ANY, parts...)At the drain, once for the keys that changed. ENTER and EXIT fire on a condition's flip; ANY fires when a key's value changed
KnobWhereDefault
at_most =, when =outputs(): at most one run per interval per entity, held changes arriving together; a condition that gates itNo limit; always on
lasts =, until =, clock =, priority =, bound =, reapply =Holds and stats (section 5)No expiry, CLOCK_OWN, PRIORITY_DEFAULT, FALSE, REAPPLY_MAX
DRAIN_MAX_PASSES, HOLD_RELEASE_BATCHEngine constants: passes per drain, and holds a dying source releases per slice8, 256

Publishing

  • Who publishes: setters, stats, holds, grants, relation writes, capability keys and the slot ledger. Native values arrive from Rust in phase N as CHANGED(entity, key).
  • Demand gating: a key is published only if something reads it, so an unread var costs one lookup.
  • Producer keys: a producer can publish a key that isn't a var with PUBLISH_CHANGE(E, key), and the key reaches the readers subscribed to E. An accessor proc that stands for that key declares READS_AS(proc, key). When the accessor reads through a relation, it names the hop: READS_AS(pad_occupied, OCCUPANTS_KEY, via = nameof(pad)). Readers then subscribe to the key on the entity the relation names, and the subscription follows the relation when it changes. The build checks that the accessor's own generated reads are covered by the key it declares.
// A global helper is followed through the argument it reads from:
/proc/cell_charge_percent(obj/item/cell/C)
	READS_FROM(C)
	return C ? 100 * C.charge / C.maxcharge : 0

// So this condition's reads are generated: the cell relation hop, and C.charge
/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/cell_low(datum/act/A)
	return cell_charge_percent(cell) < CELL_BAY_LOW_PERCENT

Generated reads

LayerRule
GenerationA query in the analysis engine (section 15), run incrementally before the DM compile. It writes code/engine/_generated/reads.dm, which is never edited by hand. Keys are interned to integers at build time. Evaluation is one generated typed accessor call, and nothing uses vars[], call() or text keys at runtime.
CoverageEvery output, condition, contribution and hook condition named anywhere in a declaration.
Resolving a readThe holder's own vars. Hops through declared relations (ref_*, owns_*, link except hot, slot contents). Procs of the same type, and of a hop's type (followed). Global helper procs, followed through READS_FROM(arg). Accessor procs with READS_AS (not followed). Native keys (native("temperature")). Stat vars. A system's state through its reactive accessors (night_shift_active()): a system is a singleton, so the hop is static, and the read follows the accessor to the tracked var. A direct read of a system's private var from another folder is a system_boundary error.
Context hopsA.actor, A.held and A.target are typed hops, so a requirement that reads the actor's hands or the held item's charge is generated. They are subscribed per pending op and dropped when it ends. A wait or an open prompt re-runs its requirements when one of these reads publishes.
ConstantsA var the analysis engine proves is never written after init (a type constant, a capability param) may be read in a condition. It is not reactive, because there is nothing to react to.
Not reactiveA.args, world.time and GLOB reads never subscribe: a condition that depends on time uses every() or after(), and one that depends on a global reads it through a system accessor. This is documented behaviour, not a gap.
Unknown readA build error with a fix hint: make it tracked, add a relation, add READS_FROM(arg) to the global proc, or add READS_AS. An unannotated global call inside a requirement, condition or output is the same error.
PurityThe generator rejects, in any followed body of an output, condition, requirement or formula, a call to a setter, hold, release, grant, revoke or rel_*, or an assignment to a tracked var. Test builds also run requirements, conditions and outputs under a no-write, no-publish, no-message guard, which catches what a static scan can't follow.
Standard outputsdraw, ui_data, push_to_rust and examine are base procs the engine calls by name. The build checks each override against the base signature, so a mistyped override is an error and not a silent no-op. Other outputs are declared with outputs().
Hand listsRejected wherever generation applies.
RuntimeIn test builds:
  • the drift audit re-runs outputs, and fails on a difference nobody published;
  • the missed-wake audit checks sleeping steps;
  • the condition audit re-evaluates stat contributions and pending ops' requirements.
In production the drift audit samples near players and corrects.

The graph contract

Generated reads, provider selection, op applicability, menus and waits are one dependency graph. This is what it promises.

QuestionContract
What a generated read isA static over-approximation: every read in a proc body subscribes, whichever branch it sits in. An APC set to "always" still subscribes to night; its recompute is cheap, returns the same value, and fires nothing because the stat did not change. Branch-sensitive subscription is not attempted.
Runtime topologyA cycle created by a runtime link, grant or activation is detected when the edge is added. The edge is refused, an error is logged with the chain, and test builds fail.
CollectionsA formula that reads a collection (a list, a set, a slot's contents, a system's members) subscribes to the collection's membership revision: it recomputes when a member is added or removed, and not otherwise.
Activation stateA capability data field that a condition reads is a declared tracked field of the activation (TRACKED on its /datum/cap_data/<cap> type, section 5). An undeclared field is not readable by a condition.
Provider selection, Match, menus, waitsTheir inputs (grants, providers, conditions, containment) are tracked reads of the same graph. A cached menu, a pending wait or a resolved provider is invalidated by that graph, never by a hand-written rule.
CachesThe reach cache key is (the actor, the actor's provider set generation, the target): it names the actor, since reach depends on where this actor is. An actor's provider set generation is bumped when an activation with provides attaches or detaches, or a held item changes. Door state (a cover, a panel) is part of reach, so a closing cover invalidates it.
System stateA system's state is private. Other code reads it through a generated reactive accessor declared with SYSTEM_ACCESSOR(system, name, nameof(var)) in code/contracts/accessors (night_shift_active()), and generated reads follow the accessor to the tracked var. The declaration holds names only, a system id and a var name, so contracts depend on nothing; the analysis engine resolves them and emits the accessor proc into code/engine/_generated, which can see the private var.
Cooldown ready stateA cooldown(t) that a button or window displays schedules an invalidation at its expiry (after() on the holder's clock). A cooldown nothing displays is a stamp and publishes nothing.

What a condition costs

CostRule
A writeAn inline recompute of the stats on the entity and one edge away that read the key, in which only the contribution whose reads changed is re-evaluated, plus a mark for each farther stat, transition condition and hook that reads it. One lookup when nobody reads it.
A readA stat read is a plain var read. A matching condition is evaluated when read, over stats that are already current.
A drainMarked evaluation runs in the marked drains (the start of D, P and R) under the kernel budget of its stat's lane. The evaluation count is capped by that budget and the rest resumes at the next marked drain, logged with the key chain. A marked flip, one night-shift change or one area's power channel across its machines, is marked by the write and spread over marked drains under the budget; the chain is logged if it spills, and no effect is lost. Hook attachment and on_change are not budgeted.
Map loadInitial evaluation is silent: stats take their values, nothing publishes and no hook runs. One settling drain follows. The type tables are built once per type from the parent's compiled form, not by re-running its ancestors.
MeasuredThe bench scenarios for stat and hold churn, the output drain and 1,000 idle machines (section 19), plus a boot-time gate for map load and type-table build.

Contexts

Handlers take (datum/act/A) (section 8 declares the five context types and their fields). This is when they run and what a context may do.

  • Where each runs. Effects, hook handlers and then() run inside a trigger, with a pooled context of the type that trigger names, living for that trigger. Conditions, outputs and a requirement asked outside an op (a menu, an early cancel) run outside one: the kernel passes a reused /datum/act/eval that allocates nothing, with holder, cap and activation, and no actor, held item or origin. A condition used inside an op's Match or Require is part of that op and gets its /datum/act/op. The build checks every field a handler reads against the context type its hook form names, so reading A.actor in a contributes() condition is a build error.
  • Never stored. A context may not be kept in a var or a list, or passed in with = or delayed(), and a lint rejects it. A.snapshot() returns a plain list of its fields for anything that must outlive the trigger.
  • Failure. The kernel wraps each handler and releases its context if it runtimes. A production watchdog frees any context held over 2 minutes with no pending op behind it, and logs the creator.
  • Nesting. A takeover that starts another action gets its own context. Nested actions and notices stop at depth 8: a nested notice past it is queued to the next drain point, and a nested action past it ends ACT_REFUSED (section 8).

Replaces:

  • Hand-written dependency lists: derived() with runs_while, drawn_from, ui_from, rust_push, derive(var, reads...) and derive_<v>(), rel(), rel_each and factor_dep; rel_one(watch =); req_proc(reads =); seq_step(reads =); on_change read lists; B4's special output table; the hand-written READS_AS for capability state.
  • Output registration by name: output(PROC_REF(x), ...) (now outputs()).
  • Watches and notifications: om_watch, observe used for state, on_channel, on_state_changed().
  • Manual refreshes: refresh_hud(), refresh_vision(), update_icon(), queue_icon_update(), SStgui.update_uis(), and the 368 manual *_changed() calls.
  • Hand-written invalidation: per-feature menu, reach and provider cache resets, and direct reads of a system's private vars (now reactive accessors).

8Actions and action paths

An action is anything that can be refused. There are two families:

  • Ops: chosen by an actor (a player, an AI controller, an admin).
  • World actions: started by code (a move, a fall, a hit).

Both run with a typed, pooled context, and both take the same hooks: requirements, takeovers, modifiers and notices (section 10). The words act, part, stage, key, condition, requirement, origin, reach, provider, authority, affordance and intent are defined once, in the vocabulary.

Contexts

/datum/act                    // pooled; every trigger's context. Five narrow subtypes, below
	var/datum/holder              // the entity whose entry is running; src in PROC_REF handlers
	var/datum/capability/cap      // set when the entry belongs to a capability
	var/datum/activation/activation   // the activation that is running this entry: its params, state and source (section 5). Null for a type-level capability until it has state; cap_data(A) creates it
	var/datum/source              // the activation's source (an item, a species, the type); also the default owner of what this trigger places (section 9)
	// the op context adds:
	var/datum/target              // what the action is done to. A datum, because a window or a prefs record can be one. With no target binding it is the holder
	var/atom/target_atom          // the same entity as an atom, set when the op declares target_type = /atom
	var/mob/actor                 // null for world actions without one
	var/obj/item/held
	var/origin                    // ORIGIN_*: where the input arrived
	var/datum/provider            // what performs the op: a hand, a held tool, a module
	var/authority                 // AUTH_*: the permission it is exercised under
	var/rolled                    // FALSE when the op's chance() failed (section 9); otherwise TRUE
	var/outcome                   // ACT_*: how the op ended; null while it waits. Set when it ends and read by the notice. A caller of perform_op() reads it from its /datum/op_result, never from the act
	var/datum/msg/reason          // the refusal reason when the op ended refused
	var/datum/request/request     // the latest request, whatever its outcome (section 13)
	var/datum/request/answer      // the answered request, after asks() (section 13)
	var/list/args                 // validated UI or topic args (they also arrive as typed proc params), or an after(with =) payload
	var/dt                        // every() and sequence work: elapsed time
	// plus the typed fields ACTION() generates: landing, landed_on, packet, ... and the snapshot fields A.held_name,
	// A.target_name and A.actor_name, taken when the op starts

Handlers still take one shape, x(datum/act/A), but the context is one of five narrow types, and each hook form names the one it gets. The build checks every field a handler reads against that type, so a field that is not set there is a build error, not a null at runtime.

ContextGiven toFields set
/datum/act/opAn op's parts (requirements, effects, feedback), and the hooks on an op or an actionholder, target, target_atom, actor, held, origin, provider, authority, rolled, source, activation, cap, args, request, answer, the snapshot fields
/datum/act/evalConditions and contributions, look_layer(when =), outputs, and a requirement asked outside an op (a menu, an early cancel)holder, cap, activation, and dt for outputs. No actor, held item, target or origin
/datum/act/noticeon_notice, on_op and on_change handlersholder, target, the notice's typed fields, outcome
/datum/act/timerafter(), delayed(), every() and sequence workholder, dt, args; for a system's per-member work target is the member; for a delayed() part, holder and the three snapshot names (A.held_name, A.target_name, A.actor_name) and nothing else (section 9)
/datum/act/requestrequest() callbacksholder (the owner), request, answer

A world action's own context (/datum/act/fall) is generated by ACTION(): it carries the common fields of the op context and its typed fields, and the hooks on that action (needs, instead, adjusts) take it. A condition has no actor unless it runs inside an op. A condition used in an op's Match or Require, including a req() used as a when(), is part of that op and gets its /datum/act/op; used anywhere else (a contribution, every(when =), look_layer(when =), an output) it gets /datum/act/eval, and reading A.actor there is a build error. An op whose target must be an atom declares target_type = /atom (or a subtype): Match checks it, and the handler reads the typed A.target_atom. A type filter on a binding (at_target(/mob/living)) sets target_type to it.

on =Lands onAlso used by
ON_TARGET (default)A.target: what the action is done toreq(T, of =) and source = in parts name a participant the same way
ON_HOLDERA.holder: the entity whose entry is running
ON_ACTORA.actor
ON_HELDA.held

while_slotted() adds ON_CONTENTS (section 10).

Holder and target. The holder is the entity whose entry is running; the target is what the action is done to. For a target's own op they are the same entity. For a held item's at_target op the holder is the item, so a baton's stun op has the baton as holder and the struck mob as target, and a holds() part with the default on = lands on the mob. For an actor's own op the holder is the actor. An op with no target binding has A.target = A.holder: an actor's own menu() op, a ui_act() on a machine's window. (An interface() open is not one of them: it is bound to hand() and remote(), so it has a target.) So an effect part with the default on = ON_TARGET lands on the holder in those ops, and an author who means the holder writes on = ON_HOLDER all the same.

Lifetime. A context lives for one trigger and is never stored in a var or passed in with = or delayed(). Section 7 ("Contexts") has the rules: when handlers run, A.snapshot(), the kernel releasing a context when a handler runtimes, the production watchdog, and the reused evaluation context that conditions and outputs get outside a trigger. Five points are specific to actions:

  • A takeover that starts another action (a reflected projectile is a new hit) gets its own context.
  • Notices are delivered synchronously when act_done() publishes, in publish order, so the publisher's next line sees the handlers' effects. The one exception is a notice nested past the depth cap, below.
  • Nested actions and notices are capped at depth 8 (ACT_MAX_DEPTH) for synchronous nesting. A nested notice past it is queued to the next drain point, delivered late and out of publish order, logged with its chain, and a test build fails on it: it is delayed, never dropped. A nested action past it ends ACT_REFUSED with the reason "too deeply nested", logs the chain and fails test builds, because ACT_TRY hands its result back to the caller and a queued action would have none to hand back.
  • While an op waits, which includes every asks(), it holds its holder, target, held item and actor through weak relations: deleting one before the op reserves its costs cancels it, with feedback (a pending request ends REQ_CANCELLED, and the op ACT_REFUSED with the reason). From the reservation until the act finishes the act holds all four strongly, so deleting one then does not cancel it, and consuming or replacing one (consumes(ON_HELD), replace_with) never cancels the op that did it. Later feedback reads the snapshot fields (A.held_name, A.target_name, A.actor_name), never the deleted object.
  • What a caller gets back. perform_op() and the test driver's input forms return a small plain /datum/op_result, never the pooled act: the act is released when the op ends (act_done or act_cancel, as always) and test builds poison a released pool entry, so a caller holding it would hold a dead reference. The engine fills the result in as the op ends. An op that finishes inside the call comes back already filled; one that still waits comes back with a null outcome, and the same pending op fills the same result in later. The pending op reaches it through a kernel relation, so a caller that drops it leaks nothing. AI behaviours use the result exactly as the test driver does. A player's click, press or menu pick allocates no result.
/datum/op_result // returned by perform_op() and the test driver's forms; not pooled, never released by the engine var/key // the op key chain that ran var/outcome // ACT_COMMITTED | ACT_REFUSED | ACT_REPLACED; null while the op still waits var/datum/msg/reason // the refusal reason when it ended refused var/rolled // FALSE when its chance() failed (the outcome is still ACT_COMMITTED) var/origin // ORIGIN_*: the input it ran under

World actions

A world action is declared once with ACTION(). It is a marker the generator consumes, not a plain macro: arguments are a name token, typed fields, and optionally FIXED and notice =.

ACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on)          // makes /datum/act/fall, act_fall(holder, landing, landed_on) and /datum/notice/fell
ACTION(move, turf/origin, turf/destination, direction)
ACTION(insert, atom/movable/item, slot_id)
ACTION(hit, datum/damage_packet/packet)                   // subtypes per entry: ACTION(hit/projectile), hit/emp, hit/blob, ...
ACTION(buckle, mob/living/buckled)
ACTION(unbuckle, mob/living/unbuckled)
ACTION(slash, mob/living/slasher, FIXED)                  // FIXED: nothing can refuse it; a claw swipe publishes it
ACTION(op, datum/op_def/op, provider, notice = /datum/notice/op_done)   // the action every op runs as
ActionTyped fieldsNotice

A target answers it with extend(/datum/act/hit/generic, instead(then(PROC_REF(smashed_by)))); a handler that returns HOOK_DECLINE lets the default generic attack land with the act's final damage.
