	asks(/datum/prompt/text/rename, step = "name"),
	asks(/datum/prompt/color/tint, step = "tint"),
	then(PROC_REF(apply_look)))
/obj/item/dogtag/proc/apply_look(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/prompt/text/rename/N = A.step("name")
	var/datum/prompt/color/tint/T = A.step("tint")
	name = N.value
	color = T.value

// Outside a trigger (a system asking ghosts, a vote): an owner for the callback, an explicit answerer, and a validity condition.
request(src, /datum/prompt/yes_no/ghost_role, then(PROC_REF(role_answered)), valid = PROC_REF(role_open), answerer = ghost, role = role)

A workflow is an ordered set of typed steps, foundation X3. An op's Wait stage is one, and so is a standalone request(). A step is a /datum/request, in these families:

Step familyAnswered byKinds
/datum/prompt/*A playertext, number, list, checklist, yes_no, color, colormatrix, mob, typepath, bitfield, file, icon, form (below)
/datum/io/*A backendsql, http, rustg_job, path (a budgeted path search, so bot AI asks for a path instead of spinning on a mutex)
/datum/client_query/*The client's own interfacewinget, measure_text, ...
TransportA tgui windowThe window a prompt, or an interface() modal, is shown in; closing it is a cancellation (section 2)
job()Background workA chunked job whose completion is the step's answer (section 2)
  • One outcome enum for every step. REQ_ANSWERED, REQ_CANCELLED, REQ_TIMED_OUT, REQ_TRANSPORT_FAILED and REQ_NO_RESULT, plus REQ_FAILED with A.request.error for a database or backend failure. A handler reads A.request.outcome; a null field never means several things. Inside an op only REQ_ANSWERED continues. Any other outcome ends the op refused with the outcome's message and nothing spent. A request() callback sees every outcome.
  • Captured fields: the resume policy. When an op first suspends at an asks() or confirms(), the fields it declares are snapshotted into the act: the fields named by a field arg of an asks() (book_id = nameof(selected_id)) and the fields listed by a captures(nameof(v), ...) op part. The part is legal on any op with a workflow step, and it is how a confirms("text") op, whose prompt names no field, captures what the player confirmed: op("print", ..., captures(nameof(selected_id)), confirms("Print it?"), then(...)). asks(..., resume = CAPTURE | LATEST | CANCEL_IF_CHANGED) says, per op or per field (resume = list(nameof(v) = LATEST)), what Do reads after resuming, and captures(..., resume =) says it for the fields it lists. CAPTURE, the default, reads the snapshot: the value the player confirmed is the value the op uses. LATEST reads the live value when the op resumes. CANCEL_IF_CHANGED compares the captured value (or the holder's revision, with no field named) with the live one and ends the op with "it changed while you were deciding". A holder's revision is a per-entity counter that every published key on it bumps; an op that compares it subscribes to it, which counts as a read of every key on the holder, so they are all published. A nameof(v) in an asks() field is read from the capture, and A.captured(nameof(v)) reads it in a handler. A lint requires that, for a field of either kind: a then() handler of an op with captured fields that reads one directly, as src.selected_id, is an error, because DM has no getter to redirect the read and it would see the live value.
  • Each step's answer is captured into the workflow context. A.answer is the latest, and A.step("name") returns the answer of the step called name (default: the kind's last path segment). Several questions in one window are a /datum/prompt/form; a chain is several steps.
  • Cancellation is one list. The answerer says no or closes the window (REQ_CANCELLED); the actor leaves range, drops the held item or another keep breaks (asks() takes the same keeps = as wait(), default WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT), or another requirement now refuses (the op's requirements are re-checked, and the window closes showing the reason); the holder, target, held item or actor is deleted before the reservation (they are held weakly while the op waits); the timeout passes (REQ_TIMED_OUT). Whichever it is, nothing was spent, because costs are reserved after the last answer.
  • One resumption rule. A workflow resumes in the op's context: its when and Require parts are re-checked, the captured fields are restored, and then the next step (an asks() or a timed wait(), in the order the op wrote them), or the parts after the last step, run. A prompt can't be answered into a state that no longer allows it.
  • Outside an op, valid =. request() takes valid = PROC_REF(x), a condition re-checked when the answer arrives, so a ghost-role request checks the role is still open; if it is not, the step ends REQ_CANCELLED.
  • Shared by every kind: typed fields set by named args and checked at build time, each with a schema (X5); weak handles (an entity-typed field reads null once the entity is gone); a timeout; cancellation when the owner dies; and metrics.
  • Two call forms: asks(/datum/request/x, field = v, ..., step =, resume =, keeps =) inside a trigger, whose answer is A.answer and whose request is A.request; request(owner, /datum/request/x, then(PROC_REF(done)), valid =, field = v, ...) outside one. then runs done(datum/act/A) on the owner (a /datum/act/request), with A.request set; prompts also take answerer =. await() stays inside the kernel and the request layer. SQL goes through /datum/io/sql/* and sql_write() only, an answer's rows are typed (row_type = /datum/io/sql/row/x), and db_query_now() stays the narrow exception (world/IsBanned and the boot schema check).
  • Costs outside an op. A request() that a player pays for takes costs = list("[RES_X]" = n) (and payer =, default the answerer): its require half is asked when it opens (a request that cannot be paid for is not shown, and the payer is told the resource's reason), the amounts are reserved when a confirming answer arrives (any answer; a yes_no only on yes) and committed after the handler, unless it returned OP_REFUSED or OP_FAILED, which releases them. An answer that can no longer be paid for ends the request REQ_CANCELLED with nothing spent. A malfunctioning AI's priced abilities are written this way (RES_CPU).
  • One open prompt per answerer per op. Asking again focuses the window that is open. An open asks() takes no slot: an actor may have several questions open (section 9).
  • notify(user, text, title =) is an info popup. It is an output: nothing to answer.
  • Prompt-kind hooks. A kind may override three procs, each x(datum/act/A) with src the prompt: prepare() fills its choices before the window opens, valid() checks what its schema cannot state (the schema has already checked type, range and sanitising; the answer is value), and on_cancel() runs when the answerer says no, closes the window or times out, or a requirement closes it. Checks on the world (still adjacent, still holding it) are requirements on the asking op, never valid().
  • UI args are schemas too. arg(name, schema) is the same validator a prompt kind uses: arg("n", int(0, 100)), arg("channel", enum(LIST)), arg("note", text(64)). arg(/datum/prompt/number/transfer_amount) uses a declared kind directly, and the handler's parameter is named for the kind's last path segment (transfer_amount).
Prompt kindAnswer (value)Schema
textA stringtext(max_len, sanitize =); required unless it has a default
numberA numbernum(min, max, step) or int(min, max)
listOne of the choicesenum(LIST)
checklistA list of choiceslist_of(enum(LIST)), with count limits
yes_noTRUE or FALSEbool. Absorbs confirm and a two-button alert
colorA colour stringA text that parses as a colour, so no try around rgb2num
colormatrixA colour matrixlist_of(num(min, max)) of twenty
mobA mob (a handle)ref(/mob), one of the candidates
typepathA type pathpath(type), descending from the root type
bitfieldAn int of flagsflags(LIST): only declared flags
fileAn uploaded fileAccepted types and size. A native client dialog, so the request layer runs it as a client query
iconAn icon fileSame: a native client dialog, never tgui
formTyped fields, one per questionEach field with its own kind and schema. Several questions in one window; a chain of steps keeps its answers by step name instead

A prompt kind states the schema of its value with SCHEMA(kind, value, ...), the same line a SQL row column uses, and never with a var of its own. Each kind carries its own timeout and default. savefile_close(S) is the named form for the del() of a savefile. Banned: raw input(), alert(), tgui_input_*, NewQuery() and Execute().

Messages, sounds, effects, chat

These stay as they are, and feedback parts use them:

  • Messages: MSG_DEF / MSG_DEF_SELF message types with %U%, %T% and %I% tokens, used by says(); act_message() for code outside a trigger.
  • Sounds and effects: play_sfx(atom, SFX_ID) with SOUND_SET rows, and the plays(SFX) feedback part; fx_sparks().
  • Chat: to_chat with the span_* helpers.

Replaces:

  • Appearance: APPEARANCE_TEMPLATE, DECLARE_APPEARANCE*, APPEARANCE_LEVEL/EMISSIVE/SLOT, appearance_overlays(), update_icon() overrides and calls; the look calls look.state, look.part, look.overlay, look.gauge, look.light, look.hide/look.show and look.set_layer (now variant, layer, glow, level, a stat for light); overlays_standing and mutable /icon caches.
  • UI: DECLARE_UI*, UI_DATA*, UI_ACT*, UI_SUBACT, DECLARE_UI_MODAL, DECLARE_UI_STATE, ui_prepare/ui_opening/... hook procs, tgui_data naming, the ui_window(...) entry (now interface(window, ...)), the polling update_uis.
  • Verbs and abilities: DECLARE_VERB*, GRANT_VERB*, verb(path, ...) entries (now verb_entry(), client and admin only), and /datum/ability with its HUD button (now a menu(button =, bind =) op).
  • Prompts and requests: om_ask, act_ask, topic_ask, verb_ask, client_ask, rerun_ask(_on), flow_ask, om_ask_sequence, om_flow_start, prompt_flow, ask(...) outside triggers, io_request, /datum/om/prompt (now /datum/prompt, one kind of /datum/request), confirm and alert (now yes_no, notify()), arg_* as a second validator (now arg(name, schema)), and the null result field that meant answered, cancelled, timed out and failed at once (now one outcome enum).
  • Topics: TOPIC_ACTION.

14Domains

Damage

Entry points (bullet_act, hitby, ex_act, emp_act, fire_act, blob_act, melee, shocks) build a pooled packet and run the /datum/act/hit/<entry> action. The pipeline then runs in order:

  1. Requirements: immunities and godmode, as stats.
  2. Takeovers: extend(/datum/act/hit/x, instead(parts..., order = ORDER_EARLY | ORDER_NORMAL | ORDER_LATE)): a shield that absorbs the hit whole, a mirror, the APC's blob rip. chance() inside means "takes over with this probability". A taken-over hit stops here and ends ACT_REPLACED: its notice goes only to listeners that asked for replaced hits.
  3. Adjustments: extend(/datum/act/hit/x, adjusts(packet.amount, scale = 0.7, when = PROC_REF(shield_up))) modifies the packet in flight (ablative shields, armour that works as a hook). It never re-enters the action.
  4. Mitigation: armour stats, materials, resistances.
  5. The sink: damage_sink(), which is integrity for objects and injure() for mobs.
  6. The notice: /datum/notice/hit/<entry>.
  7. An explosion's contents: a holder declares how hard a blast on it reaches what it carries, blast_contents() (the full blast) or blast_contents(shield = 1) (one severity step lighter; past light it reaches nothing); the explosion service reads it with explosion_contents_severity_of(holder, severity) and queues the contents in the same batch. A holder that declares nothing shields them. Replaces explosion_contents_severity() overrides.

Integrity's broken state is tracked; atom_break() and atom_fix() publish it. Library pieces: emp_disable(), reflects(), blocks(). Repair is a fixes() part, or repair_damage(). A worn piece of kit reaches the wearer through while_slotted, not through a hand-written hook on the mob:

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/clothing/suit/armor/reflective)
	while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN,                         // declared on the item: while it's worn, its wearer gets these
		blocks(10),
		reflects(PROJECTILES, 30),
		on = ON_HOLDER)

SLOT_ANY_WORN matches any worn equipment slot. on = ON_HOLDER grants the entries to the entity whose slot the item sits in, and the grant is released when the item leaves the slot, is deleted or its wearer is. A container type uses the other end, on = ON_CONTENTS, to give something to whatever it holds (stasis beds, cryo, bellies, borg docks).

Body and medical

The body's domain API stays: injure(), mend(), fully_heal(), vitality(), is_critical(), injury_load(), afflictions and their triggers, treatment tags, diagnosis and physiology. It plugs into this model in five places:

  • Factors are stats. Slowdown, accuracy, evasion, incoming injury, healing received, metabolism and the rest are STAT_* ids in the same namespace as every other stat; the old BF_* ids are gone and their combine rules are rows of the section 5 table, with one stat per armour kind and per injury category. Afflictions, reagents, species, traits and forms contribute to them with contributes(), and a worn item with while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, contributes(...), on = ON_HOLDER).
  • Timed statuses are numeric stats. STAT(/mob/living, stun, MAX, reapply = REAPPLY_MAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE) declares one, and status_at_least(), status_set(), status_adjust(), status_end(), status_remaining() and has_status() are the sugar over its holds (section 5). Immunity is immune_to(STATUS_X), a contribution that zeroes the status.
  • Body effects are capabilities granted with a duration. A species' own entries (immunities, abilities) are declared with SPECIES_CAPABILITIES(/datum/species/x, entries...) and granted by species_capabilities() while the mob's species relation names it (section 11): the species is a ref_one on the mob, changed with rel_set(M, nameof(species), S), so a species change is a relation write and the activation follows it.
  • Periodic medical work (contagion spread, symptom ticks) is every() or a Life step.
  • Traits that change vars declare contributes() instead of a var_changes table, so releasing the trait restores the true value. The one sanctioned reflection write is vars_write(E, name, value), used by VV, SDQL, the serializers, DNA and record copies and the map loader; it routes through the setter or a hold, and its own file is its only exemption.

Mob Life

  • The sequence: Life is /datum/sequence/life. Its steps are procs declared with seq_step(), ordered by runs_after =, and their conditions are typed facts on the frame (F.alive, F.placed). Subtypes override the proc, and traits and organs add steps through granted capabilities.
  • Outputs: HUD and vision are outputs() entries with generated reads, at_most and a when = nameof(client) gate. client is a tracked base var that Login and Logout write through its setter. Nothing calls a refresh.
  • Stats: movement and acting are stats (STAT_CAN_MOVE, STAT_CAN_ACT), and which origins an actor may act by is STAT_ACTS_VIA.
  • Observers: ghosts, AI eyes and blobs use every() on their own type.

Machines

  • Working: operable is a stat (STAT_OPERABLE). powered(), breakable(), construction(), emp_disable() and admin holds contribute to it.
  • Power draw: power_draw is a SUM stat: each load contributes its own draw (off, idle or active, from whatever is running), and the power system reads the total.
  • Work: machine work is every(interval, ..., when = STAT_OPERABLE), or a narrower condition.
  • Systems: machines join theirs through powered_by() or membership().
  • Components: the components() capability derives stats such as efficiency and capacity from the installed components.

Simulation and Rust

  • The frame: the native system runs vg_frame in phase N.
    • Its outbox becomes CHANGED publishes, notices (/datum/notice/native/<event>) and band crossings for on_cross.
    • DM reads Rust-owned values as native("key") (cached per frame), and those reads are generated like any other.
  • Pushes: DM-owned values Rust needs are tracked vars. push_to_rust() is an output that writes them with native_write(), and its reads are generated, so it runs once per frame after one of them changes. It runs in phase S, immediately before the native step, and S is never shed: a write made in K by an input reaches the same frame's native step, and nothing presentational can delay it. A value changed in D or P reaches the next frame's S.
  • Thresholds are watches: on_cross(native("pressure"), list(50, 100), parts..., urgent =).
  • Gas levels are a tracked var Rust turns: gas_level(into = nameof(overheated), reading = CH_GAS_TEMPERATURE, above = ARTIFACT_HEAT_BREAK, hysteresis = 50) declares the var that says the air at (or in) the holder is past a level, and the holder reacts with on_change(nameof(overheated), ENTER, parts...). Rust finds the edge on every change it sees, wherever the write came from (a heat exchanger or a pipe device writing a pipe region reaches it as a gas write does), so DM hears only crossings and reads nothing; a burst of crossings is one wake per watch per tick, the tracked setter drops a repeated value, and on_change runs once per drain (at_most = spaces it). A level is a state, so it is a tracked var, not a notice. Every change of a part of the gas (a gauge, a sleeping device, an exposure model that wants the numbers) is gas_watch() or gas_watch_many(). Design and rationale: framework_gaps.md F5.
  • Gas and heat: gas mixtures stay opaque handles (return_temperature(), vg_pump, ...). Heat moves only in Rust, conserved by construction: one primitive moves q between two reservoirs (a gas mixture, a pipe port's pipeline, a heat body, a turf solid, space), +q/−q in one operation, capped at each side's floor, and books it; every frame's change equals its booked external work (a debug assertion). DM declares flows; it never writes a temperature or an energy outside code/domains/ (the heat_raw_temperature_writes lint is a hard ban). See Gas and heat below.
  • Bindings: the generated vg_* procs and the ABI handshake stay.
  • Scheduling: DM timers and keys live only in the kernel. The Rust world scheduler keeps native watches and rate models.
  • Budgets: phase S is L0. Marked fan-out (collection edges, accessors, two or more edges) is spread over the marked drains of D, P and R under the budget of its stat's lane (section 7), and the input that feeds it is bounded per client (section 2).
  • Lighting is a host system with a hot links() (below).

Host systems

The machinery that wraps BYOND (the input inbox, the database, the tgui transport, garbage, the ticker, air, verdigris and lighting) is made of ordinary systems, defined in section 2. What the domains add:

  • Lighting stays on the DM side. Its corner and source graph is the one place the relation rules would cost too much, so it declares links(/datum/light_source::effect_str, /datum/lighting_corner::affecting, hot = TRUE): a symmetric pair with no index and no publish, torn down from both ends by that one line. A hot link is for engine graphs like this one, not for content.
  • The tgui transport owns window delivery, and the windows themselves are declared with interface() (see UI above).
  • World queries. world/Topic belongs to the input system, which routes the status, ping and server-tool queries to registered handlers: world_query(KEY, PROC_REF(x)) on a system, with x(datum/act/A) reading the parsed query from A.args and returning the reply.

World systems

Every former world service and subsystem is a system:

  • Machines and mobs: machine, mob, plant.
  • Infrastructure: power, chemistry, radiation, sound, POIs, supply, research, chat, statpanels.
  • Rounds and events: votes, events, expedition, flight, sun, night shift, explosions, turf cascade.

Each has needs, every() work, membership (with roles = list(...) declared, and join(system, E, source, role) naming one), and an api.dm. On-demand systems park while idle. Long one-shot work, such as the map loader, expedition site generation, the POI queue and zone cleanup, runs as a job(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =): step returns JOB_MORE or JOB_DONE, budget = is a percent of a tick, and the job dies with its owner.

Clients

A /client is not a datum, so everything the game attaches to one lives on its /datum/client_session, created at Login and reached as C.session. Skybox, the view-variables panel, preference panels, the admin holder, per-client lists and client-only images are owned relations of the session (owns_one, owns_many), and the session's on_destroy is the client's teardown. A registry keyed by ckey is members_of(key, role) keyed by that ckey. client_kick(C, reason) and world_shutdown() are the only callers of del() on a client or the world. A client is ready when its data has arrived, and Login delivers the client's verbs then, so no code waits ten ticks for them.

Movement and containment primitives

Moving a thing without the world noticing is a named primitive: abstract_move(AM, dest) (camera eyes, jaunt holders, area membership during map load) runs no Moved, Crossed or Uncrossed, and dview(center, range) is the pooled view list. The commit points that write loc live in code/engine/containment and nowhere else.

Mob AI

ai(behaviours) is a capability. It attaches the brain of code/modules/combat_ai, and that module stays: a /datum/ai_brain scores each /datum/ai_behavior flyweight with evaluate() (which returns DQAI_RESULT(score, target)) and runs the winner through start(), tick() and stop(). Those are the brain's calls, not the engine's, so they keep their signatures. What changes is how the brain runs and what behaviours do:

  • Scheduling: the brain's strategic loop (2 s) and tactical loop (0.25 s) are keyed every() entries of the capability on the mob's own clock (CLOCK_OWN), so suspension pauses them. They park by relevance and wake on the notices and changes a behaviour's eval_triggers name, which become on_notice and on_change hooks.
  • Acting: a behaviour acts through perform_op() (which returns a plain /datum/op_result) and perform_intent() (origin ORIGIN_AI, authority AUTH_AI), the same ops, requirements and messages a player uses, instead of calling attack_target() or shoot_target() directly. A behaviour that opts in to player use (get_player_verb_info()) becomes a menu() op under "Abilities", replacing the "Use Combat Move" dispatcher verb.
  • Pathing: dq_pathfind() becomes a /datum/io/path request, answered by the budgeted path system, so a bot step never sleeps.
  • State: aggro, targets and stances are stats and relations; cooldowns stay on the brain or the source item.

15Tooling and enforcement

One analysis engine

Every build-time check and generator is a query in one tool, the analysis engine at tools/analyze/, on one model of the tree. Today about 40 Python lints and check_grep.sh each re-read the ~5,800 files of the tree, one after another: a check_ratchets.sh run takes about 495 s and check_grep.sh about 146 s. The engine loads the tree once, in parallel, strips comments and strings once, and keeps a content-hash cache, so an incremental run rescans only the files that changed.

  • When. Phase 0b builds it, in Rust, and ports every existing lint to it, the legacy-form ratchets included, with the same baseline files and the same output. Phase 1 starts once the engine core (load, cache, parser backend, ALLOW, baselines, exemptions) and the slowest lints are ported; the remaining ports continue alongside phase 1. Each lint switches over only when a parity harness shows that the engine and the old script report identical findings on master. check_ratchets.sh and check_grep.sh then become thin wrappers over the engine, and are deleted. Phase 1's E5 adds the semantic queries below to the same engine; it does not start a new tool (section 19).
  • Backend. Text rules and parse-aware rules run on SpacemanDMM's dreammaker parser behind a backend trait. The new incremental DM compiler's front end and analysis API (parse, type and proc resolution, queries) replaces it behind that trait once it passes the phase-1 readiness gate. The engine only analyses: BYOND's dm.exe still produces the .dmb.
  • One of each. One diagnostics format, file:line: [rule] message -- hint, tagged with the tier that found it (below). One exemption mechanism, tools/ci/lint_scopes.toml and ALLOW(lint/CODE), parsed once, so the unused-ALLOW check is exact: the engine knows what fired. One incremental daemon, shared by agent worktrees.
  • Outside it. TGUI (Biome, tsc), Rust (clippy, cargo test), the icon repack and the Verdigris bindings keep their own toolchains. DreamChecker keeps running as a second opinion until the engine covers its rules.
QueryWhat it doesFrom
Today's lintsEvery ratchet and check_grep.sh rule, against today's baselines, at parity with the script it replacesPhase 0b (the slowest first; the rest alongside phase 1)
Generated readsWhat every condition, requirement and output reads, through A.actor, A.held, A.target and READS_FROM helpers, and the rank order that follows from it (section 7)E5
Semantic checksKey resolution for op, capability, stat, stage and source ids; handler signatures; field reads against the context type; context escape; ACT_TRY pairing; purity; module dependencies (module_deps); duplicate definitions; the compiled-table diff; the shim registryE5 and L
Generated identifiers and typesFrom ACTION(): /datum/act/x, act_x() and the notice type (FIXED actions are the only kind with no refuse path). From STAT, cap_keys, STAGE_DEF, SOURCE_DEF and CAPABILITY_DEF: their ids, accessors and constructors. All of it is written to code/engine/_generated/Phase 1
UI typesTypeScript from each ui_shape() and the window's ops, with each op's name and typed args (build.sh ui-types)Phase 2
The .dme generator, doc snippetsInclude order by layer, FILE_DIR and non-code includes, #ifdef UNIT_TESTS test includes (section 22); the DOC_SNIPPETS build (below)L; phase 2
Explain dumpsexplain_type offline, from the compiled tablesE5

Build errors, by tier

A string key that doesn't resolve to a declared identifier is a build error, and every build error says which tier found it:

TierFound byExamples
DM compileThe compiler and DreamChecker, on every buildA misspelt STAT_, STAGE_, SRC_ or COVER_ id; act_fall() called with the wrong argument types; a missing ..() where SHOULD_CALL_PARENT applies; a relative type path
GeneratorThe analysis engine's queries, on every buildUnknown reads; an unannotated global call inside a requirement, condition or output (add READS_FROM(arg)); a cycle in the stat and condition graph; a stat var written outside the engine; a tracked var written outside its setter; ACT_TRY without act_done or act_cancel; a stored context; UI arg() names that don't match the handler's parameters; a handler that reads a field its context type does not set
BootThe table builder, at server start (the unit-test boot runs it on every CI run)An op, capability or tag key in extend, without, configure, shares_effects, on_op or priority(above()) that resolves to nothing; an unknown capability param; one capability key with different params (naming both lines); a duplicate op key without extend; two ops on one target with the same input, intent and tier; a requirement with no resolvable reason; consumes() beside an item-sourced hold; a second CAPABILITIES for a type; a hold or contribution on a type that doesn't declare the stat; a shares_effects cycle; a stacking policy naming an unknown param; a from naming an undeclared stage; a keybind collision between ops whose when parts are not mutually exclusive; an effect declared before an asks(); a delayed() part that reads A.actor, A.target or A.held; a granted capability with state and no selector
TestTest buildsA requirement, condition or output that writes, publishes or messages (the purity guard); a UI push outside its ui_shape; perform_op with an unknown key; a runtime topology cycle (refused when its edge was added); a notice queued, or an action refused, past the depth cap; the drift, missed-wake and condition audits

Lints that stay

Retirement lints, one per legacy form, are shrink-only, reach zero, and switch on in the phase where their replacement lands (a column of the curated mapping, section 17). The lints below guard invariants that outlive the migration. Like every lint they are queries on the engine, and each has an end state, so "zero" means something:

LintProtectsTodayEnd state
initWhat an Initialize override may do5,204 baseline rows: initialize and unreasoned count the same ~2,470 overrides; 79 LateInitializeOne rule. An override stays only for per-instance state and carries a reason code. LateInitialize is after_init; world reads at init move to on_holder_init or membership(). The // INIT: form is deleted
base_varsNo new vars on /atom, /obj, /mob, ...1,509 rows, 495 of them list() inits that the instance-list lint also countsA raw var/ on a base type is the violation. STAT, TRACKED and LIST_STATE declarations pass, and live under code/base/. Capability state lives in cap_data and cap_keys, never on a base type. The list rows move to the instance-list lint alone
lifecycle_counts (with qdel_src)Deleting through the lifecycle verbs1,868 qdel( sites, and 711 qdel(src) rows counted a second timeOne lint. qdel(src) is banned (use consume(), expire(), replace_with() or slot_clear()). Any other qdel carries a reason code
instance_listA per-instance list costs memory480 ALLOWs, 361 of them generated d: codesLIST_STATE(T, var, alloc =, kind =, owns =, share =) passes, an undeclared list() var fails, system types and test directories are exempt by scope
ownership (relations)Object-typed vars are written through rel_*230 ALLOWsThe engine layer is exempt by directory, /client state moves onto client_session relations, a hot pair is links(..., hot = TRUE), and fixtures use TEST_RAW_SET
spatial, containment, latentReads and writes of contents and loc, and latent entries23 + 15 + 83 ALLOWs, about 70 of the latent ones false positivesCommit points live in code/engine/containment (exempt by directory); latent learns typed mob loops, has_latent() and an earlier materialize; what remains is a real exception
schedulerNo spawn, gameplay sleep, INVOKE_ASYNC, stoplag or set waitfor179 ALLOWs on a zero baselineZero outside the kernel, the request layer, vendored code and tests, all exempt by directory. The rest become request(), job(), after() and the tgui transport
silent_catch (catch)No bare try/catch65 ALLOWsBare catch lives only in the kernel and the two helpers: safe_call(PROC_REF(x), ...), a kernel utility that returns a /datum/result (ok, value, error), and try_parse_json(text). Tests use EXPECT_RUNTIME
cacheNo hand-rolled caches43 ALLOWsAppearance caches go to the look builder, constant tables to TYPE_TABLE; the lint keeps new ones out
system_boundaryA system's state is private1,041 sitesZero: other folders call api.dm
topic, cooldown, sys_world_time_*One input path; one cooldown and one stamp form15, 6 and 47 ALLOWsTopic only in the input system; COOLDOWN_KEYED_DECLARE, _START and _FINISHED for dynamic keys; STAMP/ELAPSED, with the kernel's own clock exempt by directory

New lints

LintRule
Sync SQLNo NewQuery, Execute or warn_execute outside the database system. db_query_now() is callable only from world/IsBanned and the database system's boot step (schema check, admin and rank tables), with no ALLOW (49 sync calls are unlinted today)
usrusr only inside verb procs; a ratchet from today's 1,693 mentions, and no ALLOW
Duplicate definitionsA var or proc defined for one type in two files fails. A file move changes which definition wins, so this and a before and after diff of the compiled tables gate every move wave
module_deps (layering)Dependencies follow a module graph, not one ladder (section 22): every cross-module reference resolves to a declared edge, the graph is acyclic, content depends on domains and never the reverse, and library, domains and content reach each other through contracts, not internals. An unmigrated directory is a legacy layer that keeps today's include order. No verb_entry under code/content
Shim lifetimeEvery compatibility shim is registered in one file with its owner phase, and the lint fails when a shim outlives its phase
ACT_TRY pairingEvery ACT_TRY has act_done or act_cancel on every path of the same proc
Context escapeA pooled datum/act may not be stored in a var or passed in with =; use A.snapshot()
Context fieldsA handler may read only the fields its context type sets (section 8): A.actor in a contribution's condition, or A.dt in a requirement, is an error
Declared checksA then() whose handler starts a world action (move_into, slot_remove, an act_* call) must name it in checks =, so the op's pre-checks cover it (section 9)
SchemasA default that fails its own schema, a var line beside a TRACKED declaration of the same var, or beside a capability param of the same name (a default has one home), a persist = TRUE var whose schema changed with no migration note, a hand-written clamp or sanitize beside a schema'd var (section 4)
Captured reads and effect returnsA then() handler of an op with captured fields (named by a captures() part or by a field arg of an asks()) that reads one directly instead of through A.captured(); a captures() on an op with no asks() or confirms(); a then() proc that returns anything but nothing or an OP_* constant (section 9)
Request purityA request's validators and re-checks are pure, and only the kernel and the request layer call await()
StructureNo /atom procs from capabilities, no manual notify calls, no raw contents scans, output purity, system boundaries, no subsystems

Exemption policy

About 1,590 ALLOW tags exist today, a third of them for gaps this design closes and many for places no lint should look at. One mechanism covers every exemption:

  • Directory exemptions live in one lint-scope file, tools/ci/lint_scopes.toml, not in 24 lint sources: unit tests and benchmarks, vendored code (code/host/vendor/), and the engine layers for the lints about engine internals. A site in an exempt directory needs no annotation, and the scope file is the only place to read what is exempt.
  • Reason codes. An annotation is ALLOW(lint/CODE): reason, with a closed code list per lint (BYOND_BOUNDARY, POOLED, TEST_FIXTURE, ...). The engine rejects an unknown code. Enforced from phase 0b.
  • Unused-ALLOW check. An annotation whose line no longer triggers its lint is an error, so annotations can't outlive their cause or sit on text the lint never reads. The engine makes it exact from phase 0b.
  • Reason quality. A reason shorter than about 20 characters, a bare word (kernel, MC, the accessor), "see above", "(allowlist)", "not edited here", a plan-phase label, or a generated d: code is rejected. The same reason repeated more than a set number of times needs a reason code instead.
  • Ceilings. Each lint has a ceiling on its annotation count, recorded in the repo and only allowed to fall. A new annotation needs an old one removed. Enforced from phase 0b.
  • One mechanism. This replaces ALLOW(base_vars), // INIT: and ALLOW(init), and the allowlists embedded in check_grep.sh and its side files (the input ladder, tool istype and verb allowlists, the deadline-polling and rust-core lists). Every exemption is countable.

Explain tooling

explain_click(actor, target, held, gesture)      // admin verb "Explain Interaction": every candidate, the filter that dropped it (entry file:line), the winner, its tier
perform_op(actor, target, key, held, trace = TRUE)
explain_type(/obj/machinery/power/apc)           // the merged table, each part tagged with its origin: type, capability or extend site (file:line)
assert_resolves(actor, target, held, GESTURE_CLICK, "cover.open")   // the test helper

The compiled table is the product of four precedence levels, configure, without, bundles and granted capabilities, so source locations would otherwise be lost: the builder records __FILE__:__LINE__ per entry and keeps an origin list per interned entry in every build, production included, at the cost of a file and a line per entry (the boot-memory gate measures it). The explain tools are admin verbs in production: "Explain Interaction" (explain_click), "Explain Type" (explain_type), "List Activations" (explain_activations, section 5) and "List Pending Ops". When nothing resolves, the engine shows the best near-miss's reason (rate-limited), so a click never silently does nothing. After a passes() commit, later refusals are silent and explain_click prints the chain.

Tests

  • Kernel and tiers: kernel_test_begin()/kernel_test_end() drive the kernel in tests (they replace om_test_begin/om_test_end). Focused runs while developing; the normal tier at integration; the exhaustive tier in CI. Conservation tests for ladders; the op hard-case acceptance tests.
  • Primitives: TEST_RAW_SET(E, var, value) is how a fixture writes a framework-owned var (only under code/modules/unit_tests and benchmarks); await_ticks(n) is how a harness waits real ticks, in place of stoplag(); assert_resolves(); EXPECT_RUNTIME(call) for a call that is meant to fail.
  • Behaviour snapshots: a resolver-agnostic harness records, per type, actor stance, held item and target state, through whichever resolver is active: the messages, the log lines, the tracked-state deltas, inventory transfers, resource commits and releases, activations, notices with their outcomes, pending work and delayed outcomes. It is built on the test driver's recorder (test_record), the one the E0 proofs read, so the harness and the proofs agree on what a transition is. It replaces the interaction-id snapshots, and each old-API test has a listed replacement.
  • The E0 proofs: ten small executable fixtures that pin the semantic contracts, each written with the test driver below on the engines' spike, required before any content conversion (section 19).
  • Doc examples are tests: the examples of section 16, and the snippets in sections 1 to 15 marked complete, are extracted and compiled by DOC_SNIPPETS (tools/ci/doc_snippets.py), required clean from phase 2. A snippet marked // excerpt shows part of a type that section 16 declares in full, and is not compiled on its own.
  • Benchmarks: scenarios for the new paths exist before phase 1, so master has numbers to compare with: 100 clicks per second on a 30-candidate stack, hover over 60 atoms, op-wait churn, stat-hold churn, a UI push storm with 20 open windows, output drain, and 1,000 idle machines.

Test driver

Test builds carry one driver, in code/tests/driver/, so a fixture can execute an input, answer a prompt, make time pass, record what happened and read what the contracts promise. assert_resolves() only asserts that a click resolves to an op key; everything else a fixture does goes through the forms below, the public engine verbs a fixture may call (grant, revoke, rel_set, ACT_TRY with act_done, move_into, action_options, screentip_for, built()), and test-only capabilities (section 19).

FormWhat it does
test_click(actor, target, held, gesture)Sends a click through the input inbox with origin ORIGIN_CLICK, resolves it as a player's click would, and returns a /datum/op_result, or null when nothing resolved
test_ui(actor, window, action, args)Presses a window button as a player would (origin ORIGIN_UI): the args cross the schema boundary, the op's ui_act() binding matches, and the op's result comes back
test_menu(actor, target, op_key)Picks the op named op_key from the context menu of target, as a player's pick would: origin ORIGIN_MENU, the actor's held item, the same gates as a click, and the op's result comes back. It is the only form that takes the menu path, which a click never does; the menu's contents are read with action_options(actor, target, held)
test_answer(actor, value | REQ_*)Answers the actor's open request with a value, or ends it with an outcome (REQ_CANCELLED, REQ_TIMED_OUT), and returns the result of the op that was waiting, the same /datum/op_result the earlier call gave back, now advanced
test_time(t)Makes time pass without waiting: it advances the kernel clock by t (write 2 SECONDS) and runs every phase, K to G, and every drain that falls due on the way, in order, so after() timers, every() work, wait() ends, hold expiries and request timeouts all fire. The world clock moves by t and each entity's own and bio clocks by t times their rates, so a stasis hold slows its occupant's timers as it would in play. It is the only form that makes time pass: test_phase never does, and real ticks are await_ticks(n)
test_drain(), test_phase(P)test_drain() runs one marked drain now: the marked recompute under each lane's budget, hook attach and detach, on_change hooks and queued outputs (section 7). test_phase(P) runs one kernel phase (K, S, N, U, D, P, R, G) once, at the current time, and moves no clock, so a timer that is not yet due stays pending. A fixture makes work due with test_time and places it exactly with these
perform_op(actor, target, key, held, origin =, authority =)Returns a /datum/op_result. origin defaults to ORIGIN_AI and names the binding the op runs through (section 8): a fixture passes ORIGIN_VERB for a menu() ability and ORIGIN_SYSTEM for any op. An op that is still in Wait comes back with a null outcome, and test_answer, test_time or await_ticks advance that same result until it carries its outcome
/datum/op_resultWhat the forms above return: key, outcome (an ACT_* value), reason, rolled and origin. It is plain and never pooled. The op's pooled act is released when the op ends and poisoned in test builds, so a fixture never holds one; the act's other fields reach a fixture through its own handlers or the record
test_record(entities...), test_recorded()The recorder. test_record(E...) starts it, and in test builds the engine verbs report to it (the calls compile out of production): op outcomes, notices with their outcome, log lines, slot transfers, resource reserve, commit and release, activation attach and detach, and the tracked-var deltas of the entities named in test_record. test_recorded() returns the record in order, as /datum/test_event rows (kind, entity, key, from, to), and clears it. The phase-2 snapshot harness is built on the same recorder
test_logs()Returns and clears the log lines written since the last call, each a record: key (the op key chain), outcome, origin, actor, target, text
test_rng(seed), test_rolls()Seeds the generator that chance() and prob() draw from, and returns the number of rolls made since the seed was set
test_notice_count(/datum/notice/x), test_spill_count()The notices of one type published (and, separately, queued past the depth cap) since the last reset, which is not the number delivered: a fixture that must know a handler ran counts in the handler. And the number of drain spills: marked passes that stopped at their budget
test_budget(lane, units)Sets a lane's per-tick budget (LANE_SIMULATION, LANE_WORLD, ...) for the marked drains that follow, in microseconds of tick time, the unit of section 2. A marked stat is charged to its own lane (section 7). In test builds each stat evaluation is charged a fixed nominal TEST_EVAL_COST (10 microseconds) instead of reading the clock, so a budget of 200 lets 20 evaluations through and a spill happens in the same place on every run

Runtime audits, codemods and docs

ToolRole
Runtime auditsOutput drift, missed wakes, condition drift, ownership leaks (find-references), activations whose source or holder is gone, reservations still open after their op ended, op contexts released, pools poisoned in tests. In production a watchdog frees any context held over two minutes with no pending op, and logs the creator
CodemodsOne idempotent script per mechanical form, in tools/dx/codemods/, on an AST rewriter over the analysis engine's parse tree (a regex can't safely rewrite nested INTERACT_*), with fixtures, idempotency tests and a key-collision report. The infrastructure lands in phase 2.5 (A4's 14, retargeted to the final forms)
DocsThis document becomes doc/rewrite/api.md plus chapters. AGENTS.md section 3 and section 9 are rewritten to these forms only in phase 2; until then an interim section says which forms compile today, and the archived OM docs are deleted afterwards

16Complex examples

Each example is complete for the parts it shows, and uses only forms from sections 1–15. From phase 2 they compile as DOC_SNIPPETS tests, together with the reference snippets marked complete (section 15); until then they are design sketches. The MSG_DEF lines behind each MSG(), and plain number defines such as BATON_HIT_COST, are left out.

A type has one CAPABILITIES composition root (a bundle may live in its own file, section 11; the examples keep each list whole, in one place). Where an example declares a type that a reference section also samples (the human in sections 3 and 8, the turret in section 5, the gripper and the carp in section 8, the stasis bed in section 10, the APC and the light in section 13), the example is that type's whole list, and the reference sample is marked as an excerpt. After each example, a note says what it shows and what it replaces.

16.1 APC: hatch, cell, construction, power, EMP, subversion, UI

/obj/machinery/power/apc
	var/cell_type = /obj/item/cell/apc        // the starting cell: a mapper or a subtype changes it here, and only here
	var/obj/item/cell/cell                    // the slot var cell_bay() works over
	var/area/area                             // link ends (below)
	var/obj/machinery/power/terminal/terminal
	var/mob/living/silicon/ai/hacker
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/power/apc, operating, chargemode, coverlocked, schema = bool, default = TRUE)   // operating is the breaker. TRACKED declares the vars and their defaults
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/power/apc, equipment, lighting, environ, schema = enum(POWERCHAN_MODES), default = POWERCHAN_ON_AUTO)
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/power/apc, nightshift_setting, schema = enum(NIGHTSHIFT_SETTINGS), default = NIGHTSHIFT_AUTO)

/obj/machinery/power/apc/high
	cell_type = /obj/item/cell/high           // no CAPABILITIES line: the type var is the one way to change the cell

STAGE_DEF(apc, frame)
STAGE_DEF(apc, board)
STAGE_DEF(apc, wired)
STAGE_DEF(apc, secured)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/power/apc)
	wall_machine(/obj/item/module/power_control, frame = apc_frame(), powered = FALSE)
	maintenance_hatch(                                        // space SPACE_HATCH behind the cover, the panel and its SPACE_PANEL, wires, lock and emag
		cover = cover(remove = force_pry(), replace = component_swap(/obj/item/frame/apc), broken = PROC_REF(stat_is_broken)),
		wires = wires(name = "APC", count = 4, status_lines = PROC_REF(wire_lights)),
		lock_wire = WIRE_IDSCAN,                              // lock(wire = WIRE_IDSCAN) brings the ID scan wire
		emag = list(wait(0.6 SECONDS), sets(LOCK_LOCKED, FALSE)),
		panel_needs_cover_closed = TRUE)                     // latch(SPACE_PANEL, COVER_OPEN, ...)
	power_wires(stat = STAT_SHORTED, count = 2, pulse_lasts = 2 MINUTES, shock = 50)   // the two main power wires
	ai_control(stat = STAT_AIDISABLED, pulse_lasts = 1 SECOND)                       // the AI control wire
	space(SPACE_CELL, inside = SPACE_HATCH, from_stage = STAGE_APC_SECURED, missing = MSG(apc/needs_electronics))
	cell_bay(nameof(cell), at = SPACE_CELL, starts = nameof(cell_type), fits = size_is(ITEMSIZE_NORMAL))
	powered_by(/datum/system/power, role = POWER_ROLE_SOURCE)
	emp_disable(between(24 SECONDS, 36 SECONDS))
	subversion_reset(tool(TOOL_MULTITOOL), at(SPACE_HATCH))
	links(/obj/machinery/power/apc::terminal, /obj/machinery/power/terminal::master)
	links(/obj/machinery/power/apc::hacker, /mob/living/silicon/ai::hacked_apcs)
	links(/obj/machinery/power/apc::area, /area::apc)
	contributes_to(nameof(area), STAT_LIGHTS_NIGHTSHIFT, PROC_REF(wants_night_lights))   // 16.11

	interface("APC", title = "Area Power Controller")   // the window, and its open op "interface.APC.open"
	ui_shape(operating, chargemode, coverlocked,                     // vars named here take their schemas
		channels = list_of(row(name = text(24), mode = enum(POWERCHAN_MODES))))
	op("breaker", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(operating)))   // no lock clause: lock() gates every TAG_UI op
	extend("breaker", binds(remote()), gesture(GESTURE_CTRL))   // a silicon's ctrl-click throws the same breaker
	extend(TAG_UI, needs(req_window_usable(remote = PROC_REF(remote_control_allowed), remote_because = MSG(apc/ai_disabled))))   // window access: the library's (section 11)
	on_change(nameof(operating), ANY, then(PROC_REF(supply_changed)))   // the area follows the breaker, whoever throws it
	op("chargemode", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(chargemode)))
	op("coverlock", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(coverlocked)))
	op("set_channel", ui_act(arg("channel", enum(APC_CHANNELS)), arg("mode", enum(POWERCHAN_MODES))),
		then(PROC_REF(set_channel_mode)))

	latch(SPACE_HATCH, PROC_REF(cover_latched), because = PROC_REF(cover_latch_reason))   // broken, or the cover lock over a charged cell
	extend("construction.undo:apc_secured", needs(req_empty(nameof(cell))))

	extend(/datum/act/hit/blob, instead(cuts_all_wires(), sets(PANEL_OPEN, TRUE), order = ORDER_EARLY))
	on_notice(/datum/notice/slashed, then(PROC_REF(lights_out)))

/proc/apc_frame()                         // a plain bundle. It is a global proc, so holder procs are named with TYPE_PROC_REF
	return construction(start(STAGE_APC_FRAME),
		stage(STAGE_APC_BOARD, item(/obj/item/module/power_control), put_in(SLOT_CONSTRUCTION),
			protrudes(because = MSG(apc/board_first))),      // a loose board keeps the cover from closing
		stage(STAGE_APC_WIRED, stack(/obj/item/stack/cable_coil, 10),
			needs(req(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, floor_intact), because = MSG(apc/floor_blocks))),
			then(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, make_terminal)),
			undone(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, cut_terminal)), protrudes(because = MSG(apc/board_first))),
		stage(STAGE_APC_SECURED, tool(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER)),
		dismantle(tool(TOOL_WELDER), becomes(/obj/item/frame/apc),
			ruined(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, frame_ruined), becomes(/obj/item/stack/material/steel))),
		at(SPACE_HATCH))
// floor_intact, make_terminal, cut_terminal and frame_ruined are ordinary (datum/act/A) procs, not shown.

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/cover_latched(datum/act/A)      // a condition: its reads (stat, coverlocked, the cell hop, its charge) are generated
	return !isnull(cover_latch_reason(A))

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/cover_latch_reason(datum/act/A)
	if(has_stat(BROKEN))
		return /datum/msg/apc/cover_broken
	if(coverlocked && cell_charge_percent(cell) > CELL_BAY_LOW_PERCENT)
		return /datum/msg/apc/cover_locked

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/set_channel_mode(datum/act/A, channel, mode)   // UI args arrive typed and validated
	switch(channel)
		if(APC_CHANNEL_EQUIPMENT)
			set_equipment(mode)
		if(APC_CHANNEL_LIGHTING)
			set_lighting(mode)
		if(APC_CHANNEL_ENVIRON)
			set_environ(mode)

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/lights_out(datum/act/A)         // a species' claw swipe is offered here only because this hook listens
	set_lighting(POWERCHAN_OFF)

/obj/machinery/power/apc/ui_data(datum/act/A)                // an output. Capability data is merged under caps.<key>, so there is no ..()
	return list("operating" = operating, "chargemode" = chargemode, "coverlocked" = coverlocked, "channels" = list(
		list("name" = "Equipment", "mode" = equipment),
		list("name" = "Lighting", "mode" = lighting),
		list("name" = "Environment", "mode" = environ)))

/obj/machinery/power/apc/push_to_rust()                      // an output: runs in phase S, before the native step, after a value it reads changed
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_OPERATING, operating && operable)
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_CHARGEMODE, chargemode)
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_CHANNELS, equipment, POWER_CHANNEL_EQUIPMENT)   // the one path for the channel modes
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_CHANNELS, lighting, POWER_CHANNEL_LIGHTING)
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_CHANNELS, environ, POWER_CHANNEL_ENVIRON)

What this shows. One list declares everything. The hatch takes the wire set and the emag effect as params, so the APC never declares wires or emag a second time. Every key is a declared identifier: STAGE_APC_*, COVER_OPEN, LOCK_LOCKED, and op keys namespaced by their capability, with the cell bay's selector (cell_bay.cell.take) and the window's (interface.APC.open). The lock gates the four UI ops without a clause on any of them. A blob hit is a takeover, not a damage row. The outputs need no ..(), and push_to_rust() runs in phase S, so a switch flipped in this tick's input is in the native step of the same frame. The three channel settings share one schema, so a bad UI value never reaches set_channel_mode.

What it replaces:

  • The MAINT bit, and the two hooks that kept it in step with the construction stage.
  • power_failed and its timed revert: emp_disable contributes to operable.
  • TRACKED_BRIDGED channel bridges and the manual changed(src) calls.
  • The UI_ACT table, and the lock check written into each handler.
  • The starting-cell Initialize, and configure(cell_bay(starts =)) on subtypes beside the var.
  • set_nightshift(), called by the night-shift service on every APC (16.11).
  • The three damage rows and emag_act().
  • A second breaker op for the silicon's ctrl-click, set_breaker() and toggle_breaker(), and the channel modes written to Rust by hand.
  • The APC's own window-access, lock-exemption and admin-ghost procs: req_window_usable() and req_silicon_or_admin() (code/library/access/window_access.dm) say them for every window.
  • Every hand-written hatch rule: when(COVER_OPEN) on the cell take, req_built() and a size proc on the cell insert, a cover-hold proc for the lock and the loose board, the replace conditions, and the lock and emag guards. The path (section 8, spaces and doors) says them: a staged SPACE_CELL, fits = size_is(...), a latch, a protruding stage, the cover's repair step and the library's lock and emag defaults.

16.1a Reach: a closed cover and the cell

// What the APC above brings for its cell. cell_bay(nameof(cell), at = SPACE_CELL) declares two ops over its slot, both placed at(SPACE_CELL):
//   "cell_bay.cell.take"     hand(), when(nameof(cell)), take_out(nameof(cell))     tier TAKE_OUT
//   "cell_bay.cell.insert"   item(/obj/item/cell), needs(size_is(...)), put_in(nameof(cell))   tier PART
// SPACE_CELL sits inside SPACE_HATCH, whose door is the cover (maintenance_hatch()), and exists from STAGE_APC_SECURED.
// The APC writes nothing about the cell's reach.
//
// 1. Empty hand, cover closed
//      cell_bay.cell.take matches, but its path (outside -> hatch -> cell) is blocked at the cover: it is set aside.
//      interface.APC.open (tier DEFAULT) answers the same input, so it wins and the window opens. Silent.
// 2. Empty hand, cover open, a cell inside
//      cell_bay.cell.take matches, its path is open, and it wins on tier TAKE_OUT. Wait: none. Do: take_out() runs the
//      world action /datum/act/remove on the slot, /datum/notice/removed is published, and whatever reads the cell
//      (the charge, the look) follows through generated reads.
// 3. A cell in hand, cover closed
//      cell_bay.cell.insert matches and is set aside at the cover. The window's hand() op ignores what is held, so it
//      does not answer a held cell: the insert runs and Require refuses with the door's reason, "Its cover is closed."
//      (On a frame not yet secured, SPACE_CELL does not exist: "You need to install the wiring and electronics first.")
// 4. Target the cell itself (contents view, drag), cover closed
//      The reach gate's walk up the containment ledger asks the APC for the path to the cell's space; it is blocked,
//      so every candidate is dropped and the engine shows the door's reason.
// 5. An AI or a borg acting through remote() (authority AUTH_REMOTE_ACCESS)
//      The cover's space applies to AUTH_PHYSICAL only, and neither cell op has a remote() binding, so neither is a
//      candidate, whatever the cover.
//
// No APC code does any of this.

Each case is the same path asked at a different point of the pipeline. Cases 1 and 3 are the silent-or-refuse rule: a candidate behind a closed door is set aside while another answers the same input (the window, for an empty hand) and refused with the door's reason when nothing does (a held cell). Case 2 is the open path. Case 4 is the reach gate: the walk up the containment ledger from the cell to the APC asks the same path, so the cell offers nothing. Case 5 is the authority mask of the space. The APC has no "is the cover open?" guard anywhere.

What "Explain Interaction" prints for case 1

Explain Interaction: Rae (human, empty hand) clicks the Engineering APC, GESTURE_CLICK
  intents      INTENT_USE                (bind profile "default", stance help)
  origin       ORIGIN_CLICK              authority AUTH_PHYSICAL
  actor gate   pass                      acts_via = ORIGIN_ALL
  providers    hand (AFF_MANIPULATE, AFF_ATTACK, AFF_HOLD, reach 1): the only candidate provider
  reach gate   pass                      the APC is on an adjacent wall, within reach 1
  index        (INTENT_USE, nothing held): 3 candidates. Every other op needs something in hand or a UI origin.

  #  candidate                       tier       result
  1  cell_bay.cell.take              TAKE_OUT   set aside: path outside -> into hatch (door cover: closed) -> into cell
                                                  "Its cover is closed."; it answers only when nothing else does
                                                  code/content/machinery/power/apc.dm:52   cell_bay(nameof(cell), at = SPACE_CELL, ...)
  2  construction.undo:apc_board     TAKE_OUT   dropped at Match: the stage is apc_secured, not apc_board
                                                  code/content/machinery/power/apc.dm:71   stage(STAGE_APC_BOARD, ...), undo derived
  3  interface.APC.open              DEFAULT    WINNER: answers the same input, so the set-aside take stays silent
                                                  code/content/machinery/power/apc.dm:42   interface("APC", title = ...)
     Require   window access: adjacent, conscious                                   pass
     Do        opens "APC" for Rae

  Not click candidates: breaker, chargemode, coverlock, set_channel (ui_act ops: ORIGIN_UI only)

Every row names the entry that put the candidate there, with its file and line, because the table builder records them. The same trace prints from perform_op(actor, apc, "cell_bay.cell.take", null, trace = TRUE), and a test pins the behaviour with assert_resolves(actor, apc, null, GESTURE_CLICK, "interface.APC.open").

16.2 Airlock: bolts, electrification, AI control, wires, access, emag

STAT(/obj/machinery/door, bolted, ANY)                   // the bolts library's stat (door_parts.dm): the motor, each AI, an admin hold it
SOURCE_DEF(door_bolts)                                   // the door's own motor: a wire, a button, a radio command, a map start
STAT(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, electrified, TOP, base = 0)   // timed holds for "30 seconds", untimed for "until released" (the suit cycler's rule)
STAT(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, main_power_out, ANY)  // a cut cable (SRC_POWER_WIRE), a tripped breaker for a minute (SRC_BREAKER)
STAT(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, backup_power_out, ANY) // the same, and the ten seconds of switchover (SRC_BACKUP_SWITCHOVER)
STAT(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, aiControlDisabled, ANY)   // ai_control(): the AI wire cut, or pulsed for a second; a round event
SOURCE_DEF(lockdown)                                     // a button, an event or a script electrifying the door

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/door/airlock)                // the base door brings machine_basics, doors(), the emag, the plasteel and repair ops
	panel()
	wires(name = "Airlock", count = PROC_REF(wire_count), randomize = PROC_REF(wires_randomized), status_lines = PROC_REF(wire_lights))
	ai_control(stat = STAT_AICONTROLDISABLED, pulse_lasts = 1 SECOND)   // each brings its wire: cut holds, a pulse holds a while
	shock_wire(stat = STAT_ELECTRIFIED)
	on_wire(WIRE_MAIN_POWER1, cut = PROC_REF(main_power_wire_cut), pulse = PROC_REF(main_power_wire_pulsed))   // the airlock's own
	bolts(drop = "drop_bolts", raise = "raise_bolts", starts = nameof(bolted_at_start), wire = WIRE_DOOR_BOLTS)
	weld_shut(offered = PROC_REF(weld_offered), starts = nameof(welded_at_start))
	door_emergency()
	ref_many(nameof(close_others), /obj/machinery/door/airlock, by = nameof(closeOtherId))   // paired doors, both ways
	on_change(nameof(main_power_out), ANY, then(PROC_REF(main_power_changed)))   // the backup's switchover

	// Touching a live door shocks you instead of doing what you meant. Window buttons and a silicon's link are not touches.
	extend(/datum/act/op, instead(when(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, req_on_origin(ORIGIN_CLICK)),
		then(PROC_REF(shock_toucher)), order = ORDER_EARLY))

	section(ctrl_click, "hammer on it (combat), hold it open (grab), ring the bell")
	op("hold_open", inputs(hand(), item(/obj/item)), gesture(GESTURE_CTRL), stance(I_GRAB), priority(above("hammer")),
		when(cond_not(req(/mob/living/silicon, of = ON_ACTOR))), needs(req_adjacent()), then(PROC_REF(hold_door_open)))

	section(controls, "the remote control window and a silicon's gestures over its link")
	interface("AiAirlock")
	extend("ui_open", inputs(remote()))                   // silicons only: remote() replaces the hand binding
	extend(TAG_UI, needs(req_silicon_or_admin(because = MSG(airlock/not_for_you)),
		req_window_usable(remote = PROC_REF(ai_control_allowed), remote_because = MSG(airlock/not_for_you))))   // the library's window access (section 11)
	op("bolt_toggle", ui_act("bolt-toggle"), needs(req_wire(WIRE_DOOR_BOLTS, because = MSG(airlock/bolt_wire_cut)),
		req(PROC_REF(actor_may_move_bolts), because = MSG(airlock/no_power_to_raise))),
		toggles_hold(STAT_BOLTED, source = ON_ACTOR), says(PROC_REF(bolts_message)), logs(LOG_GAME))
	op("shock_restore", ui_act("shock-restore"), releases(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, source = ON_ACTOR))
	op("shock_temp", ui_act("shock-temp"), needs(req(PROC_REF(power_systems_on), because = MSG(airlock/unpowered))),
		holds(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, lasts = 30 SECONDS, source = ON_ACTOR), then(PROC_REF(electrified_by)), logs(LOG_GAME))
	op("shock_perm", ui_act("shock-perm"), needs(req(PROC_REF(power_systems_on), because = MSG(airlock/unpowered))),
		holds(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, source = ON_ACTOR), then(PROC_REF(electrified_by)), logs(LOG_GAME))
	op("disrupt_main", ui_act("disrupt-main"), needs(req_is(STAT_MAIN_POWER_OUT, FALSE, because = MSG(airlock/main_offline))),
		then(PROC_REF(lose_main_power)))
	op("speed_toggle", ui_act("speed-toggle"), needs(req_wire(WIRE_SPEED, because = MSG(airlock/timing_wire_cut))), toggles(nameof(normalspeed)))
	extend("bolt_toggle", binds(remote()), gesture(GESTURE_CTRL))   // a silicon's ctrl-click is the same button
	extend("open_close", binds(remote()), gesture(GESTURE_SHIFT))

/obj/machinery/door/airlock/proc/power_systems_on(datum/act/A)
	return operable() && (!main_power_out || !backup_power_out)

/obj/machinery/door/airlock/proc/ai_control_allowed(datum/act/op/A)   // req_window_usable()'s remote rule: a condition
	return !aiControlDisabled && !all_power_lost()

/obj/machinery/door/airlock/proc/shock_toucher(datum/act/op/A)   // a shock that does not land lets the touch go on
	if((A.authority & AUTH_REMOTE_ACCESS) || !shock(A.actor, A.held ? 75 : 100))
		return HOOK_DECLINE
	return OP_OK

What this shows. Every switch of the door is a stat composed from sourced holds, so a timer is a hold that runs out and two hands never fight over one var. Two AIs bolt the door, and each one's hold has that AI as its source (source = ON_ACTOR), so one AI's "unbolt" releases only its own hold, and a deleted AI's bolts drop by themselves; the door's own motor (SRC_DOOR_BOLTS: a wire, a button) still lifts every hold when it raises them. An AI's "restore" leaves a wire pulse (the shock wire's own source) running its 30 seconds; the wires come with the capabilities that own their state (ai_control(), shock_wire(), bolts(wire =)), so the door lists only the wires that are its own. A pulsed and then cut AI wire are two holds, so the pulse running out cannot hand control back over the cut. toggles_hold does the toggle that used to need a held_by() read. The window buttons refuse through needs(), never with a message in the effect, and the library's window access (req_window_usable(), the APC's too) asks the door only for its remote rule. A silicon's gestures are the window's own ops with one more binding (binds(remote())), so there is no second op to keep in step. The shock is one takeover of every click op.

What it replaces: the 0/1/-1 ints electrified_until, main_power_lost_until, backup_power_lost_until and aiControlDisabled with their hand-keyed timers and the thirty-line electrify(); the shock written as an early effect on seventeen named ops; the window buttons' to_chat refusals; the click_ctrl() override looping over op keys; lock()/unlock() beside the bolts library; the init-time scan of every machine for closeOtherId; string sources ("ai", "wire"); intercept(); req_stat.

16.3 Portable turret: setting vs effective, EMP and emag, subtype params

TRACKED(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, enabled, schema = bool, default = TRUE)     // the player's switch: a setting
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, lethal, schema = bool, default = FALSE)
STAT(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, armed, ALL)          // effective: whether it fires now

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/porta_turret)
	powered()
	breakable()
	lock()   // gates every TAG_CONTROL and TAG_UI op below
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, nameof(enabled))
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, STAT_OPERABLE)   // power, damage, EMP and the emag's disable all arrive through operable
	emp_disable(between(6 SECONDS, 60 SECONDS))
	on_notice(/datum/notice/hit/emp, then(PROC_REF(scramble_settings)))
	emag(then(PROC_REF(go_haywire)), disables_for = 6 SECONDS)
	popup_cover(raised_while = STAT_ARMED)
	every(PROC_REF(scan_interval), then(PROC_REF(scan)), when = STAT_ARMED, key = "scan")
	interface("PortableTurret", title = "Turret Control")
	ui_shape(enabled, lethal)
	op("enabled", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(enabled)))
	op("lethal", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(lethal)))
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/porta_turret/alien)
	configure(emp_disable(lasts = between(1 MINUTE, 2 MINUTES), resist = 75))
	configure("scan", interval = 1 SECOND)

/obj/machinery/porta_turret/proc/scan_interval(datum/act/A)     // computed each run: fast while it has targets
	return length(targets) ? 1 SECOND : 3 SECONDS

/obj/machinery/porta_turret/proc/scramble_settings(datum/act/A)
	set_enabled(prob(50))
	set_lethal(prob(50))

/obj/machinery/porta_turret/ui_data(datum/act/A)
	return list("enabled" = enabled, "lethal" = lethal)
// scan and go_haywire are ordinary (datum/act/A) procs, not shown.

What this shows. enabled is what someone chose; armed is what is true now, a stat that the setting and operable both feed. An EMP is a timed hold on operable through emp_disable, so it ends by itself and never touches the setting. The scan work parks while the turret is not armed and wakes when it is, and the pop-up cover follows the same stat. The alien subtype changes params and retunes the keyed scan entry, with no code.

What it replaces: stat(nameof(armed), ALL, from = list(...)); req_clear(LOCKED) on each control; a toggle_setting by hand beside the same toggle in the UI; the hand-rolled EMP revert timer; polling process().

16.4 A human: Life, a stun, stasis, species, spontaneous vore

// /mob/living. Its vars and stats are in code/base (the base table in section 5); this one list composes it, in the library area (section 22).
CAPABILITIES(/mob/living)   // no provider here: hands come from the species (below), and a mob with none declares its own (section 8)
	contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_STUN))   // the one stun path: nothing holds can_act by hand
	contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_PARALYSIS))
	contributes(STAT_CAN_MOVE, STAT_CAN_ACT)
	outputs(PROC_REF(life_hud), at_most = LIFE_PRESENT_MIN_INTERVAL, when = nameof(client))
	outputs(PROC_REF(life_vision), at_most = LIFE_PRESENT_MIN_INTERVAL, when = nameof(client))

TRACKED(/datum/vore_preferences, can_be_drop_pred, can_be_drop_prey, schema = bool, persist = TRUE)

CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/carbon/human)
	ref_one(nameof(species), /datum/species)   // the relation species_capabilities() follows: rel_set(M, nameof(species), S) changes it, and the species' activation follows
	seq_step(PROC_REF(life_breathing), runs_after = LIFE_INPUT, when = PROC_REF(placed_and_alive),
		should_run = PROC_REF(life_breathing_due))
	seq_step(PROC_REF(life_chemicals), runs_after = LIFE_BODY, when = PROC_REF(placed_and_alive),
		should_run = PROC_REF(has_reagents_to_process))
	species_capabilities()   // the species' capabilities (its hands, its immunities, its abilities), granted while it is set
	owns_one(nameof(vore_prefs), /datum/vore_preferences)
	when(PROC_REF(spont_vore_on), spontaneous_vore())   // takeovers of fall, stumbled_into and thrown_hit, attached while the prefs allow them
SPECIES_CAPABILITIES(/datum/species/human, hands())                       // the hand provider: provides(AFF_MANIPULATE | AFF_ATTACK | AFF_HOLD, reach = 1)
SPECIES_CAPABILITIES(/datum/species/shadekin, hands(), phase_shift())     // the phase shift of 16.9: removed with the species

/mob/living/carbon/human/proc/placed_and_alive(datum/act/A)   // a condition over the frame's typed facts (section 3)
	var/datum/seq_frame/life/F = A
	return F.placed && F.alive

/mob/living/carbon/human/proc/spont_vore_on(datum/act/A)      // reads through the owned prefs: a generated hop
	return vore_prefs?.can_be_drop_pred || vore_prefs?.can_be_drop_prey
// life_hud, life_vision and the Life steps are ordinary procs, not shown.

// A stun baton. Its stun op has the baton as holder and the struck mob as target.
TRACKED(/obj/item/melee/baton, on, schema = bool, default = FALSE)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/melee/baton)
	cell_holder()
	op("toggle", in_hand(), toggles(nameof(on)))
	op("stun", at_target(/mob/living), hostile(), costs(RES_CHARGE, BATON_HIT_COST),
		needs(req_is(nameof(on), because = MSG(baton/off))),
		then(PROC_REF(stun)), plays(SFX_BATON_HIT), logs(LOG_ADMIN))
/obj/item/melee/baton/proc/stun(datum/act/A)
	status_at_least(A.target_atom, STATUS_STUN, BATON_STUN_UNITS, source = src)   // its own timer; a timed hold outlives the baton

// A stasis bed: while it works, its occupant's biology runs at a tenth of the speed.
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/stasis)
	powered()
	buckle(slots = 1)
	when(STAT_OPERABLE, while_slotted(SLOT_BUCKLE, holds(STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0.1), on = ON_CONTENTS))

A baton hit, then the bed:

  1. The stun op matches as an attack (hostile()). Require: the baton is on and its cell can pay. Do: the charge is reserved when Wait ends, status_at_least holds STATUS_STUN for BATON_STUN_UNITS Life cycles, sourced by the baton, and the charge commits once the stun has landed. can_act recomputes at once, so the mob's next click is refused by its own req_capable(). A species that grants immune_to(STATUS_STUN) reads 0 and refuses the hold, with no code here.
  2. The mob is buckled onto a working bed. The slot entry holds clock_rate_bio at 0.1. The mob's CLOCK_BIO runs at clock_rate times clock_rate_bio, so its Life frames, the stun's timer and every other status run at a tenth of the speed.
  3. The bed loses power. The bed reads its power through the area's membership, a collection edge, so its operable is marked and recomputes at the next marked drain, not inside the setter that cut the power. When it does, what reacts to the bed's own state is inline in that recompute: the when() around the slot entry is one of the inputs of the occupant's clock_rate_bio, and the occupant sits one single-valued edge away, in the bed's one slot, so that stat recomputes with the bed's and the hold stops applying. Unbuckling, or the occupant being deleted, ends the activation at once. No code releases anything.
  4. The player turns off spontaneous vore in the vore panel. The setter writes the prefs, spont_vore_on reads them through the relation, and spontaneous_vore() detaches at the drain.

What it replaces: the stunned stat and the hold on can_act; the raw baton hold that bypassed the status layer and ended if the baton was deleted; the stasis bed's buckled/unbuckled notice pair, which put the hold on whoever did the buckling; set_stasis() calls; string facts in seq_step(when = list("placed", "alive")); refresh_hud() calls; Login and Logout hooks that re-check vore prefs.

16.5 Reagents: a beaker, a syringe, a donut

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/glass/beaker)
	reagent_container(volume = 60, transfer = list(5, 10, 15, 30, 60), lid = TRUE)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/glass/beaker/large)
	configure(reagent_container(volume = 120))   // re-runs the body: the holder, the fill look and the transfer prompt follow

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/syringe)
	reagent_container(volume = 15, transfer = list(5, 10, 15))
	extend("reagent_container.inject", wait(3 SECONDS),
		needs(req(PROC_REF(skin_reachable), because = MSG(syringe/armour_blocks))),
		says(MSG(syringe/inject)), logs(LOG_ADMIN))
/obj/item/reagent_containers/syringe/proc/skin_reachable(datum/act/A)   // reads the target's suit through A.target: a typed hop
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.target
	return !istype(H) || H.can_inject(A.actor, FALSE)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/food/snacks/donut)
	edible(bites = 3)
	reagents(20, add = list(REAGENT_ID_NUTRIMENT = 3, REAGENT_ID_SUGAR = 2))   // the keys are defines, which expand to reagent ids

What this shows. The library owns the ops: reagent_container() brings pour, fill, splash, drink, inject, spray, the transfer-amount prompt and the fill look. The large beaker changes one param. The syringe keeps all of that and changes one op with extend: a wait, a requirement with a reason, feedback and an admin log. Because the requirement reads the target through A.target, the pending injection re-checks when the target puts a suit on, and is cancelled only if it now refuses. A pour or an injection is a transfer: the library reserves the volume on the source and the capacity on the target and commits both together (X2), so a full target refuses before anything leaves the beaker.

What it replaces: hand-written inject, draw and set-amount ops beside the library's; afterattack overrides; Initialize procs that only add reagents.

16.6 A belly: prey, struggling, absorption, digestion work

TRACKED(/obj/belly, digest_mode, schema = enum(DIGEST_MODES), default = DM_HOLD)   // a setting: the vore panel's UI op writes it through set_digest_mode()
TRACKED(/obj/belly, escapechance, schema = int(0, 100), default = 0)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/belly)
	interior(escape_wait = between(10 SECONDS, 60 SECONDS), escape_chance = nameof(escapechance))   // SLOT_BELLY_INTERIOR and "interior.escape"
	slot(SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED, accepts = list(/mob/living))
	while_slotted(SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED, absorbed(), on = ON_CONTENTS)
	op("struggle", inside(), cooldown(2 SECONDS), priority(above("interior.escape")),
		then(PROC_REF(struggle)))   // the click from inside; the escape is picked from the context menu (ORIGIN_MENU, which inside() accepts)
	extend(/datum/act/insert, needs(req(PROC_REF(prey_consents), because = MSG(vore/no_consent))))
	on_notice(/datum/notice/inserted, then(PROC_REF(prey_entered)))
	every(VORE_TICK, then(PROC_REF(process_prey)), when = PROC_REF(has_prey_to_process))

/obj/belly/proc/has_prey_to_process(datum/act/A)      // the digestion work parks while this is false
	return digest_mode != DM_HOLD && contents_count(src)

/obj/belly/proc/absorb(mob/living/prey)              // called by process_prey when absorption completes
	slot_transfer(prey, src, SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED)     // the absorbed capability follows from the slot
// struggle, prey_consents, prey_entered and process_prey are ordinary procs, not shown.

CAPABILITY_TYPE(absorbed, CAP_ABSORBED, /datum/capability/absorbed, key = NONE)       // stateless, so a belly's grant needs no selector
/datum/capability/absorbed/entries()
	return list(
		contributes(STAT_ACTS_VIA, ORIGIN_NONE, reason = MSG(vore/absorbed)),   // the actor gate: no clicks, UIs, abilities or commands
		contributes(STAT_SUSPENDED, TRUE),                                     // its clocks stop: no Life frames, no status timers
		extend(/datum/act/speak, instead(then(CAP_PROC(speak_to_pred)), order = ORDER_EARLY)),
		examine_line(MSG(vore/absorbed_examine)))

/datum/capability/absorbed/proc/speak_to_pred(datum/act/A)    // A.holder is the prey
	var/datum/act/speak/S = A
	var/mob/living/prey = A.holder
	var/obj/belly/B = container_of(prey)               // a spatial read, not a raw loc
	to_chat(B.owner, span_notice("[prey] whispers from within you: [S.message]"))

What this shows. Prey are the contents of slots. Absorption is a move to the absorbed slot, and while_slotted activates the absorbed capability with the slot entry as its source (X1). Prey that leave the belly, however they leave (digested, released, escaped, deleted), lose everything it gave them, and no release code exists. Struggling and the interior's escape are both inside() ops, so the struggle takes the click by a relative priority (the same-input rule, section 8) and the escape is offered in the context menu, where a pick runs it with ORIGIN_MENU. Consent is a requirement on the insert action.

What it replaces: the absorbed var, every "is absorbed" check and the release code that reset it; raw holds on absorbed prey; the setting() entry for the digest mode; intercept() on speech; two ops with the same input.

16.7 Simple mob AI: the same ops a player uses

CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/simple_mob/animal/space/carp)
	ai(list(/datum/ai_behavior/idle_wander, /datum/ai_behavior/approach_threat,
		/datum/ai_behavior/melee_attack, /datum/ai_behavior/flee_low_hp))
	natural_weapon(/datum/natural_weapon/bite, damage = 10)   // gives AFF_ATTACK, and the bite op a player-controlled carp uses too. The carp has no species and so no hands: its bite is its whole provider set

// The behaviours stay the flyweights of code/modules/combat_ai. The brain scores them with evaluate() and runs the
// winner through start(), tick() and stop(). Those are the brain's calls, not the engine's, so they keep their signatures.
/datum/ai_behavior/melee_attack/evaluate(datum/ai_brain/brain, atom/source)
	var/mob/living/owner = brain.get_owner()
	var/mob/threat = brain.primary_threat
	if(!threat || !owner.Adjacent(threat))
		return null
	return DQAI_RESULT(40, threat)

/datum/ai_behavior/melee_attack/start(datum/ai_brain/brain, atom/target, atom/source)
	. = ..()
	if(. == DQ_BEHAVIOR_FAILED)
		return
	perform_intent(brain.get_owner(), target, INTENT_ATTACK)   // the resolution, requirements, cooldown and messages of a player's click
	return DQ_BEHAVIOR_DONE

/datum/ai_behavior/flee_low_hp
	eval_triggers = list(/datum/notice/injured)                // ai() hooks it with on_notice, and the brain re-scores

What this shows. ai(behaviours) is the capability section 14 describes. It owns the /datum/ai_brain, runs the strategic (2 s) and tactical (0.25 s) loops as its own keyed every() entries, and turns each behaviour's eval_triggers into on_notice hooks.
