A behaviour acts through perform_intent() and perform_op() (origin ORIGIN_AI, authority AUTH_AI), so a carp's bite has the same requirements, cooldown and messages as a player's, and an AI actor's busy policy is BUSY_REFUSE. Movement behaviours keep step_to(); long paths become a /datum/io/path request.

What it replaces: the get_ai_behaviors TYPE_TABLE (now the list passed to ai()); attack_target() and shoot_target() calls in behaviours, with the checkClickCooldown() test in front of them; DQAI_TRIGGER_* signal keys (now notice types); the OM behaviours /datum/om/behaviour/ai_brain/strategic and tactical and their om_attach calls; dq_pathfind().

16.8 A cyborg: providers and affordances

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/gripper)
	provides(AFF_MANIPULATE | AFF_HOLD_SMALL, reach = 1, accepts = list(/obj/item/cell, /obj/item/circuitboard))
CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/borg/welder_module)   // a welder module: a tool, and nothing that handles things
	tool_quality(TOOL_WELDER)   // provides AFF_TOOL_WELDER, reach 1: not AFF_MANIPULATE
CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/silicon/robot)
	provides(AFF_CONTROL, authority = AUTH_REMOTE_ACCESS, reach = BORG_INTERFACE_REACH)   // it operates machines it can see. It has no hands: /mob/living declares no hand provider, a robot has no species to grant one, and a gripper or a module provides what it declares. The tile reach matters only to a remote() op that narrows itself to REACH_ADJACENT
// A borg-only op adds  when(req(/mob/living/silicon/robot, of = ON_ACTOR))  to hide it from everyone else,
// or  needs(req(/mob/living/silicon/robot, of = ON_ACTOR))  to refuse them with the library's reason.
The borg, at the APC of 16.1What resolves
Gripper carrying a cell, cover opencell_bay.cell.insert. The carried cell is A.held, so item(/obj/item/cell) matches, and the gripper is the provider of AFF_MANIPULATE.
Empty gripper, cover opencell_bay.cell.take. The cell goes into the gripper, because its accepts list allows cells.
Welder module selected, cover openNeither cell op: they need AFF_MANIPULATE, which a welder module does not provide. Its own tool ops still match: construction.dismantle needs AFF_TOOL_WELDER, which it does. With nothing else matching, the near-miss reason shows: "You can't do that without hands."
Across the roomRemote access only (remote(), AUTH_REMOTE_ACCESS): the window's buttons. The bay is physical and that authority never sees it.

What this shows. The APC has no borg code. The ops say which affordances they accept (a binding implies one, section 8), and providers say what they add, how far they reach, under what authority and what they carry. A module provides exactly what it declares, so a tool is not a hand, and a mob has hands only where something gives them: a species grants hands(), a gripper provides AFF_MANIPULATE, and a borg, an AI or a carp has neither unless it is given one. A borg's selected module is its held item, so swapping modules swaps providers.

What it replaces: attack_robot() overrides and isrobot() branches in handlers; the gripper's special-cased attackby forwarding; holds = list(...) on providers (now accepts =).

16.9 An ability, a granted capability, an admin hold

TRACKED(/mob/living/carbon/human, dark_energy, schema = int(0, 100), default = 100)

// Dark energy is a resource (X2): where the amount lives, and how it is spent. The base adapter reserves and releases.
RESOURCE_DEF(RES_DARK_ENERGY, adapter = /datum/resource/dark_energy, schema = int(0))
/datum/resource/dark_energy/proc/available(datum/act/A)
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.actor
	return H.dark_energy
/datum/resource/dark_energy/proc/commit(datum/reservation/R)
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = R.holder
	H.set_dark_energy(H.dark_energy - R.amount)

// Shadekin phase shift. The shadekin species grants phase_shift() through species_capabilities(): the SPECIES_CAPABILITIES line and the
// mob's ref_one species relation are in 16.4, and a species change is rel_set(M, nameof(species), S).
CAPABILITY_TYPE(phase_shift, CAP_PHASE_SHIFT, /datum/capability/phase_shift, key = NONE, cost = int(0, 100, default = 50))   // the macro declares var/cost from its schema
/datum/capability/phase_shift/entries()
	return list(
		op("shift", label("Phase shift"), menu(button = "phase_shift", bind = "shift+f"),   // "phase_shift.shift", under Abilities
			when(not(CAP_PROC(is_phased))),
			costs(RES_DARK_ENERGY, cost),                  // reserved once Require passes (there is no Wait); committed only if the grant succeeds
			grants(/datum/capability/phased, on = ON_HOLDER)),   // shift has no target binding, so A.target is the holder too; the grant lands on the holder, so its source is this activation: it ends with the capability
		op("unshift", label("Phase back in"), menu(button = "phase_shift", bind = "shift+f"),   // phasing back in is free
			when(CAP_PROC(is_phased)),
			then(CAP_PROC(shift_back))))

/datum/capability/phase_shift/proc/is_phased(datum/act/A)
	return granted(A.holder, /datum/capability/phased)

/datum/capability/phase_shift/proc/shift_back(datum/act/A)
	revoke(A.holder, /datum/capability/phased, source = A.activation)   // the same source grants() used

CAPABILITY_TYPE(phased, CAP_PHASED, /datum/capability/phased, key = NONE, drain = 1)   // drain: energy per second. The macro declares var/drain with this default
/datum/capability/phased/entries()
	return list(
		contributes(STAT_DENSITY, FALSE, priority = PRIORITY_FORCE),        // TOP: through walls and people
		contributes(STAT_INVISIBILITY, INVISIBILITY_SHADEKIN),              // MAX
		contributes(STAT_ACTS_VIA, ORIGIN_VERB | ORIGIN_HOTKEY, reason = MSG(phase/phased)),   // MASK_AND: abilities only, so the way back still works
		every(1 SECOND, then(CAP_PROC(drain_energy))))

/datum/capability/phased/proc/drain_energy(datum/act/A)
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.holder
	var/wanted = ceil(drain * A.dt / (1 SECOND))                     // whole units: the resource is an int
	if(res_spend(H, RES_DARK_ENERGY, wanted) < wanted)               // a per-tick drain takes its energy in steps
		perform_op(H, H, "phase_shift.unshift", origin = ORIGIN_SYSTEM)   // forced back through the button's own op: its log and notice apply. Its revoke ends the activation that owns this handler: the handler finishes first

// An admin holds a machine off. ADMIN_VERB passes the calling client as user, so there is no usr.
ADMIN_VERB(hold_machine_off, R_ADMIN, "Hold Machine Off", "Hold a machine off until you release it.", VERB_CAT_ADMIN, obj/machinery/M in world)
	if(held_by_source(M, STAT_OPERABLE, user.holder))
		release(M, STAT_OPERABLE, source = user.holder)
	else
		hold(M, STAT_OPERABLE, source = user.holder, reason = MSG(admin/held_off))

What this shows. The ability is two ops with a menu() binding: one entry under "Abilities", an action button and a keybind, refused with its reason when the mob has too little energy. costs(RES_DARK_ENERGY, cost) reserves the energy once the requirements pass and commits it after the grant lands, so a refused shift loses nothing. Being phased is a granted capability, and the grants() in shift lands on the op's own holder (shift is an actor's own op with no target binding, so A.target is the holder, and it says on = ON_HOLDER all the same), so the phase_shift activation owns it (X1): density, invisibility, the origin mask and the drain all arrive and leave with one grant and one revoke.

Removing the ability while it is in use. If a species change (rel_set(M, nameof(species), S) on the ref_one that the human of 16.4 declares) drops phase_shift while the mob is phased, species_capabilities() removes its activation. The one teardown path ends what the activation owns: the phased activation and its contributions go in the same step, the stats recompute, and the mob is solid and visible again. Nothing calls an op that no longer exists, because nothing needed to. A grants(..., outlives = TRUE) would opt out, and the grant would then run its course under the op source.

Ending the shift from inside it. When the energy runs out, drain_energy, a handler of the phased activation's own every(), performs the unshift op, and its revoke tears down that same activation. DM cannot unwind a running proc, so drain_energy finishes (the perform_op call has already returned), then the record drops and the every() never runs again: a revoked activation is dead at once (section 5). shift and unshift bind the same key, "shift+f", with mutually exclusive when parts, which the keybind collision rule allows.

Both capabilities are CAPABILITY_TYPEs, since their procs run as CAP_PROC(x) on the capability datum with A.holder the mob, and both declare key = NONE because a mob has only one of each. The admin's hold shows in VV with the admin as its source, and only that admin's release removes it. Editing operable in VV is vars_write(M, "operable", TRUE, source = user.holder), a hold_override that forces a broken machine on until released. Neither fights the power system's own value.

What it replaces: /datum/ability and the "Act" verb; the ability's legacy verb; hand-rolled "can act except this ability" checks; capability(KEY, ...) beside a /datum/capability/x with no key; a hand-written set_dark_energy() spend before the effect that might refuse; usr.client.holder in admin code.

16.10 A girder: branches, runtime materials, becoming a wall

STAGE_DEF(girder, standing)
STAGE_DEF(girder, loose)
STAGE_DEF(girder, struts)
STAGE_DEF(girder, reinforced)
STAGE_DEF(girder, wall)

/proc/girder()
	return construction(start(STAGE_GIRDER_STANDING),
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_LOOSE, tool(TOOL_WRENCH)),                            // the undo is derived: wrench it down again
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_STRUTS, tool(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER), from = STAGE_GIRDER_STANDING),
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED, stack(/obj/item/stack/material, 1)),     // from struts: any material hard enough
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_WALL, stack(/obj/item/stack/material, 2), wait(4 SECONDS),
			then(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/structure/girder, raise_wall)),
			from = list(STAGE_GIRDER_STANDING, STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED), undo = null),
		dismantle(any_of_tools(TOOL_WELDER, TOOL_PLASMA_CUTTER, TOOL_DIAMOND_DRILL), spawns(/obj/item/stack/material/steel, 2)))

CAPABILITIES(/obj/structure/girder)
	girder()
	extend("construction.build:girder_reinforced", needs(req(PROC_REF(stack_can_reinforce), because = MSG(girder/too_soft))))

/obj/structure/girder/proc/stack_can_reinforce(datum/act/A)   // reads the held stack through A.held: a typed hop
	var/obj/item/stack/material/S = A.held
	return S.material.integrity >= GIRDER_REINFORCE_MIN_INTEGRITY

/obj/structure/girder/proc/raise_wall(datum/act/A)
	replace_with(/turf/simulated/wall, built_material(src, STAGE_GIRDER_WALL),
		built_material(src, STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED), girder_material)    // the reinforcement is null on the plain branch

What this shows. from = gives a stage several ways in: a wall rises from a plain or a reinforced girder. Tool stages write no undo =, because the derived undo is the same tool reversed. stack() records the material actually used, so undo refunds that material and the wall is built of it: the ledger entry of an edge is written when its sheets are reserved, so raise_wall already reads the entry of the edge it runs in through built_material(src, STAGE_GIRDER_WALL). any_of_tools() uses the profile of the tool in hand, so the plasma cutter's speed shortens its wait with no special code. Stage ids are STAGE_DEFs, so a misspelt stage does not compile, and the stage's op key (construction.build:girder_reinforced) is checked against the declared stages. The instance's history, not a declared from, says where an undo goes, and built(E, STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED) is false for a wall built straight from the standing girder (section 12).

What it replaces: the girder's state var and its tool-act ladder; om_task_start timed tasks for reinforcing and walling; becomes(PROC_REF(...)) and becomes(T, n); string stage names.

16.11 A world system acting across entities: night shift

SYSTEM_DEF(nightshift)
/datum/system/nightshift
	lane = LANE_WORLD
TRACKED(/datum/system/nightshift, night, schema = bool)           // declares var/night, FALSE by default (the schema's zero value)
SYSTEM_ACCESSOR(nightshift, night_shift_active, nameof(night))     // declared in code/contracts/accessors: the one reactive way to read the system's private state

CAPABILITIES(/datum/system/nightshift)
	every(1 MINUTE, then(PROC_REF(check_time)))
	on_change(nameof(night), then(PROC_REF(announce_change)))
/datum/system/nightshift/proc/check_time(datum/act/A)
	set_night(using_map.get_nightshift())          // the setter compares: on most minutes nothing changes and nothing else runs

/datum/system/nightshift/proc/announce_change(datum/act/A)
	GLOB.priority_announcement.Announce(night ? NIGHTSHIFT_EVENING_TEXT : NIGHTSHIFT_MORNING_TEXT,
		new_title = "Automated Lighting System Announcement")

STAT(/area, lights_nightshift, ANY)                 // fed by the area's APC: contributes_to in 16.1

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/wants_night_lights(datum/act/A)   // night_shift_active() is a reactive accessor: a static hop to the system's tracked var
	switch(nightshift_setting)
		if(NIGHTSHIFT_ALWAYS)
			return TRUE
		if(NIGHTSHIFT_AUTO)
			return night_shift_active()
	return FALSE

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/light)
	look_layer("bulb-broken", when = nameof(bulb_broken))   // section 13's two entries: one list per type
	sound_loop(SFX_LIGHT_HUM, when = nameof(on))
	contributes(STAT_LIGHT_RANGE, PROC_REF(lit_range))   // a numeric contribution: the proc returns the range
/obj/machinery/light/proc/lit_range(datum/act/A)                 // the hop through power_area is a generated read
	if(!on)
		return 0
	return power_area?.lights_nightshift ? nightshift_range : brightness_range

What happens at dusk. check_time sets night. That is a write to system state, read through a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, so the stats that read it are marked, and the next marked drain (the start of D, P or R) recomputes them in rank order under the budget of LANE_SIMULATION, the lane of an area's and a light's stats (the system's own lane = LANE_WORLD budgets its every() work, not the stats it causes): each area's lights_nightshift, from its APC's wants_night_lights contribution, then, through the area's membership, each of that area's lights' light_range, from lit_range. A marked stat that changes marks its own readers, and the same drain loop recomputes them. The engine applies the new ranges. A flip across a large station can take more than one marked drain: if it outruns the budget the key chain is logged and the rest resumes at the next marked drain, which may be later in the same tick, and no light is skipped. The announcement is an on_change hook at the drain.

An APC set to "always" or "never" still subscribes to night, because generated reads are a static over-approximation: every read in the proc counts, whichever branch it sits in. Its recompute is cheap and returns the value it already had, so the stat does not change and nothing is notified. Content reads the flag through night_shift_active(), declared in contracts and visible to every layer, never as SSnightshift.night.

What it replaces: the night-shift service's loop over every APC, resumed across ticks with TICK_CHECK; apc.set_nightshift() and its tracked nightshift_lights copy; the earlier draft's re-hold of every area each minute; derive_nightshift_enabled(); direct reads of a system's private vars from content.

16.12 Worn mirror plating: takeover, modification and two mirrors

CAPABILITY_TYPE(mirror_plating, CAP_MIRROR_PLATING, /datum/capability/mirror_plating,       // the macro declares var/reflect_chance and var/absorb, with these defaults
	key = NONE, stacks = BEST(reflect_chance), reflect_chance = 30, absorb = 0.67)
/datum/capability/mirror_plating/entries()
	return list(
		// Take over: some beams bounce back. The hit ends here (ACT_REPLACED), and only a listener that asked hears it.
		extend(/datum/act/hit/projectile, instead(when(CAP_PROC(is_beam)), chance(reflect_chance),
			then(CAP_PROC(reflect)), order = ORDER_EARLY)),
		// Modify: a beam that gets through loses a third. No re-entry: the same hit goes on to armour and the body.
		extend(/datum/act/hit/projectile, adjusts(packet.amount, scale = absorb, when = CAP_PROC(is_beam))))

/datum/capability/mirror_plating/proc/is_beam(datum/act/A)     // A.holder is the wearer; the hit's fields are typed
	var/datum/act/hit/projectile/H = A
	return istype(H.packet.source, /obj/item/projectile/beam)

/datum/capability/mirror_plating/proc/reflect(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/act/hit/projectile/H = A
	var/obj/item/projectile/P = H.packet.source
	P.redirect(P.starting.x, P.starting.y, get_turf(A.holder), A.holder)   // back where it came from: if it lands, that is a new hit

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/clothing/suit/armor/laserproof)
	while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, mirror_plating(), on = ON_HOLDER)   // 30%: its wearer gets the hooks
CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/shield/mirror)
	while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, mirror_plating(reflect_chance = 45), on = ON_HOLDER)   // 45%

What this shows. Each item declares while_slotted(..., on = ON_HOLDER), so while it is worn its wearer has an activation of the plating capability, with the item as its source (X1). A granted capability's hooks are candidates for actions on the wearer, so a projectile hitting the wearer meets the takeover first (ORDER_EARLY). when() gates it to beams and chance() makes it take over some of the time. When it does not, the hit goes on, and adjusts scales its damage before mitigation and the sink, without starting the action again. Taking an item off ends its activation.

Two mirrors. A wearer in the vest (30%) who also wears the shield (45%) has two activations of one capability. stacks = BEST(reflect_chance) runs only the stronger: one roll, at 45%, not two rolls that compound to about 62%, and A.source in reflect is the shield. The vest's activation is shadowed, not removed, so taking the shield off hands the roll back to the vest. The absorb adjustment comes from the winner as well. The library's reflects() and blocks() are built this way.

What it replaces: DAMAGE_REACTION and REFLECTS rows; armour checks hand-written into the mob's bullet_act; a hook on the mob registered at equip and removed at unequip; the question "one roll or two" answered by whichever item registered last.

16.13 A prompt and a database answer in one op

TRACKED(/obj/machinery/librarycomp, selected_id, schema = int(0), default = 0)   // the archive row picked in the window; 0 is nothing selected

/// One result row: the columns of the query, typed and validated (X5).
/datum/io/sql/row/library_book
	var/author
	var/title
	var/content
SCHEMA(/datum/io/sql/row/library_book, author, text(64))
SCHEMA(/datum/io/sql/row/library_book, title, text(128))
SCHEMA(/datum/io/sql/row/library_book, content, text(MAX_BOOK_LENGTH))

/// The request kind: its parameterized query, the field it binds, and the row type of its answer.
/datum/io/sql/library_book
	query = "SELECT author, title, content FROM library WHERE id = :book_id"
	row_type = /datum/io/sql/row/library_book
	var/book_id
SCHEMA(/datum/io/sql/library_book, book_id, int(1))

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/librarycomp)
	interface("LibraryComp", title = "Book Inventory Management")
	ui_shape(selected_id)
	op("select", ui_act(arg("id", int(1))), then(PROC_REF(select_row)))
	op("print", ui_act(),
		needs(req_is(nameof(selected_id), because = MSG(library/nothing_selected))),
		captures(nameof(selected_id)),                                      // the id the player is about to confirm: snapshotted when the op first suspends, whichever step names it
		confirms("Print the selected book?"),                               // step 1: the yes/no window. Its prompt names no field, so the capture above is what holds the id
		asks(/datum/io/sql/library_book, book_id = nameof(selected_id), resume = CAPTURE),   // step 2: the archive query, bound to the captured id (CAPTURE is the default)
		cooldown(6 SECONDS),                                                // a cost: reserved only after the last answer
		then(PROC_REF(print_book)))

/obj/machinery/librarycomp/proc/select_row(datum/act/A, id)
	set_selected_id(id)

/obj/machinery/librarycomp/proc/print_book(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/io/sql/library_book/Q = A.answer      // typed. Only REQ_ANSWERED gets here: a deleted row ended the op as REQ_NO_RESULT
	var/datum/io/sql/row/library_book/B = Q.rows[1] // typed: Q.rows is a list of the declared row type
	new /obj/item/book(drop_location(), B.title, B.author, B.content)
	log_game("[key_name(A.actor)] printed archive book [A.captured(nameof(selected_id))]")   // the id the player confirmed, not the live selection

/obj/machinery/librarycomp/ui_data(datum/act/A)
	return list("selected_id" = selected_id)

One press of Print:

  1. Match and Require. The button matches the "print" op. Its requirements run: window access (range, consciousness), a selected row, and the require half of the cooldown.
  2. The first step of Wait. The op reaches confirms() in its Wait workflow and suspends there. Before it does, the op snapshots selected_id into the act, because the captures() part names it: the player is confirming that book. (The query step's book_id = nameof(selected_id) would capture it too, but the confirmation must not depend on a later step naming the field.) The yes/no window opens beside whatever else the actor has pending. Pressing Print again focuses that window rather than opening a second.
  3. Stale windows close themselves. While the window is open, the op's requirements are watched through their generated reads, and its keeps (the default includes ADJACENT). If the reader walks away, faints, or someone clears the selection, a requirement refuses, and the window closes showing that reason. If someone changes the selection to another book, a requirement still passes (a book is selected), and under CAPTURE it no longer matters: this op carries the id it captured. With resume = CANCEL_IF_CHANGED the op would end with "it changed while you were deciding".
  4. "No", or a closed window. The step ends REQ_CANCELLED and the op ends there. The cooldown was never reserved, so nothing is spent.
  5. "Yes". The when and Require parts run again and the captured fields are restored. The workflow moves to the second asks(): the request goes to the database system, bound to the captured book_id, and the op stays suspended while the database answers in phase K. Nothing blocks.
  6. The answer. The requirements run again, and only now, after the last answer, is the cooldown reserved; then Do runs. A query that matches nothing ends REQ_NO_RESULT, and the op ends refused with that outcome's message. Otherwise print_book runs with A.answer set to the answered query, typed; the cooldown commits after it returns (a proc that returns nothing reports OP_OK). With two answers A.answer holds the latest, and A.step("...") the earlier one.

What it replaces: act_ask() with a positional message and its callback chain; om_io() with a raw query string, a parameter list and a callback taking (list/result, error); COOLDOWN_START before the query, which spent the cooldown even when the answer never came; a selection read again after the confirmation, so the player confirmed one book and got another; tgui_alert_async; hand validation of params["id"].

16.14 A hopper that fills up during the wait

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/fabricator)
	powered()
	slot(SLOT_HOPPER, accepts = list(/obj/item/stack/material), capacity = HOPPER_SHEETS)   // RES_SLOT_CAPACITY reads this
	op("load", stack(/obj/item/stack/material, SHEETS_PER_LOAD), wait(2 SECONDS),
		put_in(SLOT_HOPPER),                       // declares the insert action's pre-check. stack() reserves the sheets (RES_STACK), and put_in() splits off exactly those sheets and moves the split
		says(MSG(fab/loaded)), logs(LOG_GAME))
	extend(/datum/act/insert, needs(req(PROC_REF(material_known), because = MSG(fab/unknown_material))))

Rae loads steel while Kai loads the same hopper.

  1. Match, Require. The click matches "load". Require passes, and so does the pre-check of the insert action: the hopper has room for SHEETS_PER_LOAD and the material is known.
  2. Wait. Two seconds. Nothing is reserved yet, so Rae's stack is untouched and anyone may use it.
  3. Meanwhile Kai fills the hopper to capacity.
  4. The wait ends. Require runs again, then the pre-check of the insert: the hopper's capacity is gone, so it refuses with the slot's reason ("The hopper is full."). The op ends ACT_REFUSED before any reservation was made: Rae still has every sheet, no op_done is published, no success message plays, and the refusal is logged with its key chain.
  5. If instead a takeover stops the insert (an instead hook on /datum/act/insert, say a jam guard) at effect time, the effect reports OP_REPLACED: the sheets are reserved and are released, the op ends ACT_REPLACED, and again nothing is spent.
  6. If a second effect runtimes after the insert landed, that effect reports OP_FAILED and the op ends ACT_REFUSED with the runtime logged: the reservation is released and the insert stays applied, because there is no general rollback. A pair of mutations that must be all or nothing is one engine verb, slot_transfer or replace_with, never two effects.

What this shows. Do is a commit (section 9): checks before irreversible work, reserve, effects that report, commit or release. The slot's capacity and the stack are both resources (X2), so the same contract covers them. What it replaces: a handler that took the sheets, called insert(), and refunded by hand when the hopper said no.

17Old to new

What this is. What each form game code uses today becomes. This table is curated: the 332 forms with 20 or more uses, or with a change in what content writes, in 225 rows, plus the constant families and the base-type overrides. The full generated table (all 1047 macros, procs and type procs and 1996 constants game code uses, with verdict, new form and phases) is doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv, written by mapping.py. Section 18 lists what stays.

Count method (stated once). A use is counted in game code only: every .dm under code/ outside the framework directories, unit tests and generated code, after stripping comments and string literals. Macros and constants count identifier occurrences. Global procs count call sites. A type proc counts its overrides plus its call sites, except where the same name belongs to an unrelated proc, when only overrides count. Rows are keyed by qualified name (/type/proc/name), so an English word such as system, after, leave or stage is never a row by itself. The codemod sizing in section 19 uses these figures: om_after 1310, om_ask 980, status_at_least 645, om_task_timed 424, om_hook 218, GRANT_VERB 263.

Verdicts over all 1047 macros, procs and type procs: 563 replaced, 33 renamed, 227 kept (section 18), 205 internal to the old engine (goes with its layer), 19 deleted outright. The 1996 constants follow their families: 1563 kept, 256 replaced or renamed, 85 deleted, 92 internal.

Lint on in phase. Each retirement lint switches on in the phase where its replacement lands (section 19), not before: until then the old form is the one that compiles. 1 (E1) to 1 (E6) are the engine workers, 1S the service ports, 2 the library, 3 the codemods, 4 and 5 the machine and Life tracks, 6 the content waves. The TSV also gives the phase in which the old form is gone.

Requirement rows change the contract, not only the name. The old requirement procs (REQ_TARGET_STATE, REQ_ON, REQ_PROC) took (actor, target, held) or one subject and returned TRUE-or-reason, or a boolean beside a reason string. The new ones are req(PROC_REF(x), because = ...) with x(datum/act/A) returning TRUE or FALSE; the reason text moves into because =. The conversion is mechanical once the signature changes, but a return "reason" left in place would read as TRUE, so the codemod rewrites the returns together with the signature.

Rows defined on the foundations. Where an old form now maps onto one of the five generic systems of the front matter, the new-form cell shows the generic form: a cost is a costs(RES_X, n) reservation (X2), a lasting effect is an activation (X1), a prompt or query is a workflow step (X3), a staged build is a state graph (X4), and an arg, a column or a setting range is a schema (X5), for example UI_ARG_INT is arg(name, int(min, max)).

18What stays as it is

227 of the 1047 forms game code uses stay, plus 1563 constants; the rest of this section groups them by family. The full list is the kept rows of doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv.

  • Rate limits and time. COOLDOWN_* (and COOLDOWN_KEYED_*), STAMP / ELAPSED, the time defines (SECONDS, MINUTES), and the after() family.
  • dt math helpers. approach(), decay(), chance_over(), move_toward(): what an A.dt handler calls. They are pure functions and keep their names.
  • Loot, maps and variants. DECLARE_LOOT and the LOOT_* rows, loot_spawn(), MAP_VAR, MAP_RESOLVER_VARS, and the variant tables (code/datums/variants/README.md).
  • Caching and tables. TYPE_TABLE*, GLOBAL_TABLE*, DECLARE_SHARED_CACHE* and CACHED*.
  • Containment and lifecycle. the lifecycle verbs (consume(), replace_with(), expire(), slot_clear()), on_destroy(), lifecycle_keep(), QDELETED; the spatial reads (contents_of(), locate_within(), FOR_CONTENTS, is_in_holder), the slot reads and moves (slot_item(), slot_contents(), move_into(), slot_remove()), the SLOT_ID_* ids and owner_of().
  • Registries and memberships. the REGISTRY_* ids, members_of(), join() / leave(), membership().
  • Statuses, traits and body effects. has_status, apply_body_effect, remove_body_effect, has_body_effect, set_stasis stay as named sugar over holds and grants, with typed ids (STATUS_X). BF_* body factors are now STAT_* in the same namespace (renamed, not kept).
  • Messages, sounds, chat. MSG_* and MSG_DEF, SOUND_SET, play_sfx(), fx_sparks(), to_chat and the span_* helpers.
  • tgui transport internals. the window pool, prewarm and assets, TGUI_WINDOW_*, ui_open() for code outside an op, and ADMIN_VERB for admin commands.
  • Kernel and system API. SYSTEM_DEF and the system hooks, seq_step() and seq_anchor, native_write(), PUBLISH / PUBLISH_CHANGE, TRACKED, grant / revoke / granted, rel_set / rel_add / rel_remove / rel_clear.
  • Domain APIs. the body API (injure, mend, fully_heal, vitality, factor), the gas mixture handle API, the heat API and the generated vg_* bindings.
  • Input and capability vocabulary. ORIGIN_*, REACH_*, AUTH_* (the split of the old route mask), AFF_*, INTENT_*, GESTURE_*, CAP_*, SPACE_*, keybind and input-adapter ids, and the library constructors section 11 names (cell_bay, gas_store, powered_by, ...).
  • Language helpers. GLOBAL_* declarations, CONFIG_GET, logging, rust_g wrappers, the is* type helpers and the LAZY* list macros.

The rule. An om_* form not listed in section 17 is internal to the old engine: content does not call it, and it goes with its layer.

19Migration plan

Phases 0 to 7, plus 0b (a clean base), 1S (service ports) and 2.5 (codemod infrastructure). Each phase lands on master when it passes its gate, under the existing rules: one worktree per worker; compile, focused tests while developing, one full suite at integration; fast-forward to master; follow-ups fixed on master. Phases 1 and 2 build what this document defines. Phase 2.5 builds the tool that moves callers, and phases 3 to 6 move every caller. Phase 7 deletes what is left of the legacy layer.

Three rules hold the plan together. Old and new forms coexist only through a small registered set of shims, never through ad-hoc wrappers (Coexistence rules). Every gate is a number measured against a pinned baseline commit (Gates). Every file under code/ has a destination, including the ~3,900 that never had a legacy form (section 22).

Moved out of phase 0. The decision that DM timers are kernel-only (no om_world_at, no DM-owned keys published into Rust, no reactor) is E6 work now, not phase 0. "Delete the unused OM machinery first" became a zero-caller sweep in phase 0b, because master still has callers for bundle.dm, check_library.dm and derived.dm.

Phase 0b · a clean base (now)

Nothing in phase 1 should start on a red, slow or noisy base. Order of work: land the safe branches, run the ratchets, clear the fix-now items, build the analysis engine and port the lints to it, then pin and measure.

The analysis engine

Each lint re-reads the tree. On a clean worktree a check_ratchets.sh run takes about 495 s and check_grep.sh about 146 s (sys_lint 139 s, api_lints 61 s, ownership 42 s, state_schema 39 s, lifecycle 33 s, scheduler 30 s, ...), because about 40 Python scripts and a grep script each read about 5,800 files one after another. Phase 0b builds the analysis engine of section 15 now, so every later phase works on one fast tool:

  • The tool. tools/analyze/, in Rust: one parallel load of the tree; a content-hash cache, so an incremental run rescans only changed files; comment and string stripping done once; text rules and parse-aware rules on SpacemanDMM's dreammaker parser behind a backend trait, so the incremental compiler's analysis API can replace it later; ALLOW annotations, baselines and exemptions handled once, with the same baseline files and the same output format as today; and the reason codes and per-lint ceilings of section 15's exemption policy, which are enforced here and not in a later phase.
  • Every lint is ported, the legacy-form ratchets included, but not all before phase 1. The engine core (load, cache, parser backend, ALLOW, baselines and exemptions) and the slowest lints (sys_lint, api_lints, ownership, state_schema, lifecycle, scheduler and check_grep.sh) are ported first, and phase 1 starts then; the remaining ports continue alongside phase 1 and finish before phase 2 opens. A lint switches to the engine only when a parity harness shows the engine and the old script report identical findings on master. check_ratchets.sh and check_grep.sh then become thin wrappers over the engine, and are deleted once CI calls the engine directly.
  • Later phases extend it. Phase 1's E5 adds the semantic queries (generated reads, key resolution, signatures, layering and the rest) to this engine rather than starting a new tool, and phase 2.5's codemods rewrite on its parse tree.
  • Gate for phase 1: the engine core in place, reason codes and ceilings enforced, the slowest lints at parity, and a full run measured cold and incremental, with the numbers recorded in doc/rewrite/baselines.md. The target is seconds, not minutes. Gate for the rest: parity on every remaining lint, before phase 2 opens; until each port matches, its old script keeps gating master.
  • What parity proves. Parity shows only that the engine reproduces the old findings. It says nothing about the new semantic checks of phase 1, whose soundness each check proves with its own fixtures: a seeded violation it must report and a clean case it must pass.

AGENTS.md, step one

AGENTS.md says "write what exists on master today": a legacy form stays correct until its replacement has landed and its retirement lint is on (the "switches on" column of section 17). A table gives each target form and what to write today. AGENTS.md stays the source of truth for what compiles; this document is the source of truth for where the code is going. Step two, the full rewrite to the new forms, lands with phase 2.

Measurement

  • Pin. The baseline for phase 1 is the first master commit with green ratchets after the merges above. It is recorded in doc/rewrite/baselines.md, one row per phase: commit, bench run ids, noise floors.
  • New bench scenarios on the old paths. The eight scenarios in Gates are written against public entry points (Click, tgui_act, om_hold, changed), so master has a number for each before phase 1 builds a replacement.
  • Noise floors. Five A/A pairs per scenario at the pin (method in Gates).
  • Zero-caller sweep. Delete OM machinery that git grep shows unused outside its own file (unused COMPONENT_* flags, unused library checks). bundle.dm, check_library.dm and derived.dm still have callers (/datum/om/bundle/* in library.dm; 51 OM_DERIVE_FIELD uses in 46 files), so they stay until their steps.

Phase 1 · engines

Phase 1 opens with three short gates that can fail the plan cheaply, run in parallel by one Opus worker: the name spike, the analysis-engine readiness gate and E0. Then seven workers run in parallel, each in its own directory against E0's contracts. Each engine is gated by a small fixture built from test-only capabilities (declared under code/tests/engine/ and compiled only under UNIT_TESTS), so no phase-1 gate needs the library.

The E0 proofs

Ten executable proofs, runnable DM fixtures on the engines' spike, settle the contracts most likely to force an expensive redesign after content conversion begins. They are written first, as failing fixtures against E0's stubs, and go green as E1 to E6 land. All ten must pass before any content conversion: they gate phase 1 and the first phase 3 step. Each is written with the forms of the test driver (section 15), the public engine verbs a fixture may call (grant, revoke, rel_set, ACT_TRY with act_done, move_into, action_options, screentip_for, built()), and test-only capabilities and types declared under code/tests/engine/. A fixture mob that needs hands gets them as a real one does, from a test species that declares hands(). An outcome is read from the /datum/op_result the driver returns, never from a pooled act; time passes only through test_time; and what happened is read from the recorder (test_recorded()) and test_logs().

E5, semantic queries
on tools/analyze/ (built in 0b); output in code/engine/_generated		The semantic layer of the 0b engine: type, proc and var resolution on its backend; generated reads with READS_AS/READS_FROM, relation hops, the condition audit, ranks and the cycle error; key resolution for op, capability, stat, stage and source ids; handler-signature, context-escape, ACT_TRY pairing and purity checks. The other engines write their generators (ACTION, CAPABILITY_DEF, STAT, ui_shape to TypeScript) as queries on this API, in the diagnostics format and exemption mechanism the engine already has.Reads spike and oracle first, on 20 real handlers (5 UI data procs, 5 condition procs, 5 draw procs, 5 Rust-push procs): the generated reads must contain every read in the existing hand-written lists (derived(), drawn_from, ui_from, rust_push) for all 20, with at most one handler needing a READS_AS/READS_FROM annotation. If it misses, E5 ships as a checker that verifies hand-listed reads, and sections 7 and 9 revise what reads means. Then: an unknown read in a requirement is a build error at file:line; an unannotated global call in a condition is an error carrying the READS_FROM hint; a cycle in the condition and stat graph is an error; the rank order matches a golden; one-file incremental analysis stays within its budget (Gates)

Phase 1 lands when the opening gates passed and the six engine fixtures, the L checks and the budgets in Gates hold on a full suite. The library examples are not a phase-1 gate: they need phase 2.

Phase 1S · service ports

The survey counted 56 ports: the 12 host subsystems (E6, because they need kernel internals) and 44 world services (here). 1S runs beside phase 2 and must land before phase 3 starts, so the codemods never touch a service twice. The port list is regenerated from git grep '^/datum/world_service/' at the start of 1S (it finds 49 types today, including the dq_yield_probe test probe and the machine and mob services that phases 4 and 5 dissolve).

  • Each port: /datum/world_service/x becomes SYSTEM_DEF(x) with needs (replacing boot_after and order_after), a lane, every() or an on-demand declaration, roles and membership where it keeps a roster, and an api.dm; state becomes VAR_PRIVATE; LAZY_SERVICE accessors become system access. Each port is written in final forms into code/world/systems (or code/domains/<domain> for a domain's own system) and the old file is deleted, so no later step touches it.
  • Five clusters, one worker each: round flow (antag, emergency shuttle, transfer, events, persist, maintenance); client and presentation plumbing (chat, runechat, statpanels, character setup, asset loading, skybox, vis overlays); simulation (radiation, explosions, turf cascade, motion tracker, solars, nightshift, planets, instruments, sounds); data services (supply, research, chemistry, circuits, xenoarch, mail, radio, vote, transcore); world content (expedition, flight, POIs, pAI, plants).
  • End: when the last service is ported, /datum/world_service, world_lanes.dm, LAZY_SERVICE, boot_after and order_after are deleted.

Phase 2 · library

Eight Opus workers, one per library area. Every capability in section 11 is rewritten as a CAPABILITY_DEF or CAPABILITY_TYPE in code/library/<area>; each old cap_* constructor becomes a registered shim over the new definition (see Coexistence rules) so unconverted callers keep working. B1, B2 and B3 resume on the new forms: B2's landed capabilities are re-expressed, and B1 and B3 start from their open branches.

Behaviour snapshot harness

The old gate said "the i7 snapshots pass unchanged". Those snapshots are keyed by interaction id and change when interactions become ops, so they cannot hold. Phase 2 builds a resolver-agnostic harness first, records it on the old resolver, and uses it as the behaviour gate for phases 2 to 6.

  • What it records: per (type, actor stance, held item, target state), through whichever resolver is active, as one line per case: the messages shown, the log lines, the tracked-state deltas, inventory transfers, resource commits and releases, activations made and removed, notices with their outcomes, pending work, and delayed outcomes. It is built on the test driver's recorder (test_record, section 15), the same one E0's sixth proof reads at phase 1.
  • Matrix: the types the I7 snapshots list today, about 390 (dq_i7_bulk_capture.dm 287, dq_i7_structures_bulk_capture.dm 85, dq_i7_items_bulk_capture.dm 18) plus the types in the four gate examples; the intents and gestures of the input table; a fixed set of 12 held items (empty hand, crowbar, screwdriver, wrench, welder, wirecutters, multitool, ID card, beaker, cell, a stack, a weapon); the target states each type declares.
  • Recording: at the pinned commit, on the old resolver. Golden files are checked in under tools/dx/snapshots/ and regenerated only with a recorded reason. A diff is either a bug or an entry in intended_changes.md with its reason; the gate is zero diffs not in that file.
  • The 10 hard cases of doc/rewrite/operations_and_actions.md section 7 (hand lost while holding, blocked hand recovers, two-handed port loss, borg gripper loss, feral mouth, contained actor, prompt returns after the target moved, explosion over many targets, notice deleting its source, two features enrolling one entity) are rewritten as test-driver fixtures (assert_resolves where the case only resolves) and kept.

Examples as tests, and AGENTS.md

This also retires the 152 doc_snippets baseline rows as old docs are rewritten or deleted.

Phase 2.5 · codemod infrastructure

Phase 3 runs scripts that rewrite thousands of sites, so the tool is built and tested first. A regex cannot safely rewrite nested macro calls such as INTERACT_ITEM(name, effect, REQ_ON(...), ...). Inputs: the analysis engine with phase 1's queries (parse tree, resolution) and, for each codemod, its target form from phase 2. Workers: one Opus for the rewriter and key-collision report, one Sonnet for fixtures.

  • AST rewriter. Built on the analysis engine's parse tree, with each macro call and its nested arguments as nodes. Edits are span replacements that keep comments and formatting. The flat scripts landed in 0b (timers, ownership, fields, damage reactions, default children, verbs, grants, reagents, events) are ported to it and retargeted to the final forms; INTERACT_*, UI_ACT and TOPIC_ACTION are new.
  • Fixtures per codemod: input and expected output for every shape; "already converted" inputs that must be no-ops; and negative inputs that must stay untouched and be listed as residue. a4-skel's fixtures are the seed.
  • Key synthesis. An op needs a type-unique stable key. It is derived from the entry's name (UI and topic ops reuse the action name). Where an inherited or capability-brought key collides, the codemod emits extend() if the old semantics appended and a macro-kind prefix if the entries were independent. The key-collision report lists every synthesized key and every resolution before the freeze window.
  • Residue. A dry run on today's tree reports, per form, the count it cannot convert. Those integers are committed in tools/dx/codemods/residue.json before the step's window opens (Gates).
  • Reason codes and ceilings. Already enforced by the engine since phase 0b (section 15's exemption policy); a codemod that removes the last site of a form lowers that lint's ceiling in the same commit.

Coexistence rules

For most of phases 2 and 3, old and new forms run side by side on the same entities. Each seam below has one direction, one mechanism and one removal point. Nothing else is allowed to bridge old and new.

SeamDirectionMechanismRemoved when
Events: PUBLISH and on_notice against OM_EMIT and om_hookBoth waysEach side emits the other's twin, generated from tools/dx/codemods/om_event_map.json (CI already checks the generated file). No hand-written bridge.Per event, when its last om_hook or EVENT_HANDLER converts (step A7). The last event deletes the map.
State: a TRACKED write against the legacy CHANGE_* channelsNew to oldA build-time shim table records, per var, the channel its old declaration raised. The setter raises the tracked key and that channel, so the machine and Life pipelines, which wake on channels, never miss a wake. An old OM_FIELD write publishes its tracked key to generated reads and on_change (an E1/E3 deliverable, fixture-gated).The channel shim goes with the pipeline that consumes it: machine channels with machine_pipeline.dm (end of phase 5), Life channels with Life pipeline.dm (end of phase 6). OM_FIELD itself at step A4.
Declarations: legacy constructors inside CAPABILITIESOld entries in the new tableA closed list: cap_op and its presets, refine, cap_require, replace, lib_op, adopt_entry, op_attach; the cap_* library constructors as shims over the phase-2 definitions; DECLARE_INTERACTIONS entries; the capabilities(), reactions() and relations() procs read as one list. Each carries the step that deletes it.Procs at A2; op constructors and interaction entries at B1; each cap_* library shim when its last caller converts.
Resolution: old interaction entries beside new opsOne passThe new resolver is the only entry point. Legacy entries are candidates carrying the tier and intent of their macro, and INTERACTION_HANDLED_PASS maps to passes(), so a click resolves among both and pass-through behaves as it does today.Step B1.

Contributions: om_apply and om_hold beside hold()None neededOne contribution store. Old and new writers interoperate by construction, with no shim.The old procs go with grants (A8) and statuses (phase 5).

Scheduling: the OM scheduler and pipelines beside systemsBoth under the kernelOnce the Master Controller is gone (E6), the OM scheduler and the machine and Life pipelines run as kernel items, until their tracks delete them.Machine pipeline end of phase 5; scheduler and Life pipeline end of phase 6.
Verbs: DECLARE_VERB*, type_verbs(), GRANT_VERB beside verb_entry and ability opsBoth feed one storeThe verb store is the only writer of verbs; every form goes through it.Step A8.
Requirements: REQ_* macros beside req()Old inside oldLegacy REQ_* are valid only inside legacy op constructors; the new needs() takes requirements only.Step B2.
  • One registry. Every shim is one line in code/engine/shims.dm (name, the step that owns it, the step that removes it). The shim lint fails if a shim is still present once its removing step's post/<step> tag exists, or if a shim exists without a line.
  • Retirement lints switch on with the replacement. A retirement lint turns on as a ratchet (no new site) the moment its replacement is on master, and becomes a hard ban in the commit that deletes the last caller. The "switches on in" column of section 17 says when. Before that, AGENTS.md says to write the old form.

Phase 3 · codemods, tree-wide

Phase 3 converts every mechanical form across the whole tree. One form per commit, one freeze window per step, run by the script from 2.5, with a full suite per commit. A step starts when its target form is on master and its codemod passes the 2.5 gates. The steps are ordered by what they need. Layout moves and the icon rename are separate mechanical commits, never mixed with a semantic change: a commit either moves files or renames icons, or changes meaning, never both.

Polarity and signature (B2). A requirement is now a boolean plus a reason, and every proc takes (datum/act/A). Old REQ_ON(subject, proc, "reason") was already a boolean predicate with a separate reason. Old REQ_TARGET_STATE procs took (actor, target, held) and returned TRUE or the reason text. The codemod rewrites the proc head and every return, and moves the reason text into a generated MSG_DEF. A reason string left in a return would read as TRUE and silently allow the op, so E5 flags any requirement proc that can return anything but TRUE or FALSE, and the fixtures include that shape. A body that fits neither shape is left and counted as residue.

Mob and machine forms are not mechanical at tree scale. status_at_least (645) and the other status_* procs (names kept as sugar), apply_body_effect (175), add_trait/has_trait/remove_trait (42, 120, 25), set_stasis (18), incapacitated( (250) and the BF_* ids belong to phase 5. operable() (311) and stat_add/stat_remove (27, 14) belong to phase 4.

Freeze windows

  1. Announce the window one working day ahead with the step, its tag names and the dry-run report: converted count, residue count and the key-collision report.
  2. Tag master pre/<step>. While the window is open only the step's own commits merge to master.
  3. Run the codemod as one commit whose message names the codemod and its counts; then DreamChecker, the focused tests, one full suite and the bench scenarios named in the step's gate.
  4. Merge, tag post/<step>, close the window. A window is hours; one that reaches a day is split.

In-flight branches rebase by re-running the codemod, not by merging around it (tools/dx/codemods/rebase_branch <branch> <step>):

  1. Merge pre/<step> into the branch. These are ordinary conflicts; none come from the codemod.
  2. Run the step's codemod on the branch's tree. It is idempotent: master's sites are no-ops, and the branch's own new sites convert.
  3. Merge post/<step>. The codemod is deterministic, so branch and master made identical edits to every converted site and git merges them cleanly. A conflict left is in a line the codemod does not own.

Order against phases 4, 5 and 6

Phases 4 and 5 start only after phase 3 has landed in full, icon rename included, because the tracks edit the same machine and mob files the codemods rewrite. They may then run beside each other and beside the waves that are neither machine nor mob. Machine waves wait for phase 4; mob waves wait for phase 5.

Tags, revert and soak

  • Tags. pre/<step> and post/<step> around every tree-wide commit: the phase-3 steps, the icon rename and each move wave. archive/<branch> for abandoned branches.
  • Revert recipe, nothing stacked yet: git revert the step's commit.
  • Revert recipe, later steps stacked (steps build on each other, so reverting one alone is not mechanical): git switch -c redo/<step> pre/<step>; fix the codemod; re-run it and then every later step's codemod in order, applying each step's recorded post-fixes (tools/dx/codemods/post_fixes/<step>.patch, the hand edits made after the script ran); cherry-pick master's non-codemod commits made since pre/<step>; land through a new window. Each step's revert is tried once on a scratch branch before its window opens: it must build and pass the focused tests.
  • Soak windows. The tagged build runs on the live server for at least 7 days and at least 10 rounds, whichever is longer. The scripted checklist below runs at the start and after every hotfix. Exit: no stuck-machine, unclickable-object or death-spiral report, and no new error signature in the runtime log against the week before. One soak follows step B1 (click resolution only, since resolution order changes for about 4,300 sites); one follows phase 4 and one follows phase 5, each before the pipeline it frees is deleted. The deletion lands one phase after the last caller converts.
Checklist areaStepsPass

APCCover off with a crowbar, cell out and in, cover on, lock with an ID, breaker from the UI, an EMP pulse, AI hack and resetEach change shows in the sprite, the UI and the area's power

Phase 6 · content waves

Waves W01 to W35 (about 1,900 files) finish what the codemods left. Their file lists are regenerated after phase 3 from the lints' output: the files that still carry residue, which means the semantic leftovers (wait() conversions the script could not classify, verbs it could not sort, requirement bodies that fit neither shape, instance lists that need judgment, slot() declarations). Each wave does four things in order:

  1. Convert the files in the wave.
  2. Prove them: zero old forms per file, tests, the behaviour snapshot harness for the wave's types, the construction round-trip where it applies, and the bench scenarios.
  3. Move them in a pure git mv commit, once the duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff pass (section 22).
  4. Merge.

Staffing: four to six Sonnet workers at once; Opus reviews and lands each wave. Order: machine waves W26 to W34 after phase 4 (W34, medical machines, after occupant); mob waves after phase 5. Two waves are domain work rather than file lists, and have their own tests:

Phase 7 · delete and lock

  • Delete whatever is left of the legacy layer (section 20). Most of it is already gone; this is the sweep, not the first deletion.
  • Lints: every legacy-form lint is at zero, so it becomes a hard ban and its baseline file is deleted. Lints that stay run as queries on the analysis engine; DreamChecker is removed once the engine covers its rules.
  • Docs: delete the archived OM docs; convert this document to doc/rewrite/api.md and chapters; the coverage script finds zero uses of any deleted form.
  • Completion test (section 22): every legacy-form lint at zero and the move table complete. Then the exhaustive tier and the bench against the phase-0 pin.

Gates

Every gate is a number. The method is the same for all of them.

  • Pinned baseline. Each phase's baseline is a master commit recorded in doc/rewrite/baselines.md when the phase starts: commit, bench run ids, noise floors. A gate compares against its own phase's pin, so "today's master" never moves under it. Phase 7 compares against the phase-0 pin.
  • Noise floor. For each scenario, five A/A pairs at the pin (two independent runs of bench --runs=3 per pair). The floor n is the largest relative difference inside any pair, rounded up to the next 0.5%. bench-compare's significance test must also reject "no difference" before a regression is called.
  • Pass rule. A metric passes when new ≤ pin × (1 + max(n, p)), p being the budget in the table below. A budget of "pin" means p = 0: only noise is allowed. The budgets here are the defaults, ratified when 0b records the first noise floors.
  • Residue. One integer per retired form in tools/dx/codemods/residue.json, set by the step's dry run (the count the codemod reports it cannot convert) and committed before the window opens. A step's gate is its lint count at or under that integer. Every integer is 0 by the end of phase 6; nonzero residues are listed by file in the wave plan.

Bench scenarios

Added in phase 0b, before phase 1. Each drives public entry points that old and new code both offer (Click, tgui_act, om_hold or hold, changed), so master has a number for every path the plan replaces. They join the existing tick, idle, Life, power and boot scenarios.

ScenarioDrivesMetrics
click_resolve_30100 clicks per second for 10 seconds on a target whose candidate stack has 30 entries (hand, item, alt and drag mixed)p50 and p99 cost per click; ticks over budget
hover_60Hover over 60 atoms in turn (screentip and menu evaluation)Cost per hover; cache hit rate
ui_windows_2020 open UI windows whose data changes every tick (the UI push storm)Cost per tick; bytes pushed
op_wait_churn1,000 waits (prying, welding) started per second, 30% cancelledCost per start and per cancel; pending records left at the end (must be 0)
stat_hold_churn10,000 hold and release pairs per second over 1,000 entities with 3 sources eachCost per pair; recomputes per pair
output_drain5,000 entities dirtied in one tick, each with a look, a UI and a Rust outputPhase R cost; outputs run per entity per frame (must be 1)
idle_machines_1k1,000 settled machinesWork items run per tick; tick cost
apc_flip_50One APC channel flips with 50 dependent machines (E3's spike), measured inline and marked for the drainCost per flip; recomputes per flip

Gates that apply to every phase

  • Retirement lints: no new site of a form whose replacement has landed. Shrink-only baselines keep counting down.
  • Runtime audits: output drift, missed wakes, condition drift and reference leaks are clean in the full suite.
  • Behaviour: zero snapshot or hard-case diffs that are not recorded in intended_changes.md with a reason.
  • Plan lints: the shim lint and the duplicate-definition lint are clean.
  • Bench: tick, idle, Life and boot memory are measured before and after every engine, library and track phase.

Risks

RiskMitigation
Per-instance cost of stats, inline recompute on a fan-out, or part-engine dispatch overheadBitfield contributions, interned compiled tables and integer keys; E3's apc_flip_50 spike and the idle and boot-memory budgets in phase 1.
The analysis front end is not ready, or its parse diverges from SpacemanDMM'sThe readiness gate (0 parse divergence over every file) opens phase 1. If it fails, the engine keeps its dreammaker backend behind the same trait and the front end swaps in later. The engine only analyses: dm.exe still produces the .dmb. Every lint moves onto the engine only after its parity harness matches the old script, and DreamChecker keeps running until the engine covers its rules.
The 0b engine takes longer than plannedLints switch over one at a time behind the parity harness, so the old scripts keep gating master until each port matches. Phase 1 starts once the engine core and the slowest lints are ported; the other ports run alongside it, since E5 needs the engine's load, cache and backend, not every port.
Generated reads cannot cover real DM (dynamic vars[], call, global helpers)E5's spike on 20 handlers with an oracle; the fallback is a checker over hand-listed reads; an unannotated global call is a build error carrying the READS_FROM hint.
The semantic contracts (consistency, commit, capture, stacking, reach) turn out wrong after content conversion startsThe ten E0 proofs run before any conversion and gate every phase-3 step; marked fan-out is measured by proof 8, which may promote membership edges to inline.
The new semantic checks pass because they are unsound, not because the tree is cleanLint-engine parity proves only that old findings are reproduced. Every new check ships with a seeded violation it must catch and a clean case it must pass.
Builds get slowerOne-file incremental analysis within 5 s and a full pass within 20% of a clean compile; one daemon shared by agent worktrees.
Codemods that subtly change semantics (the reagent add/starts class of bug, requirement polarity)One form per commit; fixtures with negative cases; exact-value tests along inheritance chains; the snapshot harness; E5 flags a requirement proc that returns text; Opus reviews behaviour diffs.
Source conflicts: open branches against tree-wide commitsThe triage table closes 21 branches before phase 1; freeze windows; in-flight branches rebase by re-running the codemod, not by merging; pre/ and post/ tags.
A shim outlives its phase and becomes permanentOne registry file; the shim lint fails once the removing step has landed.
A file move changes which definition winsThe duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff gate every move wave; the legacy layer keeps today's order.
Merge hazards in generated files, baselines, maps and dmi filesGenerated files are never hand-merged (the .dme, _generated, baselines via --update). Waves never touch maps or icons (varmap and rename run centrally).
The size of the UI and interaction conversion (~4,300 sites)Skeleton codemods first, agents finish. Each UI op keeps its old action name, so TSX doesn't change. A soak follows B1.
Click behaviour changes from the new resolution orderThe snapshot harness encodes today's behaviour; passes() preserves pass-through; legacy entries resolve in the same pass until B1.
A pipeline is deleted before the live server has exercised its replacementTags, the revert recipe, soak windows, and deletion one phase after the last caller.
Gates drift with a moving baseline, or sit below the noiseA pinned baseline per phase; noise floors from A/A pairs; the significance test.
Agents write code that cannot compile because AGENTS.md describes the targetThe two-step AGENTS.md with its "on master" column; a retirement lint switches on only when its replacement lands.

Corrections to older docs. om_changed() does not exist: the archived docs still name it, but the real procs are changed() and om_raise_change(). SSbehaviours no longer fires; it is already folded into the kernel tick and only keeps boot, the audit switch and bench counters. Neither needs a mapping row, since section 17 lists only forms game code uses.

21Decisions settled here

The docs synthesis found places where two documents, or a document and the code, define different forms for the same job. This design settles each one. The losing form joins the deletion list. Rows are grouped by area; the section that defines a form is the one that covers its area.

Names and vocabulary

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Names that clash with BYOND built-insSTAT(T, name, RULE, ...); outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =); stage(STAGE_X, ...) for construction; verb_entry(path, login =, when =); blocks(chance); plays(SFX); configure(KEY, variant = T); components(slots), req_component(T), component_swap; look.layer(...), look_layer(name, when =). The phase 1 compile spike confirms each on the pinned BYOND build, and a clash renames the form everywhere before E1 starts.stat() entries, output(), step(), verb() entries, block(), sound() parts, as =, machine parts()/req_part/part_swap, look.part/look_part
Overloaded wordsOne meaning each. Setting is a player-chosen tracked var (a capability's knobs are its params). Part is an op part. Act is /datum/act, and the verb-panel entry is "Abilities". Hold is a stat hold (a provider's filter is accepts =). After is the timer after(): ordering is runs_after =, the holder-clock form inside an op is delayed(t, parts...), and hook order is order =. Stage is an op stage or a build stage, never unqualified. The definitions live once, in the front-matter Vocabulary section; the section 8 box is a pointer to it.Capability settings, holds = filters, the "Act" panel entry, the after = ordering argument, then_later, a separate vocabulary box per section
Effect targetsON_TARGET (default), ON_HOLDER, ON_ACTOR, ON_HELD; ON_CONTENTS and ON_HOLDER for while_slottedON_SELF, OP_ACTOR/OP_HELD
Duplicate spellingsitem(T) plus put_in(slot); becomes(T) replaces the holder and spawns(T, n) adds; costs(RES_FUEL | RES_USES | RES_CHARGE | RES_STACK, n) beside consumes(); label("Rename") names an op, with no null name argument; labelable() is the item capabilityinsert(T); becomes(T, n) and becomes(PROC_REF); fuel(), uses(), uses_charge(); a null op name
Library namingPlain nouns for every library constructor (cover(), panel(), reagents(), trait()). cap_ is reserved for framework procs (cap_data). A capability's params are its settings: CAPABILITY_DEF and CAPABILITY_TYPE below.The cap_<noun> prefix rule, cap_trait, a separate reach(range)

Conditions, requirements and the one signature

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
ConditionsA boolean with no reason: nameof(v) or a STAT_X / capability key id (a truthy test), not(), all_of(), any_of(), PROC_REF(x) or CAP_PROC(x) returning TRUE or FALSE, a library requirement's boolean view. Strings are never conditions.when = list("placed", "alive") and other quoted fact names
Requirement languageA requirement is a condition plus a reason, in one language. Library constructors carry a default reason (MSG_DEF(req/<ctor>, ...)); generic tests give one: req_is(KEY, value, because =), req_at_least(KEY, n, because =), req(PROC_REF(c), because = MSG(x)); a dynamic reason is because = PROC_REF(reason_proc). A requirement with no resolvable reason is a build error. needs() takes requirements only, and when() takes conditions.REQ_*/PRED_*/REQ_ON/REQ_BECAUSE/HOLD_* macros, /datum/predicate, REQ_FIELD*, else_say, procs that return null to allow or a reason, req_stat/req_set/req_clear, a bare nameof(v) as a requirement
One handler signatureEvery proc the engine calls to run a part or a trigger is x(datum/act/A) with A.holder always set: effects, requirements, conditions, hook handlers, every() and sequence work, outputs, then(), because = PROC_REF, CAP_PROC. PROC_REF runs on the holder, CAP_PROC on the capability datum. Outside a trigger the kernel passes a reused, non-allocating evaluation context. safe_call is a kernel utility and an exemption, with the engine interfaces that have a signature of their own: draw(look), push_to_rust(), entries(), the resource adapter procs, a schema's normalize(A, value) and a system's lifecycle hooks.The (mob/user, atom/holder, obj/item/held) global check procs, (holder) condition procs, two-argument handlers such as toggle_phase(M, A)
Relaxing a requirementRequirements take id = "x"; a subtype relaxes one with extend("cover.open", drop = "x"). any_of joins reasons, all_of reports the first failing. req(T, of = ON_HELD) is the type test; none_of is dropped.Replacing a whole op to drop one check
PurityTest builds run requirements and conditions under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs.Review-only convention
Why a gate refusedThe actor and reach gates filter silently so input can fall through. If nothing survives, the engine shows the best near-miss's reason, rate-limited. Holds on acts_via take a reason.Per-handler "you can't do that" messages for being absorbed, restrained or out of reach
Reaching a slotReaching the target is Match (silent). An op, slot or graph placed at(SPACE_X) needs the path to that space open: set aside while another candidate answers, refused with the blocking door's reason when none does. Latches, protrusions, staged spaces and fits = say the rest (section 8).Manual "is the panel open?" guards before reaching contents; when(COVER_OPEN), when(PANEL_OPEN), req_panel_closed(), req_built() on an insert, a per-type cover rule; compartment(BAY_X, door =) and BAY_*

Stats, sources and keys

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Declaring a statSTAT(T, name, RULE, ...) declares the var, its default and the id STAT_NAME together, so they cannot disagree. A stat var set in a type definition is a type-level contribution.A stat(nameof(v), RULE) entry beside a separate var/ line
Same stat name on two typesThe id is per name. Two types may declare the same name only with the same rule and base (a shared meaning, such as operable); otherwise it is a build error naming both declarations.Silently merging unrelated stats that happen to share a name
Combine rulesOne table, each rule with a base value: ALL, ANY, SUM, PRODUCT, MAX, MIN, TOP (highest priority, ties to the most recent), SET (the union of its members, no ties), MASK_AND, MASK_OR, FORMULA. The contradictions are fixed: power_draw is SUM, acts_via is MASK_AND, density is TOP, opacity ANY, and the bio clock runs at clock_rate × clock_rate_bio, both MIN.The earlier table with no bitmask rule and conflicting rules for power draw, stasis and the body factors
When a stat is currentLogical state is settled before the setter returns, on the written entity and one single-valued relation edge away: stats there (including contributions gated by a condition) recompute inline in build-time rank order, bounded by the cycle check. Every other stat the write reaches, through a collection or membership edge, a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR or two or more edges, is marked and recomputed at the next marked drain (the start of phase D, P or R) in rank order under the budget of its lane; the build derives which from the relation declarations. Hook attachment, hooks and outputs wait for the drain. A stat var read is a plain var read (see "What is consistent when", section 7).An unqualified freshness promise; "pulled on read" (DM has no property getters, so a var cannot recompute when read); "shared source" and "system source" as separate criteria for what is marked
Body factors and statusesBF_* factors are STAT_* ids in the same namespace; BF_ARMOR(kind) and BF_INCOMING(category) become one stat per kind (STAT_ARMOR_BLUNT, ...) and per category (STAT_INCOMING_THERMAL, ...). A status is a numeric intensity stat (STAT(..., MAX, reapply = REAPPLY_MAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE)). The sugar is defined on it: status_at_least(M, STATUS_X, units, intensity =, source =), status_set (= hold_until), status_adjust, status_end, status_remaining, has_status. Immunity is immune_to(STATUS_X), which sets the companion stat STAT_<NAME>_IMMUNE. Stuns are statuses, nothing holds can_act by hand, and there is no stunned stat.A raw hold on a boolean stunned, EFFECT_IMMUNE_*, "immunities are ANY stats", a separate body-factor namespace
Derived valuesSTAT(T, v, FORMULA, formula = PROC_REF(f)), evaluated in the stat layer. A stat fed by several sources lists them as separate contributes() lines.derive(nameof(v)) with the magic derive_<v>(), stat(..., from = list(...))
Contributing across entitiescontributes_to(nameof(rel), STAT_X, cond | value, priority =): released when the relation changes or either end is deleted. A system keeps a tracked night var and areas contribute from it.A loop that re-holds every related entity on a timer (the night-shift system)
Holdssource is required on hold(). A hold with lasts = ends at its time even if its source is deleted (the baton's stun survives the baton); a hold without it ends with its source. reapply = REAPPLY_* on the stat sets what a repeat does; hold_until sets an absolute end; hold_override(E, STAT_X, value, priority = PRIORITY_ADMIN) is the forcing form. A hold on a clock-rate stat runs on CLOCK_WORLD, and remaining-time reads return deciseconds on their own clock. A source holding more than HOLD_RELEASE_BATCH holds (default 256) releases them in slices in phase G, coalesced per target, and a slice's recompute runs at the next marked drain.A timed hold dying with its short-lived source; per-caller "extend if longer" wrappers
SourcesA datum or a SOURCE_DEF(ai_control) flyweight (SRC_AI_CONTROL); text or null is an error; an activation is a datum and a valid source. An op's holds() and grants() default to the op's activation when they land on its own holder (they end with it; outlives = TRUE opts out) and to the op source, owned by the target's store, when they land on another entity. source = SRC_HELD_ITEM means "the item owns it while it is in that hand or slot", and the item's deletion ends it separately; an op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold is a build error. source = A.actor, or source = ON_ACTOR as a part, is the per-actor source ("this AI's bolts"). A slot's source is the occupying item for ON_HOLDER and the slot entry for ON_CONTENTS. SRC_VV is the source of admin edits, and SRC_ALL is a wildcard accepted by release() and status_end() only.String sources such as "ai" and "wire"
Capability state keyscap_keys(CAP_COVER, OPEN = MSG(cover/closed), ...) generates the COVER_OPEN ids, the <cap>_<key>(holder) accessors (cover_open, panel_open, lock_locked), default reasons and read registration. READS_AS stays only for non-capability producer keys, with a build check that the accessor's own reads are a subset. A cap key's declared reason serves its usual test; the opposite test needs because =. A stat or state word holds at most 24 flags, so cap_keys() allocates one word per capability.Hand-written READS_AS for capability state; accessors that duplicate a var; one shared bit space
Op, capability and tag keysOp keys are literals. Ops a capability brings are namespaced automatically ("cover.open"), and those of a capability with a selector include it (cell_bay.cell.insert); a type's own ops are bare; construction keys are construction.build:<stage>, construction.undo:<stage>, construction.dismantle. A capability key is (CAP_X, selector), the selector named by the definition's key =: an identical repeat dedupes, the same key with different params is a build error. The build resolves every literal in extend, without, configure, shares_effects, perform_op, on_op and priority(above()); an unknown key at runtime is an error, not a no-op.Unprefixed keys that collide across capabilities, computed keys, a silent perform_op on an unknown key, a selector inferred from parameter order
Every string key is declaredBuild stages are STAGE_DEF ids, sources SOURCE_DEF, messages MSG_DEF; sequence facts are typed vars on the context; prompt and request payloads are typed vars set by named args; UI args arrive as typed proc parameters. An unresolved identifier is a build error and no key is computed.Free-text keys resolved at runtime
Typed actionsACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on) is a generator marker producing /datum/act/fall, act_fall(...) and the notice /datum/notice/fell, always in the past tense. Call sites use ACT_TRY (the final act, after every adjusts(); the caller executes with its fields through ACT_FINAL), ACT_PASS (a shared constant, nothing allocated, returned only when nothing hooks the action and no listener wants its notice), act_done, act_cancel; a lint requires one of the last two on every path. The op action's notice is /datum/notice/op_done; ACTION(move, ...) has origin, destination, direction; ACTION(insert, atom/movable/item, ...) takes any movable. Every action has one outcome (ACT_COMMITTED, ACT_REFUSED, ACT_REPLACED).Hand-written act and notice pairs; slashed, ui_opened and other hand-named notices; executing from locals after hooks changed the act
Defining a capabilityCAPABILITY_DEF(name, CAP_X, params...) for the function form and CAPABILITY_TYPE(name, CAP_X, /datum/capability/x, params...) for the datum form. The params are the settings, configure(CAP_X, p = v) re-runs the body, and an unknown param is a build error. Bundles are plain procs returning entries; wrap one in a capability when something must address it. There is one definition of powered, a capability over powered_by(system, role).capability(KEY, settings...) with free settings, two definitions of powered, type procs as bundles
One composition root per typeOne CAPABILITIES(T) block; a second on the same type is a build error. A long block is split into section(name, "doc")s of the same block (the entries stay the type's own: PROC_REF, nameof(v)); shared snippets are capabilities or plain procs returning entries. The table builder records __FILE__:__LINE__ per entry in every build.capabilities(), reactions(), relations(), derived(), CAPABILITY() (the docs' "three table procs" rule); lists merged by include order; origin lists as debug-only data
Capability datacap_data(A) returns the typed /datum/cap_data/<cap> (RETURN_TYPE) of A.activation: one per activation, not one per holder. A field a condition reads is a declared tracked field. A type-level capability allocates its activation lazily, on the first state write or cap_data read. Accessors and cap_of address the type-level activation; a granted capability with state declares a selector.cap_data(holder, KEY)["x"], per-holder capability data, an activation allocated per instance for every type-level capability
Grant valuesgranted_trait(), granted_language(), granted_access(), granted_verb(), hidden_verb(); a granted verb is grant(E, granted_verb(path), source).Separate grant stores per kind
Busy stateAn actor's busy state is its pending op (busy policy BUSY_REPLACE or BUSY_REFUSE); there is no base busy stat. A thing occupied for a while (a slot machine spinning) declares a stat named for that state and holds it with lasts =.om_hold_busy, om_in_use, a shared STAT_BUSY

State, relations and lifecycle

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Two state conceptsTRACKED(T, a, b, c) (variadic: settings and plain state) and STAT (composed, including FORMULA). Capability state is cap_keys(). TRACKED_BRIDGED survives only as the build-time shim table of section 19, until the machine and Life pipelines go.3-argument TRACKED, setting(nameof(v), choices =), from =, derive(), OM_FIELD and its relatives
Declaring links and ownershipFour relation entries in CAPABILITIES: ref_one/ref_many (a reference, with on_other_deleted = CLEAR | DELETE_ME), owns_one/owns_many (on_destroy = DELETE | SPILL | PRIVATE_COPY, copy_on_write =), links(a::x, b::y) (paired, declared once; hot = TRUE for the engine's hot paths), and slot(...). Writes are rel_set, rel_add, rel_remove, rel_clear, rel_move, rel_take (the last two pick a member with member = or key =), dispatching on the declared kind; own_* and proto_* merge into them. A copy-on-write var holds a shared prototype until rel_private(E, nameof(v)) returns the holder's private copy, made on the first call; rel_is_private asks.ownership(), owns/shares/proto, the 16 OWN/REL/PROTO macros, the ten-parameter rel_one, the ten own_* writers, OWN_SPILL/OWN_CONTAINED
Slot declarationsslot(id, accepts =, fits =, capacity =, exposure =, at = SPACE_X, on_destroy =) over the containment ledger./datum/slot_def, /datum/om/relation/slot, SLOT_DROP_HOLDER
Moving an item between slotsslot_transfer(item, to_holder, slot_id, actor =, authority =) always runs the remove and insert actions and their hooks. A forced transfer needs an authority datum (authority = A).A transfer as one ledger write that bypasses the actions and their requirements
Lasting effects while something is in a slotwhile_slotted(SLOT_X, entries..., on = ON_CONTENTS | ON_HOLDER) grants the entries to the contained item (or to the holder), sourced by the occupying item for ON_HOLDER and by the slot entry for ON_CONTENTS, released on exit, delete or holder delete. The slot owner declares ON_CONTENTS (stasis beds, bellies, cryo, borg docks); the item declares ON_HOLDER (worn shields and mirrors).Loose per-stat holds placed by the effect's owner and released by hand (the belly's two prey holds), a buckle notice pair for stasis
Per-instance listsLIST_STATE(T, var, alloc = LAZY | EAGER, kind = LIST | SET | MAP, owns = NONE | ROWS, share = NONE | COW_TABLE) declares a per-instance list with four independent choices, each with a default; the instance-list lint accepts declared ones.ALLOW(instance_list) (480 annotations), the single policy word LAZY | ROWS | SET | TABLE
