Initial contentsstarts = T | list(T...) | pick_one(list) | PROC_REF(x) | when(cond, T), starts_args = list(...), and the capability hook on_holder_preinit(A) for work that must precede the parent's init.ALLOW(decl) (128), starts_from and starts_at
Writing a var by nameTrait var_changes become contributes() and may name only stats. vars_write(E, name, value, source = SRC_VV) is the one primitive for VV, SDQL and serialization.Raw vars[name] = x writers (about 50 ALLOW(api))
Entities carried in dataA generation-checked handle: ref_token(E) and resolve_token(token).Raw references or REF() text in player-copyable data
Initial evaluationInitial stat evaluation is silent (no notices, no hooks) until map load completes.A notice storm at boot
Rust-owned factsRead as native("key"). DM-owned settings are tracked vars pushed by the push_to_rust() output with native_write().Generated per-field get/set procs (rust_bindings.md sections 0 and 3); update_rust_device()
DM timers and keys in RustKernel only. The Rust world scheduler keeps native watches and rate models. The work is E6's, not phase 0's.om_world_at, DM-owned keys published into Rust, the reactor

Execution and hooks

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Execution modelWrites are immediate and mark their dependents. Stats on the written entity and one single-valued edge away recompute inline; every other stat the write reaches is marked and recomputed at the next marked drain (the start of D, P or R) under the budget of its lane, and what does not finish resumes at the next marked drain, which may be later in the same tick. Hook attachment, on_change hooks and outputs drain at the end of the current trigger and at the start of phases D, P, S and R, and on_change runs at the drain with the keys that changed. Ranks come from the generated reads graph (tracked vars, stats, conditions, hooks, outputs); a cycle in the condition and stat graph is a build error, and an iteration cap per drain logs the key chain and spills the rest to the next drain point. Phase S pushes to Rust before the native step N; phase R runs only presentation outputs. The phase order K, S, N, U, D, P, R, G is deliberate: input resolved in K reaches the same frame's native step, and work scheduled in D and P reaches it the next frame. Notices deliver synchronously at act_done in publish order, to the listeners of the action's outcome, to a depth of 8: a nested notice beyond it is queued to the next drain point (late, out of order) and a nested action beyond it ends ACT_REFUSED.Unspecified ordering between recompute, hooks and outputs; push_to_rust() in the presentation phase; dropping the notice past depth 8; queueing a nested action
When conditions evaluateConditions used for matching (when() on ops, look_layer(when =), menus) are evaluated when read, at resolution or output time. Conditions that drive transitions (entry-level when() attaching and detaching hooks, observes and outputs, every(when =) start and stop, on_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT) edges, while_slotted) are evaluated at the drain. An op under when() is not attached or detached: its applicability is read at resolution.One undefined rule for both kinds; ops in the drain list
ContextsPooled /datum/act in five narrow types (op, eval, notice, timer, request), each hook form naming its type and the build checking field reads against it; never stored in vars or passed in with = (lint); A.snapshot() returns a plain list; A.args carries typed UI and topic arguments and after(..., with =) payloads (scalars and flyweights only); the kernel wraps each handler and releases its context on a runtime; a production watchdog frees contexts held over 2 minutes with no pending op and logs the creator. A condition has no actor unless it runs inside an op, where it gets the op's context.op_ctx, guard_ctx, stored contexts, one context with many conditionally valid fields
Hook formsRefuse: extend(target, needs(...)). Take over: extend(/datum/act/x, instead(parts..., order = ORDER_EARLY | ORDER_NORMAL | ORDER_LATE)) (chance() inside means "takes over with probability p"; the action ends ACT_REPLACED and its notice goes only to listeners that asked for it). Modify in flight: extend(/datum/act/hit, adjusts(field, by =, scale =, when =)). React: on_notice(..., outcome =), on_op, on_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT) for a condition's edges, on_change(key, ANY) for a key's change (numeric keys only with ANY). Unrefusable: ACTION(x, ..., FIXED) plus PUBLISH. Another entity listens: observe(source, /datum/notice/x, listener, parts...), whose handler runs on the listener (A.holder is the listener, A.target the observed entity).intercept(), NOTICE(), DAMAGE_REACTION, power_change overrides, guard/guarded, a "replaced" flag on the ordinary notice
on_change shapeson_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT, ...) takes a boolean condition and fires on its edges. on_change(key, ANY, ...) takes a key and fires when its value changed. A numeric key is allowed only with ANY; ENTER or EXIT on one is a build error.A numeric key treated as an ANY condition; ANY on a condition
Hook candidates and orderA target's type table plus every capability granted to it (grants, while_slotted, species, traits), with the same precedence. Order is order =, then runs_after = CAP_X, then declaration order. Notice names come from the action in the past tense (inserted, buckled, unbuckled, fell, crossed, uncrossed, removed); the world actions include unbuckle, uncross, remove.Hooks that see only the base type table
Event coalescingNotices never coalesce. "Facts changed" is a stat or derived value.The coalescing material_facts_changed event
Mob Life target/datum/sequence/life with seq_step procs, compute_facts() on the frame and run_step_now(E, PROC_REF(step))./datum/system/life with step(mob, dt) (migration guide B30, kernel.md section 3.5); seq_condition, seq_replan, seq_plan_key
Machine pipeline targetPer-machine every(when =), stats and membership.Per-power-mode member_step variants on the power system

Actions, ops and verbs

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Op grammarFour stages: Match (silent, when), Require (loud, needs, plus the pre-checks of the actions the effects start), Wait (the timed wait and every asks() and confirms(), in declaration order, as the op's workflow), Do. Costs are reserved after the last answer. Do is a commit: chance() rolls first (a failed roll commits the costs, runs no effect, ends ACT_COMMITTED with A.rolled = FALSE and publishes no op_done unless a listener opted in with ACT_ROLL_FAILED or ACT_ANY); effects run in order, each reporting OP_OK, OP_REFUSED, OP_REPLACED or OP_FAILED (a proc that returns nothing is OP_OK, and act_outcome_to_op(F) carries a world action's outcome); then commit or release; then feedback and notice only if committed with a successful roll. asks() is a workflow step in Wait and never a part of the effect list: an effect written above one is a build error, and cancelling costs nothing. confirms("text") is asks(/datum/prompt/yes_no, question = "text"), and captures(nameof(v), ...) names the fields a confirm-only op must capture.Hand-written guards that decide between hiding and refusing (applies_to, available_on, else_say, works_*); spending before the effect that might refuse; asks() as a suspension point in the Do list
Ties and priorityTarget, then held, then actor; a subtype before its parent; then capability declaration order. Tiers, high to low: SUBVERT, CLAW, TAKE_OUT, ATTACK, PART, NORMAL, DEFAULT (tool() yields PART). Relative: priority(above("cover.open")), priority(below(CAP_LOCK)). Two ops on one target with the same input, intent and tier are a build error, unless their when parts are mutually exclusive. The claw swipe is a species or mob op that yields CLAW.A silent "first declared wins"; a claws() capability
One input tableFive separate facts, one table: ORIGIN_* (CLICK, MENU, UI, VERB, AI, HOTKEY, SYSTEM; ORIGIN_ALL, ORIGIN_NONE), REACH_* policies an op names (ADJACENT, RANGE(n), INSIDE, VIEW, ANY), providers with AFF_* (MANIPULATE, HOLD, HOLD_SMALL, ATTACK, CONTROL, OBSERVE, TOOL_<Q>), AUTH_* (PHYSICAL, REMOTE_ACCESS, ADMIN, AI) and the channel (the type that declares the op), with INTENT_* and GESTURE_*. Bindings give the usual combinations: hand(), tool(), item(), inside() (each accepting ORIGIN_CLICK | ORIGIN_MENU | ORIGIN_AI), remote(), ui_act(), menu(), ai(). perform_op() by key runs through whichever binding accepts the origin it is given, ORIGIN_AI by default, which every physical binding and ai() accept. Reach lives on the provider, in tiles: provides(AFF_X, reach =, line_of_sight =, authority =, accepts =); REACH_ANY is never a provider value. There is no hand provider on /mob/living: a species grants hands(), and a mob with no species declares its own providers (a carp's bite, a borg's modules, the AI's remote control); the provider that performs an op is the held item's, then the hand, then any other by shortest reach. origin(), reach(), by() and authority() replace what a binding implies; stance() and hostile() are sugar.The ROUTE_* mask, via(), AFF_INTERFACE, AFF_TK, a range on at_target(), the defaults that lived in a code comment, the context menu as part of ORIGIN_VERB, reach = REACH_ANY on a provider
Waitswait(t, keeps = WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT) (HELD | ADJACENT | TARGET_PRESENT | ALIVE), and the same keeps = on asks(). A read change re-runs requirements and cancels only if one now refuses. The pending op is a kernel record related to actor, target and held through weak relations: deleting any of them before the reservation cancels it with feedback, and from the reservation the act holds them strongly. any number of pending ops per actor, exclusive only where claims() overlap (a timed physical wait holds hands and body; a question holds nothing); a conflicting input is BUSY_REPLACE for players ("You stop what you were doing."), BUSY_REFUSE for AI; at most OP_PENDING_CAP per actor. Within Wait, wait() and each asks() or confirms() run in the order the op declares them.om_task_*, om_busy, strong references held across a prompt
Costs and chainingcosts(RES_X, n), consumes() and cooldown(t) are reservations against resource adapters (X2: holder_of, available, reserve, commit, release, over a /datum/reservation; RES_ITEM and RES_COOLDOWN cover the last two): reserved after the last answer, committed after the effects succeed, released on refusal or failure. A failed commit is OP_FAILED with a log line. An op's own costs() replaces its tool profile's (write costs(RES_FUEL, +1) to add). passes() lets later candidates run after it commits; their refusals are silent. toggles_hold(STAT_X, value, source =) is an op part. Sharing only effects between ops is the explicit, rare shares_effects(key) (no Match, no Wait, none of its costs, may not asks()).Four cost parts; manual re-dispatch; performs(key) as the standard way to give an op another input
Gameplay verbsGameplay abilities are ops with menu(button = ICON_STATE, bind = "shift+f"), listed under one "Abilities" verb-panel entry with action buttons and keybinds. verb_entry(), ADMIN_VERB and DEBUG_VERB are only for client, OOC and admin commands; the layering lint forbids verb_entry under code/content. IC verbs are sorted by the A8 classifier: gameplay ones become Abilities ops, only real client commands keep verb_entry.DECLARE_VERB for gameplay abilities, per-ability verb panels
Emag defaultemag(parts...) is one-shot: gated on the holder not being subverted already, as DECLARE_EMAG is today. emag(..., repeatable = TRUE) is the repeatable form (today's DECLARE_EMAG_REPEATABLE). No codemod has to add a gate, so a missed site cannot become silently repeatable.An ungated default that every one-shot site must tighten with extend(CAP_EMAG, needs(req_not_subverted()))
AbilitiesActor ops in granted capabilities./datum/ability (rules.md section 5)
Override surfaceEvery entry takes an optional key =; extend, without and configure accept any entry key (configure("scan", interval = ...) retunes an inherited keyed entry); extend("x", drop = "id") relaxes a requirement; extend("x", replaces(parts...)) swaps an effect list.refine, replace, unnamed entries
Explain toolingexplain_click(actor, target, held, gesture) (admin verb "Explain Interaction"), perform_op(actor, target, key, held, authority =, trace =), explain_type(/type) (also offline, as an analysis query), explain_activations(E) and the pending-op list are admin verbs in production, and assert_resolves is the test helper. Origin lists are kept in every build, at a file and a line per interned entry. An empty resolution shows the best near-miss's reason, rate-limited. Build errors are tagged with their tier: DM compile, generator, boot, test."Why was this refused?" as a separate debug verb; emag_target as a special form; origin lists as debug-only data
Resolution costTwo passes. Pass 1 uses an interned index by (intent, held type) and the cheap gates, running no requirement code. Pass 2 runs requirements for the winner only; menus and screentips evaluate lazily (at_most = 0.2 SECONDS) behind a cache keyed by (actor, target, held, the act generation of each, the actor's provider set generation); the reach walk is cached per (actor, provider set generation, target).Evaluating every candidate's requirements on every click; a "reach stat" in the cache key; a key of generation counters with no entity in it; a reach walk cached per containment-ledger version

Look, UI, prompts and requests

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
UI forminterface(window, title =, rights =, host =, input = hand()) (declaring it adds the open op, bound to the input and to remote()), the ui_data(datum/act/A) output, and a declared ui_shape(operating, channels = list_of(row(...))) whose fields take schemas, from which the TypeScript types are generated, UI ops and their typed args included. Test builds validate pushed data against the shape. Window access (range, conscious, rights) is a requirement with generated reads. The tgui transport keeps a window pool, prewarm and assets, and has no polling.DECLARE_UI/UI_ACT/UI_DATA (AGENTS.md), ui_window() plus interface() plus auto-open, the polling fire() and update_uis INVOKE_ASYNC, the act_<x>() naming convention (migration guide A7), the UI_BOOL family of shape words
PromptsKinds: text, number, list, checklist, yes_no (it absorbs confirm and alert), color, colormatrix, mob, typepath, bitfield, file, icon, form, all answering in a uniform value field whose validation is a schema. asks() inside a trigger; notify(user, text, title =) is an info popup with no answer. One open prompt per answerer per op: asking again focuses it. Stale windows close themselves, with the reason, when a requirement the prompt reads now refuses. UI arg(name, schema) is the same validator.Eight *_ask variants, ask_text/ask_number as separate procs, raw input(), alert() and tgui_input_*, arg_* as a second validator
One request concept/datum/request is a workflow step (X3), with kinds /datum/prompt/* (player answers), /datum/io/sql | http | rustg_job | path (backends) and /datum/client_query/winget | measure_text | .... They share typed fields with schemas, weak handles, a timeout, re-check on answer, cancel when the owner dies, one outcome enum and metrics. Inside a trigger: asks(/datum/request/x, field = v..., step =, resume =); outside: request(owner, /datum/request/x, then(PROC_REF(done)), valid =, field = v...); prompts take answerer =. await() stays kernel and request-layer only. request_urgent() is renamed kernel_urgent(), so "request" means only /datum/request.io_request, ask(answerer, ...) outside triggers, request_urgent(), a null result field that meant several outcomes
Look callslook.variant/layer/glow/level plus look.identity and look.composite, and look_layer entries, after the global icon rename. Phase 3 step C2 rewrites the calls.look.state/overlay/gauge/light (kept only until the rename finishes)
Chunked jobs and pathsjob(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =): the step returns JOB_MORE or JOB_DONE, and budget = is a percent of a tick (map loader, site generation, POI queue, zone cleanup). /datum/io/path is served by a budgeted path system.stoplag and INVOKE_ASYNC in those four, the pathfinder mutex
Faultssafe_call(PROC_REF(x), ...) returns a /datum/result (ok, value, error); try_parse_json(text); EXPECT_RUNTIME in tests. Raw try/catch only inside the kernel.65 ALLOW(silent_catch)
Body effectsCapabilities granted with a duration.The separate body-effect store

Kernel, time and I/O

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Host systemsDatabase, tgui transport, garbage, ticker, verdigris, lighting, input and the rest are ordinary systems: SYSTEM_DEF with phase = KERNEL_PHASE_K (air runs in phase N) and latency_class = LATENCY_L0. "No subsystems" is now true, and the MC_* metric defines become KERNEL_*.SUBSYSTEM_DEF, fire(), the Master Controller, SS_KERNEL_HOSTED, the MC_* metric names
Systems' membersA system declares roles = list(...); join(system, E, source, role) and leave(system, E, source, role); every(members =) sweeps members in systems only, the member being A.target.System roster vars, a role-less join
InputOne typed inbox for every client entry point (click and modifiers, drag, Topic, verb, tgui act, say and emote, held keys). An event resolves on the spot if budget remains and queues otherwise; phase K drains round-robin across clients under the input budget, and each client's inbox is bounded (INPUT_CLIENT_MAX, default 64) with repeatable inputs coalesced: over the cap the oldest coalescible input is dropped and logged, and admin and say input never is. Each event resolves through the action path at drain time, and a stale target is dropped with the gate reason. The inbox owns client/Topic, and the tick meter's per-input wait metrics move to it.SSinput, SSverb_manager, the speech lane, the click holdback, opt-in queue macros and callbacks, an unbounded "never shed" queue
Databasesystem(/datum/system/db) owns the connection, round records and schema check, with /datum/io/sql as the backend. Content uses only asks() or request() with /datum/io/sql/*, and sql_write(). A kind declares row_type = /datum/io/sql/row/x and its rows is a list of those typed row datums. db_query_now() is allowed only in world/IsBanned and the database system's boot step (schema check, admin and rank tables), by lint with no ALLOW. Login lookups are async, and the client is "ready" when its data arrives.NewQuery()/Execute(), synchronous Execute in handlers (49 sites)
SQL result rowsA kind declares row_type = /datum/io/sql/row/x (typed vars for the result columns), and the answer's rows is a list of those row datums.Untyped per-row lists; result columns copied into the request's own vars
Time formsFive: after(), every(), hold(lasts =), COOLDOWN (with COOLDOWN_KEYED_DECLARE/_START/_FINISHED for dynamic keys), STAMP/ELAPSED. Section 3 lists the 14 old spellings and what each became. every(interval, parts..., when =, lane =, key =) plus runs_after = for ordering. expire(N) is the lifecycle verb.The fourteen spellings; every() knobs beyond those
Repeating workevery(interval, parts..., when =)periodic_cadence/periodic_step/should_run, DECLARE_PERIODIC_WHILE, DECLARE_REPEAT, member_should_run/member_step, the OM behaviour every var
The one timerafter(owner, delay, parts..., key =, clock =, with =); delayed(t, parts...) is its holder-clock form inside an op.rx_after as public API, after_slot, OWN_TIMER, varargs after
Timed stateHolds. COOLDOWN_* for rate limits. STAMP/ELAPSED for "how long since".timed_set, EXPIRY_* (the two lints that contradict each other both retire), om_grant_for
Boot-phase I/O and vendored codeBlocking I/O is allowed only before the first tick and is named as such. Vendored code lives in code/host/vendor/, exempt from lints.ALLOW(scheduler) in modules/tgs

Tooling and exemptions

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Build-time analysisOne analysis engine, tools/analyze/ in Rust, built in phase 0b: one parallel load of the tree, a content-hash cache for incremental runs, comments and strings stripped once, text and parse-aware rules on SpacemanDMM's dreammaker parser behind a backend trait, and ALLOW, baselines and exemptions handled once. Every existing lint is ported to it, legacy-form ratchets included, each switched over only when a parity harness shows identical findings to its script on master. Phase 1 starts once the engine core and the slowest lints are ported; the other ports continue alongside it; check_ratchets.sh and check_grep.sh become wrappers, then go. Phase 1's E5 adds the semantic queries to the same engine: generated reads, key resolution, handler-signature checks, layering, duplicate definitions, the compiled-table diff, context escape, ACT_TRY pairing, purity, the shim registry, the .dme generator and the ACTION/CAPABILITY_DEF/STAT/ui_shape generators. One diagnostics format (file:line: [rule] message -- hint, tagged with its tier) and one incremental daemon shared by agent worktrees. The incremental DM compiler's front end replaces dreammaker behind the trait once it passes the phase-1 readiness gate; it analyses only, and dm.exe still produces the .dmb. TGUI, Rust, the icon pipeline and the Verdigris bindings keep their toolchains.About 40 Python lints and check_grep.sh each re-reading the tree (about 495 s and 146 s a run), DreamChecker rules and separate generators (DreamChecker stays as a second opinion until the engine covers its rules); "legacy ratchets stay Python until zero"
ExemptionsOne mechanism: directory scopes in tools/ci/lint_scopes.toml (unit tests, benchmarks, vendored code), ALLOW(lint/CODE) reason codes, a minimum reason quality, an exact unused-ALLOW check, and per-lint ceilings, all enforced from phase 0b.ALLOW(base_vars), // INIT:, ALLOW(init), the three check_grep.sh allowlists
Reason codes and ceilingsEnforced by the engine from phase 0b, not 2.5: the closed code list per lint and the per-lint ceilings switch on with the engine core, and a lint's existing annotations are re-coded as it is ported.Deferring them to phase 2.5 while the engine already exists
The 0b gate for phase 1Phase 1 starts once the analysis engine core (load, cache, parser backend, ALLOW, baselines, exemptions) and the slowest lints are ported; the remaining lint ports continue alongside phase 1 and finish before phase 2 opens.Every lint at parity as a precondition of phase 1
Test primitivesTEST_RAW_SET, await_ticks, assert_resolves, the test driver (test_click, test_ui, test_menu, test_answer, test_time, test_drain, test_phase, perform_op returning a /datum/op_result, test_logs, test_rng, the notice and spill counters, test_budget, and the recorder test_record and test_recorded), the resolver-agnostic behaviour snapshot harness (built on that recorder) (messages, log lines, tracked deltas, inventory transfers, resource commits and releases, activations, notices with outcomes, pending work and delayed outcomes) and the ten E0 proofs.Interaction-id snapshots, "unchanged snapshots" as a gate; E0 proofs written with only assert_resolves
Examples as testsThe examples of section 16, and the reference snippets marked complete, compile under DOC_SNIPPETS from phase 2; a snippet marked as an excerpt does not compile on its own, and a form not defined in sections 1 to 15 fails the build."Design, not compiled" examples
MappingSection 17, "Old to new", is curated (rows with at least 20 uses or any content-facing semantic change, plus a "switches on in phase N" column); the full generated table is doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv. Section 18 is 10 to 15 lines grouped by family, with the rule that an om_* form not listed is internal.One 897-row table and its copy

Plan and layout

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Conversion strategyBranch triage, a clean base and the analysis engine first; engines; then the library on the new forms; then codemod infrastructure; then mechanical codemods tree-wide per form in freeze windows; then waves that finish every remaining form and move their files; then delete the legacy layer. Old and new forms coexist only through the registered shim set (section 19), each shim with an owner step and a lint that fails if it outlives it."Migrate no callers" vs "no shims" vs "one folder at a time" (all three retire); "wrappers exist only while callers remain" as the only rule
Phases and track namesPhases 0, 0b, 1, 1S, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7; steps A1 to C2 inside phase 3; waves W01 to W37 and move waves M01 to M12. The retired track names (S1 to S11, S0 to S4, A1 to A16, W1 to W6) are replaced by descriptions or commit refs.The overloaded track names
What gates phase 1Small fixtures built from test-only capabilities, one per engine, plus the opening gates (name spike, analysis readiness, E0 contracts). The library examples gate phase 2."The APC, turret, airlock and beaker built exactly as shown" as a phase-1 gate (it needed phase 2's library)
Gates are numbersA pinned baseline commit per phase, a noise floor from A/A pairs and bench-compare's significance test, numeric budgets, a residue integer per form, and eight new bench scenarios added before phase 1."Within 2% of master", "within budget", "residue" undefined, a baseline that moves
Open branchesA triage table in phase 0b: land, rebase after phase N, or abandon, one row per branch with commits ahead.Salvage by memory
AGENTS.mdTwo steps. Phase 0b: "write what exists on master today", a legacy form being banned only when its replacement has landed and its retirement lint is on. Phase 2: the full rewrite to the new forms, with an "on master" column.One rewrite in phase 0 that told agents to write forms that did not yet compile
LayoutCompile order and dependency direction are separate. Compile order: defines, contracts, host, base, then the legacy layer, engine, library, domains, content, world, with code/tests/ outside the order. Dependency direction is a module graph checked by the module_deps lint, not one ladder; library, domains and content depend on contracts, not each other's internals; content depends on domains, never the reverse; adapters live where both sides are visible. Base stat declarations live in code/base. A generated .dme; a duplicate-definition lint and a compiled-table diff gate every move; the ~3,900 files with no legacy form move in waves M01 to M12; the completion test is "legacy-form lints at zero and the move table complete".Six folders with no home for helpers, base types, tgui, admin or tests; one strict ladder of layers; "old directories empty" as the completion test; "a pure git mv changes nothing"
DocsThis document is doc/rewrite/final_api.html, and becomes doc/rewrite/api.md plus chapters at phase 7. AGENTS.md follows the two steps above. Archived OM docs and stale chapter sections are deleted at phase 7.Status markers that disagree, and "archived but still describes the code" pointers

Semantic contracts (round 3)

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
R1 Consistency: immediate state against deferred workImmediate: stats on the written entity and one single-valued relation edge away (including condition-gated contributions and while_slotted stat entries), permissions, containment and capability keys, and native-read vars on the written entity. Marked, then recomputed at the next marked drain under budget and resumed at the following one if unfinished: every other stat the write reaches (collection or membership edges, a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, two or more edges, a batched release). The build derives which from the relation declarations. Deferred to the drain: hook attachment and detachment, observes and outputs granted under a condition, on_change, outputs, cleanup; an op under when() is read at resolution. Two exceptions to "ends with its source": a timed hold on another entity outlives it, and a hold an activation's op places on its own holder (or a slot-sourced hold) is bound even when timed. E0 proof 8 measures marked fan-out and may promote membership edges to inline.An unqualified freshness promise for stats; pull-on-read for native-read vars; "the one exception"; a "shared source" criterion
R2 Op commit and failure contractDo is a commit: Require and the pre-checks of the actions the effects start run before irreversible work (then(..., checks =); the library effect parts declare theirs); every asks() runs in Wait, before the reservation, and an effect before an asks() is a build error; resources are reserved after the last answer, committed after the effects, released on refusal or failure; chance() rolls after the reservation; each effect reports OP_OK, OP_REFUSED, OP_REPLACED or OP_FAILED; the outcome is the first non-OK effect, else committed; only a committed op with a successful roll publishes on_op, success messages and fingerprints; there is no general rollback; compound atomic mutations are single engine verbs. The act holds its holder, target, held item and actor weakly until the reservation (deleting one cancels the op) and strongly from it, so consuming or replacing them never cancels the op, and feedback reads snapshot fields."Effects never refuse"; spending before the effect; a runtime that left the outcome undefined; asks() inside the effect list; strong references held across a prompt
R3 Input bindings replace op-borrowingAn op is a semantic definition and its inputs are bindings: op(key, inputs(hand(), ui_act()), ...) or extend(key, binds(ui_act())). Every binding runs the same Match, Require, Wait and Do. The old way of giving an op another input is off the public surface; sharing only effects is the explicit, rare shares_effects(key) (no Match, no Wait, none of its costs, may not asks(), one notice). ai() is written only for an op with no physical binding.performs(key), a second op that borrows the first's effects as the way to add an input
R4 Capability definition, activation and contributionsDefinition (immutable, interned), activation (one per definition key, source and holder, with params, per-activation state, scope and its contributions; a type-level capability's is created lazily and an entry-level when() is a bit in a per-holder mask), contributions. stacks = STACK | UNIQUE | BEST(param) on the definition covers behaviour (hooks, rolls, ops, periodic work); stat contributions always combine through the stat's rule. Reflection is BEST(chance): one roll, A.source the winning mirror. Removing an activation marks it dead at once (dispatch skips it), removes its contributions, runs on_deactivate and ends what it owns unless outlives = TRUE; an activation that ends itself from its own handler lets the handler finish first. Attach validates before it applies, and grant() returns the activation or null with the reason logged. Identity is explicit: key = names the selector or NONE; accessors address the type-level activation, and a granted capability with state declares a selector.A discriminator inferred from the first required param; per-holder capability data; an op source as owner of an effect that must die with the capability; an activation allocated per instance
R5 Requests: snapshot policy and outcomesasks(..., resume = CAPTURE | LATEST | CANCEL_IF_CHANGED, keeps =), default CAPTURE and WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT: the fields the op declares (those a field arg of an asks() names, and those a captures(nameof(v), ...) part lists, which a confirm-only op needs) are snapshotted when it first suspends, and Do reads the snapshot through A.captured(), which a lint requires of any then() that reads a captured field of either kind. A holder's revision (for CANCEL_IF_CHANGED with no field) is a per-entity counter bumped by any published key. Reach while waiting is a keep. Outcomes are one typed enum (REQ_ANSWERED, REQ_CANCELLED, REQ_TIMED_OUT, REQ_TRANSPORT_FAILED, REQ_NO_RESULT; REQ_FAILED with the error for a backend), read as A.request.outcome. request() outside an op takes valid = PROC_REF(x), re-checked on answer.A selection read again after the confirmation; a null result field meaning several things; a handler reading a captured field live
R6 Routes split into five partsOrigin (ORIGIN_*, with ORIGIN_MENU for the context menu and radial), reach policy (REACH_*), provider, authority (AUTH_*) and channel. For REACH_ADJACENT the reach gate collects the candidate providers first, takes the largest reach among them, then each op checks its own provider, so a spear's reach of 2 is honoured; REACH_RANGE(n) and REACH_VIEW use the op's own range or the actor's view. acts_via is a mask of origins. Acting on your container is reach(REACH_INSIDE) (inside()); tool bindings need AFF_TOOL_<Q>, which a module declares; menu ops are the actor's own and object verbs migrate to the target's menu() entries.One ROUTE_* mask mixing transport, reach and authority; an early adjacency gate that rejected a provider's extended reach; a welder module that needed AFF_MANIPULATE; the context menu as an origin the physical bindings refused
R7 Reactive graph contractGenerated reads are a static over-approximation (every read in a proc subscribes). A runtime link or grant that would make a cycle is refused when the edge is added, with an error and the chain. A formula over a collection subscribes to its membership revision. Capability data read by a condition is a declared tracked field. Provider selection, Match, menus and waits sit on the same graph, and the reach cache key is (the actor, the actor's provider set generation, the target), with cover state part of reach. System state is private and read through SYSTEM_ACCESSOR accessors, declared in contracts. A displayed cooldown(t) schedules an invalidation at expiry."An APC in always mode does not read night"; SSnightshift.night read from content; hand-written cache resets; a reach stat in the cache key
R8 Native sync and budgetsPhase S (simulation sync) runs push_to_rust() immediately before the native step N, is never shed (L0), and leaves phase R presentation only; an input in K reaches the same frame's native step for effects settled inline. Marked fan-out is drained under budget in the marked drains (the start of D, P and R), resumes at the next marked drain and logs the key chain if it spills. Lane budgets are microseconds of tick time per tick (a job() budget stays a percent). The input inbox is bounded per client (INPUT_CLIENT_MAX, 64), coalesces repeatable inputs, rotates fairly across clients and never drops admin or say input.push_to_rust() in the presentation phase; an unbounded input queue; a spill target that changes by section
R9 Action context and outcomesACT_TRY returns the final act and the caller executes with its fields (ACT_FINAL). Every action ends ACT_COMMITTED, ACT_REFUSED or ACT_REPLACED; on_notice defaults to committed and outcome = ACT_REPLACED | ACT_ANY opts in. ACT_MAX_DEPTH stays 8 for synchronous nesting: a nested notice beyond it is queued to the next drain point (delivered late and out of publish order), logged, and fails a test build; a nested action beyond it ends ACT_REFUSED ("too deeply nested"), logs the chain and fails test builds, because a queued action would have no result to hand back.Executing from locals; a past-tense notice for a replaced action; dropping the ninth notice; queueing a nested action
R10 LayersCompile order and dependency direction are separate. A contracts layer (ids, act types, defines, accessor declarations: never procs) sits low in compile order; a proc stays in its own module, since DM resolves procs across the whole tree whatever the file order. Dependencies are a module graph checked by module_deps. Content depends on domains, never the reverse; a domain owns a reusable model and content is the concrete things using it, so a folder may split into <x>/model and <x>/content. Adapters live where both sides are visible (powered() is in the power domain).One strict ladder of layers; powered() in the library naming the power system; capability signatures or forward declarations in contracts
Sections and the composition rootOne compiled declaration table per type stays; a long CAPABILITIES block is split into section(name, "doc")s, which Explain prints with each entry's file:line. The named BUNDLE marker was dropped: a global list of one type's entries needed TYPE_PROC_REF and put one-type names in the global namespace.BUNDLE(name); "one file per type" as a rule
Narrow contexts/datum/act/op, eval, notice, timer and request. The handler stays x(datum/act/A), each hook form names its type, and the build checks field reads against it. A condition used in an op's Match or Require gets the op's context; elsewhere it gets eval, which has no actor.One context with many conditionally valid fields; "conditions have no actor" beside actor requirements in when()
Explain tools in productionOrigin lists are kept in every build (a file and a line per entry); explain_click, explain_type, explain_activations and the pending-op list are admin verbs.Origin lists as test-build data
REQ_IN_INVENTORYcarried(): held, worn or in a container the actor carries, not in_hand(), so no behaviour narrows (186 sites).Mapping it to in_hand()
The insert action's participantACTION(insert, atom/movable/item, ...): any movable, since slots hold mobs.obj/item/item as the participant
A.target's typeA.target is a datum. An op that needs an atom declares target_type = /atom, Match checks it, and the handler reads the typed A.target_atom.A.target typed as an atom
SRC_HELD_ITEM's lifetimeIt lasts while the item is in that hand or slot, and ends separately when the item is deleted."Ends when the item leaves", conflating leaving with deletion
Schema identifiers against runtime dataKeys, stat ids, op keys, sources and stages are schema: declared, checked by the build. Keyed cooldown keys, vars_write names, keyed-relation ids and runtime capability selectors are data: validated on arrival."Every key is declared" beside undeclared dynamic keys

The five foundations (round 3)

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
X1 Scoped activationEverything applied to a holder for a while is an activation: grant(), entry-level when(), while_slotted, observe(), memberships and join(), species capabilities, worn and held item effects, timed holds and timed grants. One record (definition, source, holder, params, state, contributions, scope, stacking), one attach path (build and validate, then apply: a failure applies nothing and grant() returns null), one teardown path (end owned dependents, remove contributions, run on_deactivate); detach marks the record dead at once. Type-level entries allocate lazily. Scope changes are tracked graph edges (a species change is a write to the species relation); explain_activations(E) lists them.Per-mechanism lifetime rules and release code
X2 Resource transactionsRESOURCE_DEF(RES_X, adapter =, schema =) with holder_of, available, reserve, commit and release over a /datum/reservation(resource, holder, amount, op). costs(), consumes() (RES_ITEM), cooldown() (RES_COOLDOWN), fuel, charge, stacks, blood, dark energy, reagent transfers, slot and storage capacity and the state-graph ledger are reservations that expire with the op. put_in() under stack(T, n) splits off exactly the reserved units. Continuous draw (power_draw, a per-tick drain) stays a stat; only discrete spends are transactions.Spend-then-effect sequences with hand-written refunds
X3 WorkflowsA workflow is an ordered set of typed steps (prompts, SQL, path, transport, job()) with one outcome enum, captured fields (the resume policy), one cancellation list and one resumption rule: the op's context, requirements re-checked, captured fields restored. An op's workflow runs in its Wait stage, before the reservation. asks(), confirms(), request(), DB queries, interface modals and prompt chains (step =, A.step("name")) are defined on it. db_query_now stays the narrow exception. A workflow is not a general scripting system.Separate prompt, flow and I/O engines
X4 State graphsSTATE_GRAPH(GRAPH_X) with STAGE_DEF nodes and ops as edges; per instance a current stage, a history stack and a ledger. construction and deployment are graphs, each with its own capability key and op prefix: undo pops the history and refunds that transition's ledger entry (written at reserve time, finalised at commit); the one undo op of a stage takes the union of its ways in; "at or past a stage" is reachability from the start through the history; a part-built start seeds its history lazily along the one path that leads to it, or along the via = path when several do (a build error without one). Machine modes stay plain tracked enums."At or past" by stage order; undo to a declared predecessor; one-off deployable state machines; a deployment with no ledger refund
X5 Value schemasschema = on TRACKED, STAT, capability params, op args (arg(name, schema)), ui_shape fields, prompt and request input (a prompt kind states it with SCHEMA(kind, value, ...)), SQL row columns (SCHEMA), preferences and persistence, and VV edits. Kinds: bool, int, num, enum, flags, text, ref, path, list_of, map_of, row; options normalize (x(A, value)), default (its one home; TRACKED declares the var), persist, on_invalid = CLAMP | REJECT. A numeric write clamps and any other failure rejects; an int rejects a non-integer from input and rounds one from an internal write; player input is checked before Match and a failure ends the op ACT_REFUSED with the schema's reason.Stats as the answer to validation; hand-written clamps and sanitize; arg_* as a second validator; a default written in two places

Migration gates (round 3)

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
The E0 gateTen executable proofs on the engines' spike must pass before any content conversion, each written with the test driver of section 15 (time moved by test_time, what happened read from its recorder, outcomes read from a plain op result): a door's density at once; one effect from two sources; removing an ability mid-effect; a capture holding against a changed selection; a refused insertion losing no resource; one op by click, UI and AI; caches updating under change; a station-wide cascade under budget; a construction with two predecessors undoing to the right one; a schema clamping a bad UI write with the generated type's range comment matching.Converting content before the contracts are proven; proofs with no way to execute an input
TelekinesisA provider: provides(AFF_MANIPULATE, reach = TK_RANGE, line_of_sight = TRUE), with TK_RANGE in tiles (today's TK_MAXRANGE, 15). It does any hand op on a target in range and in sight, used only when no nearer provider reaches; doors and requirements still apply, and it gives no tool or attack affordances. Body-contact ops say reach(REACH_RANGE(1)).INTERACT_TK opted in per entry; attack_tk() overrides per type
What lint-engine parity provesOnly that old findings are reproduced. The soundness of each new semantic check is proven by its own fixtures (a seeded violation it must catch and a clean case it must pass).Parity as evidence that the new checks are sound
Mechanical commitsLayout moves and icon renames are separate mechanical commits, never mixed with a semantic change.Moves and meaning changes in one commit

Pre-implementation review (round 4)

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Phase orderK, S, N, U, D, P, R, G, stated as deliberate in section 2: input resolved in K reaches the same frame's native step, and writes in D and P (scheduled work) reach it the next frame.K, D, P, S, N, R (the order the round-3 spec named); an order nobody had decided
The settle ruleOne rule, derived by the build from the relation declarations, with no author declaration. Inline (before the setter returns): stats on the written entity, and on the entity at the end of one single-valued edge (a holder; a slot occupant; the container of a slot of capacity 1; a one-to-one link). Marked (the next marked drain, in rank order, under the budget of the stat's lane): anything reached through a collection or membership edge, a SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, or two or more edges. An area's power channel reaching its machines is a membership edge, so it is marked. Native-read vars on the written entity are always inline. E0 proof 8 measures marked fan-out and may promote specific membership edges to inline.Three criteria (a system source, one direct edge, a "shared source") with no mechanism to decide between them
SpillOne spill rule: a recompute that outruns its budget resumes at the next marked drain, which may be later in the same tick. Phase S's guarantee covers the effects settled inline (the written entity and one single-valued edge away), and a marked effect is pushed by whichever frame's S follows the drain that settles it.Spill targets of "next drain", "next tick" and "the first drain of the next tick" for one mechanism
Where asks() runsIn Wait, as the op's workflow, before the reservation. An effect written before an asks() is a build error. chance() rolls after the last answer and the reservation. The act holds its holder, target, held item and actor through weak relations until the reserve step and strongly from it until it finishes: deleting one before the reserve step cancels the op (REQ_CANCELLED for the request, ACT_REFUSED with the reason for the op), and after it deletion does not.asks() as a suspension point in the Do list; a build warning for an effect before the first asks(); strong references held across a prompt
Activation lifecycleA type-level capability has one activation per (holder, definition), created lazily on the first state write or cap_data read; entry-level when() blocks are bits in a per-holder mask. Revoke marks the activation dead synchronously and dispatch skips it at once; only the index rebuild waits for the drain. Teardown from the activation's own handler: the handler finishes, then the record drops, with no further runs (DM cannot unwind a running proc). grant() returns the activation, or null with the reason logged. Accessors and cap_of address the type-level activation, and a granted capability with state declares a selector.An activation allocated per instance; a revoked activation's hooks firing until the drain; undefined teardown re-entrancy; an accessor that cannot say which of two grants it means
Test driverThe table in section 15: test_click, test_ui, test_menu, test_answer, test_time (the one form that makes time pass), test_drain and test_phase (which move no clock), perform_op returning a plain /datum/op_result with .outcome, test_logs, test_rng(seed) with a roll counter, notice and spill counters, test_budget(lane, units) in microseconds of tick time with a fixed nominal cost per evaluation in test builds, and the recorder (test_record, test_recorded). Proof 6 compares the result, the record and the log lines minus the origin field. Proof 10 compares the doc comment on the generated type with the schema.E0 proofs written with assert_resolves alone, which asserts a resolution and observes nothing else; a driver with no way to advance time or to read transfers and reservations; a pooled act handed back to a caller
Input gate detailsORIGIN_MENU is the context menu and radial; hand(), tool(), item() and inside() accept ORIGIN_CLICK | ORIGIN_MENU | ORIGIN_AI. provides(reach =) is tiles only, and REACH_ANY is an op policy, never a provider value. TK_RANGE is today's TK_MAXRANGE (15) and BORG_INTERFACE_REACH is 7 tiles, the view range. The distance steps (2 and 4) of the reach gate apply to REACH_ADJACENT only, and the exposure step (3) applies to every spatial policy. There is no reach stat: caches key on the actor's provider set generation, and a menu cache key is (actor, target, held, their act generations, provider set generation). There is no hand provider on /mob/living: a species grants hands(), and the provider that performs an op is the held item's, then the hand, then any other by shortest reach.A reach stat; reach = REACH_ANY on a provider; the context menu as ORIGIN_VERB; three-step provider selection for every spatial policy
The target of an op with no target bindingA.target is A.holder for an op with no target binding: an actor's own menu() op, a self ui_act() (an interface() open has a target, since it is bound to hand() and remote()). A part that means the holder still writes on = ON_HOLDER.A null target for an actor's own op, so a default grants() landed nowhere
Op effect returnsOP_OK, OP_REFUSED, OP_REPLACED and OP_FAILED are defines. A proc that returns nothing reports OP_OK, any other return must be an OP_* constant (a lint), a runtime is OP_FAILED, and act_outcome_to_op(F) carries a world action's outcome. The library parts report for themselves: put_in() refused or replaced, grants() returning null.Effect return values with no definition; a cooldown that "commits after OP_OK" from a proc that returns nothing
A failed chance()The costs commit (the attempt cost them), no effect runs, and the op ends ACT_COMMITTED with A.rolled = FALSE. op_done is not published by default; on_op(key, outcome = ACT_ROLL_FAILED | ACT_ANY) opts in, with the same ACT_* filter vocabulary as on_notice.A failed roll announced as a committed op, so on_op("lock.pick") fired for a failed pick
Resource protocol/datum/reservation(resource, holder, amount, op); the adapter names the holder with holder_of(A), and the base adapter totals open reservations in the holder's own lazy reservations list (LIST_STATE(/datum, reservations)); a commit that returns nothing is OP_OK and a failing one is OP_FAILED with a log line; res_spend(E, RES_X, n, actor =, held =, target =) takes its context explicitly, rounds an amount for an int resource and returns n or 0, since a reservation is all or nothing; RES_ITEM and RES_COOLDOWN are the adapters of consumes() and cooldown(). Under a stack(T, n) binding, put_in() splits off exactly the reserved n units and moves that split, and the commit consumes nothing further.A reservation token with no definition; a base adapter that could not name its holder; two costs() forms that reserve nothing; a fractional spend against an int schema
Schema detailsnormalize is x(datum/act/A, value). default = is the one home of a default, and TRACKED declares the var, so a var line beside it is a lint error; a capability param's default is its schema's default =, or the param = default of the definition line when it has none, and CAPABILITY_TYPE declares the var. int rejects a non-integer from a UI arg or a request and rounds one from an internal setter write. Boundary validation runs before Match, ends the op ACT_REFUSED with the schema's reason and publishes no notice beyond a refused op's; clamp logs are rate limited. A prompt kind states its schema with SCHEMA(kind, value, ...).A normalize proc with no way to receive the value; a default in the var line, the option and the schema; clamp logs with no rate limit; a schema = var on a prompt kind
Deployment and the graph ledgerdeployment records its spending in the ledger like a build, and undeploying refunds it. It has its own capability key (CAP_DEPLOYMENT) and op prefix (deployment.deploy:<stage>, deployment.undeploy:<stage>). A stage's one undo op takes the union of its ways in, selected by the top history entry's edge. A ledger entry is written at reserve time and finalised at commit, so an edge's own then() can read it. A part-built start seeds its history lazily along the one path that leads to it, or along the via = path when several do (a build error without one), with empty ledger entries. An edge with no derivable undo contributes no input to a stage's undo op."The same graph, without the ledger refund of a build" beside "records in the ledger like a build"; one undo op with no stated input; a ledger read before it was written
The source of a slot activationThe occupying item for ON_HOLDER, and the slot entry (the owning type's declaration, per holder) for ON_CONTENTS, where the occupant is the holder itself."The slotted item" as the source of both, which made A.source meaningless for ON_CONTENTS and broke BEST ties
Request capture, detailsA lint requires a then() that reads a captured field to use A.captured(). A holder's revision is a per-entity counter bumped by any published key. asks() takes keeps = with the default WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT, so reach while waiting is re-checked as part of keeps.A handler reading a captured field live; a revision that was never defined; an op with asks() and no keeps, which the actor could walk away from
delayed(), keybinds and ACT_PASSThe parts of a delayed() run on a timer context that holds the holder and the three snapshot names, so only on = ON_HOLDER and says() are legal there and anything else is a build error. A keybind collision is a boot error unless the two ops' when parts are mutually exclusive. ACT_TRY returns ACT_PASS only when nothing hooks the action and no listener wants its notice, so an on_notice listener alone gets a real act.delayed() parts that read an actor that was never stored; a keybind rule that forbade shift and unshift sharing a key; a committed notice lost because the act was ACT_PASS
DM feasibilityA bare COVER_OPEN is the default selector's id; a non-default selector's id is generated (COVER_HATCH_OPEN, or cover_key(COVER_OPEN, "hatch")), and no macro is written both with and without arguments. Contracts hold ids, act types, defines and accessor declarations, never procs; DM resolves procs across the whole tree whatever the file order, so a proc stays in its module and the module_deps lint enforces the direction. A running proc cannot be unwound, so "the handler finishes, then the record drops" is the only teardown rule. A captured field is read through A.captured(), because DM has no getters.COVER_OPEN and COVER_OPEN("hatch") as one macro name in two forms; capability signatures or forward declarations in contracts; an unwind of a running handler
Lane budget unitA lane budget is microseconds of tick time per server tick. job(budget =) stays a percent of a tick. Test builds charge each stat evaluation a fixed nominal cost instead of reading the clock, so budgets and spills are deterministic.A budget with no unit and no test knob

Test driver and capture fixes (round 5)

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
What perform_op returnsA small plain /datum/op_result (key, outcome, reason, rolled, origin), never the pooled act. The act is released when the op ends, as always, and test builds poison a released pool entry. An op that finishes inside the call returns the result filled in; one that waits returns it with a null outcome and the same pending op fills it in later through a kernel relation. The test driver's input forms and AI behaviours use it the same way. A player's click, press or menu pick allocates none.Handing the pooled act to a caller; a leased act with a deferred release; every E0 proof reading .outcome from a dead reference
Time in teststest_time(t) advances the kernel clock, runs every phase and drain that falls due, and fires timers, every() work, wait() ends, hold expiries and request timeouts, with no real wait; the world clock moves by t and own and bio clocks by t times their rates. test_phase(P) and test_drain() place work exactly at the current time and move no clock. A wait ends and a timer fires only when test_time has moved past it.Ending a two-second wait with test_phase(D); a driver that can only wait real ticks
The test recordertest_record(entities...) and test_recorded(). In test builds the engine verbs report to it (compiled out of production): op outcomes, notices with their outcome, log lines, slot transfers, resource reserve, commit and release, activation attach and detach, and the tracked deltas of the entities the test names. Proof 6 reads it at phase 1, and the phase-2 snapshot harness is built on the same recorder. Its store belongs to E6, and each engine adds its report calls.A snapshot record only the phase-2 harness could make, so a phase-1 proof could not compare transfers and reservations; two recorders that disagree about a transition
A menu pick in teststest_menu(actor, target, op_key) is an ORIGIN_MENU pick with the actor's held item, through the same gates as a click. The menu's contents are read with action_options.An ORIGIN_MENU that no fixture could exercise
The E0 proofs' vocabularyEach proof is written with the driver's forms, the public engine verbs a fixture may call (grant, revoke, rel_set, ACT_TRY with act_done, move_into, action_options, screentip_for, built()) and test-only capabilities and types. Outcomes come from the op result, not a pooled act; a value a proof needs from inside an op (A.source, A.captured()) is written out by a test-only handler."Uses nothing else" beside proofs that call grant and rel_set; a proof reading the .source of an unnamed act; a proof reading a ledger from log lines that carry none
captures()captures(nameof(v), ..., resume = CAPTURE) is an op part, legal on any op with a Wait workflow (asks() or confirms()) and a build error on one without. It snapshots the named fields at the op's first asks() or confirms(), beside the fields an asks() field arg names. The captured-read lint applies to both: a then() that reads a captured field reads it through A.captured(nameof(v)). A confirms() op writes captures(nameof(selected_id)).A capture that exists only when some later asks() happens to name the field, so a confirm-only op confirmed one object and acted on another
Wait orderWithin Wait, the timed wait() and each asks() or confirms() run in the order the op declares them ("confirm, then work" is writable). A wait that no declaration places (a tool profile's, or an extend() on an op that wrote none) runs first. The reservation follows the last step.A timed wait that always precedes every prompt
A param's defaultOne home: the schema's default = when the param has a schema, else the param = default of the definition line. CAPABILITY_TYPE declares var/<param> on the datum, so a datum body never repeats a param's var line, and the schemas lint rejects it. A TRACKED var's example line is likewise dropped (var/night).A param default in the definition and again in a var line; a var line beside TRACKED in an example
Cache keysThe menu and screentip cache key is (actor, target, held, the act generation of each, the actor's provider set generation). The reach walk is cached per (actor, provider set generation, target). A generation is a counter per entity, so a key must name the entity it counts.A key of counters alone, where two actors with equal counters share an entry
HandsThere is no hand provider on /mob/living. A species with hands grants hands() (provides(AFF_MANIPULATE | AFF_ATTACK | AFF_HOLD, reach = 1)) in its SPECIES_CAPABILITIES, and species_capabilities() grants it while the mob's species relation names that species; a mob with no species declares its own providers (a carp's bite, a borg's modules and interface, the AI's remote control). A restraint refuses through the implicit req_capable(); a lost last hand drops the provider through a when() over the body.A base hand provider every mob inherited, with no way to take it away (a borg that could still open cells, a carp that could open airlocks)
The outcome filterOne vocabulary for on_notice and on_op: ACT_COMMITTED | ACT_REFUSED | ACT_REPLACED | ACT_ROLL_FAILED | ACT_ANY. ACT_ROLL_FAILED is a filter value only (a failed roll still ends ACT_COMMITTED with A.rolled = FALSE); ACT_COMMITTED hears committed ops whose roll succeeded; ACT_ANY is all four. OP_* is the vocabulary of an effect's report and nothing else.OP_ROLL_FAILED and OP_ANY as filter names, with no statement of what OP_ANY covered
perform_op and bindingsperform_op() by key runs through whichever binding of the op accepts the origin given (ORIGIN_AI by default). Every physical binding accepts ORIGIN_AI and runs with the click's provider and reach gate; ai() accepts it for an op with no physical binding and uses the op's own reach and no provider. An op whose bindings accept the origin given is refused with a reason; a controller passes ORIGIN_VERB for a menu() ability, or the ability declares ai()."perform_op reaches an op only if it has ai()" beside physical bindings that accept ORIGIN_AI
Seeding a placed instanceIf exactly one path leads from the graph's start to the placed stage, that path is seeded lazily with empty ledger entries. If several do, the type says configure(construction_graph(start = STAGE_X, via = list(STAGE_A, ...))) and a missing via = is a build error. An edge with no derivable undo and no undo = contributes no input to the stage's union undo; the door kit names undo = list(tool(TOOL_CROWBAR))."The shortest path from the start", which marked a boarded door as never boarded
Marked recompute: where, and which laneMarked stats recompute only in the marked drains, the drains at the start of phases D, P and R, in rank order, each evaluation charged to the stat's own lane: LANE_SIMULATION for an entity's stat, the system's declared lane for a stat on the system. The budget bounds this evaluation only; hook attachment, on_change and queueing always complete at their drain point. The end-of-trigger drain and phase S's recompute nothing marked, so a click never carries a cascade and S is never shed. test_budget(lane, units) sets a lane's per-tick budget for the marked drains."The lane's budget" with no lane named; a budget that might stop hooks; a budgeted drain in phase S
Edge kinds follow capacityA slot of capacity 1 is a single-valued edge in both directions (a bed's buckle slot); a slot that holds several (a locker, a belly) is a collection edge from its container to its occupants."An occupant reading its container" as single-valued for every slot
The species relationThe mob type declares ref_one(nameof(species), /datum/species) (the human's list in 16.4), and a species change is rel_set(M, nameof(species), S). SPECIES_CAPABILITIES lines for the human and the shadekin are in 16.4.A species change that no example declared a relation for
The reach gate in four stepsCollect providers; distance (the largest reach); exposure (the containment walk); each op's own provider. REACH_RANGE(n) and REACH_VIEW skip the two distance steps (2 and 4) and not the exposure walk. A provider with no reach = serves REACH_RANGE(n), REACH_VIEW, REACH_ANY and REACH_INSIDE."Skip steps 2 and 3" beside "the exposure walk of step 2 applies"
Smaller pointsA hold batch's slice recompute runs at the next marked drain (phase G is not a drain point). Phase U has no drain point. An interface() open has a target (it is bound to hand() and remote()). TARGET_PRESENT is about the target moving; deletion cancels whatever the keeps say. A.activation is null for a type-level capability until it has state, and cap_data(A) creates it. The cap_data example is a stateful capability with a selector (cover); absorbed declares key = NONE. SYSTEM_ACCESSOR in contracts holds names only and the generator emits the proc. A commit that returns nothing is OP_OK; res_spend returns n or 0; the holder's reservations list is a declared LIST_STATE. The mapping row for INTERACT_SILICON lists ORIGIN_MENU.Each was a leftover that the surrounding text contradicted

22Source layout

The new tree is organised by what a file is for, not by where it came from. Framework code, shared capabilities, large systems with their own model, ordinary content, the contracts between them, and the glue every layer needs each get a top-level folder under code/. Two things are kept apart: the order files compile in, and which module may depend on which. Every file under code/ has a destination, including the ~3,900 that never had a legacy form. A file moves into the new tree only when it is fully migrated, or, if it has nothing to migrate, in a bulk move wave.

The tree

code/defines/{engine,library,host,base,content}
    // macros and constants only; replaces __defines/. Files whose macros depend on each other keep their order (order.txt).
code/contracts/{ids,acts,accessors}
    // the names more than one module uses, declarations only: ids (SRC_X, TAG_X, STAGE_X, RES_X, CAP_X), the act types ACTION() generates, defines
    // and SYSTEM_ACCESSOR declarations. Never procs: DM resolves a proc by name across the whole tree whatever the file order, so a proc stays in its
    // module with no forward declaration here, and the module_deps lint, not the compile order, enforces who may call it. Low in compile order, so library,
    // domains and content depend on contracts, not on each other's internals.
code/host/{helpers,globals,click,client,admin,tgui,assets,config,vendor}
    // glue every layer needs: helpers, global vars, click and HUD glue, client and admin plumbing,
    // the tgui backend, assets, config. vendor/ is third-party code (TGS), exempt from lints.
code/base/{atom,area,turf,obj,mob}
    // the root types /atom /atom/movable /obj /obj/item /obj/machinery /obj/structure /mob /mob/living /turf /area:
    // their vars, base procs and base STAT and TRACKED declarations.
code/engine/{declare,kernel,time,state,stats,refs,change,actions,parts,hooks,present,io,_generated}
    // the framework, one folder per concept. _generated holds the analysis engine's output
    // (reads tables, STAT ids, ACTION types) and is never hand-edited.
code/library/{machine,access,containers,reagents,items,structures,construction,providers,mobs}
    // shared capabilities and bundles (section 11)
code/domains/{body,damage,atmos,power,lighting,life,ai,verdigris}/{model,content}
    // big systems with a reusable model of their own; a domain with concrete things of its own splits into model and content
code/content/<domain>/...
    // concrete things built on the domains: e.g. content/machinery/power/apc.dm, content/doors/airlock.dm
code/world/{systems,round,maps}
    // world-level systems (vote, supply, research, ...), map glue, round flow
code/tests/
    // cross-cutting tests, snapshot capture and benchmarks; compiled only under UNIT_TESTS

Compile order

Compile order is the order of this table, with the legacy layer between base and engine. The legacy layer is every file not yet moved, kept in exactly today's relative include order. code/engine/_generated/ compiles last of all, as code/_generated/ does today, because it names types defined everywhere. Compile order decides only what DM makes order-dependent, macros and the override chain of a var or proc; it says nothing about which module may call which.

OrderLayerHolds
1definesMacros and constants only
2contractsIds (capability ids included), act types, defines and accessor declarations: names, never procs or bodies
3hostHelpers, globals, click and HUD glue, client and admin plumbing, tgui backend, assets, config, vendored code
4baseThe root types, their base vars and procs, and the base declarations (below)
·legacyFiles not yet migrated, in today's order, shrinking as they move
5engineThe framework
6libraryShared capabilities and bundles
7domainsBig systems with a reusable model of their own
8contentConcrete things built on the domains
9worldWorld-level systems, map glue, round flow

Dependency direction

Who may depend on whom is a module graph, not the compile ladder. A module is a directory with an api.dm (for a system) or a declared dependency list, and the analysis engine's module_deps lint checks it: every cross-module reference resolves to a declared edge, the graph has no cycle, and the direction rules below hold. Examples need edges a ladder cannot give them, such as a library capability that reads the power system, content that reads the night-shift flag, host input that calls engine operations, and base lifecycle methods that need framework services; each is an edge to a contract or an adapter, not a reach into internals.

ModuleMay depend on
contractsDefines only
hostDefines, contracts; base types by path
baseDefines, contracts, host. Not the procs or types of engine, library, domains, content or world
engineDefines, contracts, host, base
libraryContracts and engine. Another library area only through its contracts, never its internals
domainsContracts, engine, library; another domain only through its api.dm and contracts
contentContracts, engine, library, and the api.dm and model of the domains it builds on
worldEverything above, through api.dm and contracts
legacyEverything (it is what is being retired)
  • Domain against content. A domain owns a reusable model: the power grid, atmospherics, the chemistry model, the body. Content is the concrete things that use it: an APC, a pump, a beaker. Content depends on domains and never the reverse. Size does not decide which is which: research, chemistry, materials, flight and construction each hold a model and concrete things, so a folder that holds both splits into <x>/model and <x>/content, and the model never references the content.
  • Adapters live where both sides are visible. powered() and powered_by() name the power system, so they are defined in the power domain, code/domains/power, not in the library; the library never names a domain's system, and a machine reaches both through contracts.
  • System state is private. Content reads a system through the accessor its api.dm declares with SYSTEM_ACCESSOR, whose declaration sits in contracts (night_shift_active(), section 7), so the APC code never names the night-shift system. The declaration holds names only, a system id and a var name, so contracts still depend on defines alone: the analysis engine resolves them and emits the accessor proc into code/engine/_generated, which can see the system's private var.

Rules

RuleDetail
A composition root per typeA type has one CAPABILITIES list, its composition root. The root, the type's procs, its MSG_DEFs and its SYSTEM_DEFs sit together by default; a long list is split into sections of the same block, and nothing requires one file per type. The root types split: vars, base procs and standalone declaration lines in code/base, and the one CAPABILITIES list in the library area that composes it (code/library/machine/machine.dm for /obj/machinery), so base never references the library.
Tests beside codeA test of one file sits next to it (apc.dm, apc_test.dm). Cross-cutting tests, snapshot capture (the 1,968-line dq_i7_bulk_capture.dm snapshots hundreds of types) and benchmarks sit in code/tests/. Neither is in the generated .dme directly: the generator writes code/tests/_tests.dm, an include list under #if defined(UNIT_TESTS), in place of today's hand-sorted _unit_tests.dm list.
Generated .dmedeepquarry.dme is generated by a query on the analysis engine (phase 1, tool L) from a checked-in template and the tree. The template holds what must not move: code\genesis_call.dme first, the FILE_DIR block, the BEGIN and END markers, the root files (code/__byond_version_compat.dm, __odlint.dm, _compile_options.dm, world.dm and the rest of the loose code/*.dm) in today's order, and the tail: interface\*.dm, interface\skin.dmf (the .dmf last, as today) and the #ifdef DOC_SNIPPETS block. Between head and tail the generator writes the layers in order: sorted by path inside a layer, except where a directory's order.txt says otherwise; the legacy layer from tools/build/dme_legacy_order.txt; _generated last. A hand edit to the generated file fails the check.
module_deps lintA module references its declared dependencies and the contracts; host and base are readable by all. STAT, ACTION, TRACKED and MSG_DEF lines are declarations the generator reads, not references. No verb_entry under code/content. Nothing under engine, library, domains or content may use an old (pre-design) form. Exempt by path in lint_scopes.toml: the engine's own raw tracked and stat writes and its catch, code/tests/, and code/host/vendor/. The legacy layer is exempt from the old-form rule but not from the next one.
Duplicate-definition lintNo (type, var) or (type, proc) is defined in two files. Re-opening a type in several files to set different vars is normal; defining the same member twice means the last file compiled wins, and a move changes which that is. Ratcheted with a baseline in phase 1; no wave may add a pair, and a wave that moves a file in a pair must resolve it.
Compiled-table diffBefore and after each move commit, a tool walks the object tree and dumps, per type, the hash of every var's winning definition text and each proc's ordered list of defining files. The diff may show only file paths and line numbers changing. A changed winner, initial value or override order fails the wave.
IconsIcons follow the same domain split, in the phase-3 icon rename. Waves never touch maps or icons.

Base declarations: where the engine's stats live

The engine's stats name base types: operable, can_move, can_act and acts_via on /obj/machinery and /mob/living, and the world actions (move, cross, fall, hit) name /turf and /mob/living. A dependency rule that said "engine may not reference content" read as if these declarations had to sit in the engine. They don't.

  • They are standalone STAT(...) lines in code/base/<type>/ and ACTION(...) lines in code/contracts/acts/ (an action's name and fields are shared by every module that hooks it), both below the engine. The macros are defines (layer 1).
  • The analysis engine reads them and emits the resulting var/ lines, STAT_* ids and /datum/act types into code/engine/_generated/. So the engine reads generated ids, never base files, and a base file never names an engine proc or type.
  • Stats and actions of a type that is not a root (a turret's armed) live with that type, in its capability or content file.

Where the other files go

Section 19's phase 6 waves cover about 1,900 files that still carry a legacy form after phase 3. The other ~3,900 files have nothing to convert, so each directory below has a destination and a bulk move wave. A move wave is a pure git mv in its own freeze window, regenerates the .dme, and must pass the duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff. A directory's files split between its move wave and the content waves: a content wave moves its own files when it proves them.

WaveOld directory (files today)New home
M01code/__defines (281; the ~12 legacy define files are deleted, not moved)code/defines/<module>/
M02_helpers (72), _global_vars (25), _onclick (46)code/host/helpers, globals, click
M03The root-type files in code/game/atom, area, turfs, atoms_movable.dm, objects.dm, items.dm, the mob and living defines (about 90)code/base/{atom,area,turf,obj,mob}
M04ATMOSPHERICS (57), modules/lighting (11), modules/heat (5)code/domains/atmos, lighting
M05modules/client (140), admin (124), tgui (57), tgui_panel, tgchat, asset_cache (36), controllers/configuration and measure; modules/tgs (19, vendored)code/host/client, admin, tgui, assets, config; code/host/vendor/tgs
M06modules/unit_tests (299), modules/benchmarks (11)Beside the code they test, or code/tests/
M07game/objects (570)code/content/items, structures, effects
M08game/machinery (185), game/mecha (90)code/content/machinery, mecha
M09modules/mob (700), clothing (198), organs (48), body (24), medical (121)code/content/mobs, clothing; code/domains/body for body, organs and medical
M10game/gamemodes (135), antagonist (36), jobs (23), modules/shuttles (19), events (52), gamemaster (41)code/world/round
M11The remaining modules/* directories (about 1,300 files in ~100 directories: reagents 68, food 52, hydroponics 74, research 101, projectiles 118, vore 59, xenoarcheaology 58, integrated_electronics 46, modular_computers 66, materials 64, ...)code/content/<same name>; modules/power (73) to code/domains/power; modules/combat_ai (60) to code/domains/ai. A system a directory holds (the expedition and flight services, for example) is already in code/world/systems from phase 1S.
M12datums/entity_state (101), outfits (26), wires (24), supplypacks (21), gear (21), uplink (17), loot, looping_sounds, locations and the other content datumscode/content/<same name>
·controllers/kernel, controllers/subsystems (29), the framework directories of code/datumsNot bulk-moved: ported, kept or deleted file by file in section 20 (E6 and 1S write the systems directly into code/engine, code/domains and code/world)

Migration rule

A file lives in the new tree only when it is fully migrated or has nothing to migrate. Each content wave does the same four things in order:

  1. Convert the files in the wave.
  2. Prove them: zero old forms (per-file ratchet), tests, the behaviour snapshot harness, the construction round-trip and the bench.
  3. Move them in a pure git mv commit, with no edits, so history follows. The duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff must pass first, because moving a file under a generated .dme changes compile order and so can change which definition wins.
  4. Merge.

A move wave is a pure move, and the icon rename is a pure rename: layout moves and icon renames are separate mechanical commits, never mixed with a semantic change, so the compiled-table diff and the icon check each have one thing to prove.

Old APIs are deleted in the commit their last caller goes. The lint for that form is a ratchet from the day its replacement lands and a hard ban from that commit (Coexistence rules).

Completion test. The migration is done when every legacy-form lint is at zero with its baseline deleted, and the move table is complete: every .dm file under code/ is in a layer folder, the legacy list dme_legacy_order.txt is empty and the shim registry is empty. An old directory that still exists (it may hold non-code files) is not itself a failure, and an empty one is not proof of completion.

The design is against origin/master 6dcd1e30f9. The inventories behind it are five read-only surveys (the OM layer, the sys layer, the current framework, subsystems and services, and the docs) plus a mechanical scan of every framework macro, proc and hook (api_surface.py). Section 17 and the full table beside this page, doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv, are generated from that scan (mapping.py), and coverage.py checks that every form game code uses is named in this document or in that table. Code in sections 1–16 is design, not compiled: from phase 2 the examples of section 16 compile as DOC_SNIPPETS tests.
Revision 3 (1 October 2026) writes in the semantic contracts of the external review (consistency, the op commit, input bindings, activations, request capture, the five-part input, the graph contract, phase S, action outcomes, layers) and restates the existing systems on five foundations: scoped activation, resource transactions, workflows, state graphs and value schemas. Ten executable E0 proofs gate any content conversion.
Revision 4 (2 October 2026) applies the pre-implementation review: one settle rule for stats (inline on the written entity and one single-valued edge away, marked beyond), every asks() in Wait before the reservation, the activation lifecycle (lazy type-level activations, synchronous detach, self-teardown, grant() results), a test driver for the E0 proofs, the input gate (ORIGIN_MENU, tile reach, provider choice), A.target for ops with no target binding, depth-cap handling for actions, resource and schema details, and the deployment graph's own key.
Revision 5 (2 October 2026) applies the re-check of revision 4: a test driver that can run the E0 proofs (test_time, a recorder, test_menu, and a plain /datum/op_result in place of the pooled act), the captures() part for confirm-only ops, cache keys that name their entities, hands granted by species instead of inherited, one outcome filter vocabulary, perform_op through any binding that accepts the origin, state-graph seeding by path with via =, and the marked drains of D, P and R with a named lane.
